- **FM27 GUI test seams sit behind a `test-hooks` feature (plan task 16, 2026-11-03).** No encoded byte changes and `identity.json` is untouched.
  - **The feature.** `sdc-gui` has a `test-hooks` feature, off by default; it also turns on the `render3d`, `modscan` and `pgpsim` hooks (Note GJ2). Its own dev-dependency (`sdc-gui = { path = ".", features = ["test-hooks"] }`, the engine's pattern, Note K) turns it on for `cargo test` and `--all-targets`; `cargo build --release` never enables it (resolver 2). `#[doc(hidden)]` is not used in `crates/gui/src`.
  - **The rule for a new seam.** A `pub` item that only tests use, and every counter, field or write that only such an item reads, is `#[cfg(feature = "test-hooks")]`. A production item that tests also call (`finish_cs_pbr`, `finish_cm_json`, `finish_xpmf`, `statics_of`, `StatCache::clear`) stays plain `pub`. When a production path takes a test's replacement (an image builder, say), the production code calls a private function and the seam is a gated wrapper over it.
  - **What is gated.** The counters and getters `filtered_builds`, `problems_builds`, `problem_lookups`, `export_files_builds`, `view_builds`, `set_cards_drawn`, `helper_rows_drawn`, `cache_builds`, `header_reads_on_this_thread`, `plan_calls`, `visible_builds`, `row_builds`, `project_builds` and `dds_thumb_requests`; the plan-hold hooks (`hold_plan_results`, `take_held`, `handle_held`, `end_export_with`); the Meshes pill-read gate (`project_gate`, `project_alive`, `project_started`, `hold_rows_read`); the Set 3D encoder and viewport stand-ins (`hold_workers`, `inject`, `inject_as`, `inject_stale`, `has_alt`, `token_of`, `alt_token_of`, `set_plugin_reader`, `attach_test_thread`, `fake_render_thread`, `render_sent_for_test`, `loaded_for_test`); and small getters (`votes_pending`, `generation`, `plan_marks`). A build without the feature does none of the counting.
  - **Tests.** Every test that reaches a seam runs under `cargo test -p sdc-gui`; `tools\doc-shots.ps1` runs `cargo test -p sdc-gui --test doc_shots`, so it gets the feature too. Gate: `cargo clippy -p sdc-gui -- -D warnings` (no feature) and `--all-targets` both pass.
  - Supersedes: tune-up #7 item 5 (the public test seams on `AppModel`; the tune-up list is outside the repo). In part: CL19, GC2 and GC11 (the `#[doc(hidden)]` wording).
- **FM28 Tent for alpha-weighted colour mips (plan task 17, owner: negative lobes ring at cutout edges, 2026-11-03).**
  - **Rule.** On sRGB colour whose alpha policy weights the mips (`cutout`, `blend`, `cutout_blend`) `mips.filter` may be `box` or `tent`; `mitchell` and `kaiser` stay barred there (scoped: `CFG-INVALID`; unscoped: box with `CFG-FILTER-IGNORED`). HDR with alpha weighting still takes box only. Box is the default and its bytes do not change.
  - **Weighted tent.** `filter::resample_rgb_weighted` runs the tent taps of `resample_rgb` from mip 0 (widened per level, S-mips-3), each tap multiplied by the source alpha, RGB divided by the weighted sum. A texel whose taps are all transparent takes the plain tent RGB. Alpha stays the area-exact box chain, bit for bit. The same RGB is used by `resize` (S-mips-6) when the resize filter is tent. `mips::effective_filter` still decides: the weighted path runs when `weighted_rgb` is true, which is the predicate the box chain already used.
  - **Encode versions.** Every sub-type's arm is already 2.0.0 (FM9) and 2.0.0 is the current engine version, so no arm changes. A 1.x output of an alpha-weighted texture with `mips.filter = tent` was box (`CFG-FILTER-IGNORED`); the 2.0 re-encode writes the tent.
  - **GUI.** The Filter combo reads `filter_allowed`, so Tent is enabled on an alpha-weighted texture and Mitchell and Kaiser are disabled with the reason as hover text: "alpha-tested and blended colour takes box or tent; Mitchell and Kaiser ring at cutout edges".
  - **Tests.** `identity.json` gains `cov/textures/c/cutout_bc3_tent.dds` (a cutout BC3 with `mips.filter = tent`) and the test checks it differs from the box cutout; every existing key is unchanged. `mips::{weighted_tent_ignores_transparent_colour, weighted_tent_alpha_is_the_box_alpha}`, `filter::weighted_tent_is_the_plain_tent_for_opaque_input_and_falls_back_where_nothing_has_weight`, `resize::weighted_tent_resize_keeps_box_alpha_and_ignores_invisible_colour`, `policy_checks::{tent_is_allowed_for_cutout_colour, kaiser_on_cutout_falls_back_to_box_with_cfg_filter_ignored}`, `fields::the_filter_menu_offers_tent_for_a_cutout_texture`.
  - Supersedes: In part: S-mips-2 (tent barred on alpha-weighted colour; owner question 2) and the Deferred bullet of Note S (alpha-weighted tent).
- **FM29 Automatic power of two; `resize` is Half, Quarter or Eighth with a size floor (plan task 18, 2026-11-03).**
  - **The power of two.** `resize::pot_dims` sizes every texture with mips. With L the longer side and Lp its next power of two, L rounds up to Lp when L ≥ 0.95·Lp, else down to Lp/2. The shorter side S is scaled by the same factor first, Ss = S·L'/L (L' the rounded longer side), then rounds up to its next power of two when Ss ≥ 0.85 of it, else down; Ss < 1 gives 1. Examples (test `resize::pot_rule_examples`): 1000² → 1024², 1024×768 → 1024×512, 1000×600 → 1024×512, 48×40 → 32×16, 3×5 → 2×4 (then `DIM-SMALL`), 900×1 → 512×1. Integer arithmetic, no float rounding.
  - **Exempt textures.** `mips.levels = "none"` skips `pot_dims` (`resize::pot_exempt`): the texture keeps its size and gets `DIM-NPOT`, now a **Warning**, raised only there. `resize` still shrinks an exempt texture. A cube's source is never resized (`target_dims` returns it unchanged); its default face is `pot_dims((cell, cell))`, at most 4096, so a cell within 5 % of the next power of two now rounds up (a 1500×250 strip, cell 250 → face 256). An explicit `cubemap.face_size` is unchanged.
  - **`resize`** is `none`, `half`, `quarter` or `eighth`: `resize::shrink` halves both axes 1, 2 or 3 times (each at least 1 px), each time only while the longer side / 2 is still ≥ `resize_floor`. `resize_floor` is a new top-level `Profile` field, default 512 (`DEFAULT_RESIZE_FLOOR`), valid 1..=16384 (outside: `CFG-INVALID` "resize_floor N outside 1..=16384", test `policy_checks::a_resize_floor_out_of_range_is_cfg_invalid`; 0 would mean the same as 1, so it is refused rather than given a meaning), not serialised at its default, so existing profile hashes and identities hold where it is unset. `sdc-base` sets `[subtypes.height] resize_floor = 256`, so a 512² height map under `cs-compact` or `vanilla-compact` (`resize = "half"`) is written 256². `target_dims(dims, p)` = `shrink(pot_dims or dims, p.resize, p.resize_floor)` when `resize_relative_to` is `self` (with a slot, FM44's halving rule), used by the pipeline, the DIM checks, the bake plan, `estimated_bytes`, admission and the GUI's size estimate.
  - **Enlarging.** Tessalith's first upscale: `resize::enlarge` runs only on the axes that grow, by linear interpolation, texel-centre aligned, clamped edges; alpha-weighted colour is premultiplied, then divided by the interpolated alpha (plain RGB where it is 0). A roughness member grows on r² like the shrink. The existing down path (area-exact box, polyphase non-box) then shrinks the other axis, so `resize_work` is enlarge, then shrink when the size still differs. Admission (S-mips-11) charges 64 B per input texel when an axis grows, 32 when it only shrinks.
  - **Removed.** `resize = "pot-down"` and `drop_top_mip` (any value, ruling P21) are `migrate::REMOVED` rows: "the power of two is automatic; delete the key" and "use resize = \"half\"". `drop_top_mip` is gone from `Profile`, `FIELD_PATHS`, the pipeline and cube run (`chain.remove(0)`), the DIM block and the GUI (the Advanced *Half size* row, its lock and `set_field` special cases, the size estimate's dropped level). The texture panel's **Resize to a power of two** button is gone.
  - **DIM checks.** They read `target_dims`. `DIM-RESIZED` reads "{w}x{h} → {w'}x{h'}" plus " (resize = X)" when `resize` is set, data unchanged. Complex grass: `CFG-INVALID` when `shrink` would change the size (a non-POT grass is `GRASS-SHAPE`, as before).
  - **Low detail.** `checks::low_detail_eligible` needs `shrink(pot_dims(dims), Half, resize_floor) != pot_dims(dims)`; the `drop_top_mip` clause is gone (CL20's Eligibility bullet). `tests/low_detail_corpus.rs` judges eligibility on `target_dims`, the size `pipeline.rs` passes (`chain[0]`). Re-run on the owner's 10k set (2026-11-03, release): 10,000 jobs, 6,126 profile-eligible, 5,962 measured, 45 below 0.25, the same histogram as CL20 (the set is all 1024², where the power-of-two step changes nothing), so CL20's numbers stand.
  - **GUI.** Resize offers None, Half, Quarter, Eighth; a **Size floor** row (64 to 2048 px; a hand-written floor off that list, such as 300, is added to the combo so it reads "300 px", test `fields::the_resize_row_offers_the_halving_steps`) is an Advanced texture field (doc shot `advanced-texture-fields` on GUI-Advanced-Drawer) and a preset-editor row under *Resize & mips*. It is never locked, so Copy settings can carry `resize` and `resize_floor` together (a lock while Resize is None made the floor tick a no-op on targets whose resize was being set in the same copy). The `CHK-LOW-DETAIL` sentence ends "consider Resize: Half".
  - **Bake identity.** `BakeJob::identity_sha256` hashes `[resize, resize_floor]` only when the floor is not the default, else `resize` alone as before.
  - **Byte changes (encode versions).** Every arm is already 2.0.0 (FM9), so none moves. Outputs that change against 1.x and earlier 2.0 builds: NPOT sources with mips encode at the `pot_dims` size (before: `DIM-NPOT` blocked unless allowed, then written at the source size); 1024×768 becomes 1024×512; a 512 height map shrinks to 256 under the compact presets; enlarged axes use linear interpolation; exempt textures still take Half, Quarter and Eighth; cube faces from cells within 5 % of a power of two round up. `identity.json`: every existing key unchanged; new `cov/textures/c/npot_1000_bc1.dds` (1000² diffuse BC1 box → 1024²) and `cov/textures/c/height_half.dds` (512² height, `resize = "half"` → 256²). `encode_inputs.json` changes the height rows of cs and vanilla (profile only), `profile_hashes.json` 12 lines.
  - **Tests.** `resize::{pot_rule_examples, shrink_respects_the_floor, no_mip_textures_keep_their_size, enlarge_keeps_a_constant_image_constant, enlarge_is_linear_and_weighted_by_alpha}`; `golden::{npot_diffuse_converts_at_its_power_of_two_size, a_1000_square_diffuse_writes_1024, three_by_five_becomes_two_by_four_and_is_dim_small, no_mip_textures_are_written_at_their_own_size}`; `policy_checks::npot_with_mips_is_resized_and_without_mips_is_a_dim_npot_warning`; `low_detail::the_dim_checks_see_the_size_after_resize`; `presets::{pot_down_and_drop_top_mip_are_named, height_maps_shrink_to_a_256_floor}`; GUI `resize_field.rs` (was `drop_top_mip_field.rs`), `out_size::the_resized_size_agrees_in_all_three_places` (estimate, DIM-RESIZED and the written header), `model::an_npot_texture_is_resized_and_asks_no_question`. The GUI fixture's `odd.png` is 3 px (blocked by `DIM-SMALL`), since a 48 px NPOT is no longer blocked.
  - Supersedes, whole: T4 (`HALF_FLOOR`, no POT step), CL21 and CL23 (`drop_top_mip`), CL26 (the Advanced Half size row). In part: S-mips-6 (`pot-down`, owner question 3), S-mips-7 (`DIM-NPOT` after resize, the `pot-down` message), S-mips-10 (the Resize options and the `DIM-NPOT` button), the Deferred bullet of Note S (`pot-up`), CL20's Eligibility bullet, CL22, CL25, the CL cache-key bullet, CN19's Track C bullet, FC2 (the Half size tick and names), FC's `Half size has one meaning` and glossary bullets, and FM24's Half size bullet.
- **FM30 Sources of different sizes are resized (plan task 19, 2026-11-03).**
  - **Rule.** `pack::common_size` is the largest source by area when every source has one aspect ratio (`w_a·h_b == w_b·h_a`, exact, in `u64`), else none. A pack, a derived job and an assigned alpha fill bring every smaller source up to that size by `resize::enlarge` (linear, FM29); nothing is ever shrunk to the common size. Packed channels are data, so a pack's sources and a fill helper are interpolated unweighted; a derived job's colour maps and a fill's target are premultiplied by their alpha. The packed image then goes through `target_dims` like any output: 1000×500 and 512×256 sources pack at 1000×500 and are written 1024×512.
  - **Still refused.** A different aspect ratio is `PACK-SIZE-MISMATCH` (Error), message "sources of {output} have different aspect ratios: {file WxH, …}"; a derived set fails the same way (TA22). `PackError::SizeMismatch` now says "sources have different aspect ratios". A file replaced since planning with another aspect ratio (or a larger size) fails at assembly as before.
  - **`PACK-SOURCE-RESIZED`** (Info; `Code::ALL` has 93): "{output}: {file WxH, …} resized to {W}x{H}", data `{output, width, height, sources: [{path, width, height}]}`, on the packed, derived or filled job. It is read from the file headers at planning (`resized_warning`), so the pack cache does not hold it. It has no Problems sentence: Info results never get a row, the message shows on hover and in Messages.
  - **Suggested fills** keep Ruling 7: one whose helper is not the target's size is skipped ("sizes differ"), so an existing project never gains a fill. Only an assigned fill is resized (`FillCandidate::resized`; `apply_fill` enlarges the smaller of the two, so a helper larger than its target makes the colour texture write at the helper's size (the colour is interpolated plain, since the fill replaces its alpha; test `helper_fill_run::assigned_helper_of_another_size_is_resized`), and a suggested fill whose sizes drifted since planning still fails "size changed since planning").
  - **Admission.** The pack and derive pass-2 estimates (`grown_bytes`) and `job_bytes` (`job_dims`) count every source at the common size, so an enlarged source is not charged at its own size. Same-size jobs are charged as before.
  - **Encode versions.** Every arm is already 2.0.0 (FM9). A two-size pack used to fail, so no existing output changes: `identity.json` keeps every key and gains `cov/textures/c/twosize_rmaos.dds` (a 64² roughness and a 32² AO packed into a 64² `_rmaos`).
  - **Tests.** `pack_plan::{pack_sources_of_two_sizes_are_resized, pack_sources_of_one_size_have_no_resize_notice, pack_sources_of_different_aspect_still_refuse}`, `pack_run::a_1000x500_and_512x256_pack_resizes_then_goes_power_of_two`, `pack_assemble::{sources_of_one_aspect_are_enlarged_to_the_largest, sources_of_different_aspect_are_refused_with_every_size}`, `derive_plan::{sources_of_different_sizes_are_resized, sources_of_different_aspect_ratios_fail_only_that_set}`, `helper_fill_plan::{assigned_fill_of_other_size_lands_with_a_resize_notice, assigned_fill_of_other_aspect_fails_target}`.
  - Supersedes: In part: M9 (a size difference no longer fails a recipe), S-mips-7 (`PACK-SIZE-MISMATCH` compares sizes), the Deferred bullet of Note S (resizing pack sources), AF7 (the duplicate check), CN's derive TA22 paragraph (its mismatch clause; the aspect part stays) and DY8 (an assigned fill of another size).
- **FM31 Polish: window size, HDR tiles, helper-row keys, filled-analysis cache (plan task 20, 2026-11-03).**
  - **Window size.** egui 0.36.2 draws a `Window` bigger than the `default_rect` it was given (3 points with the default style: 1 plus twice the frame stroke, and more with some content), so a saved W×H came back (W+3)×(H+3) and was saved again. `ui::set_preview::settings_window_saved_rect`, which `layout::capture` reads, is the drawn rect less that excess. The excess is measured once per session, when the drawn size has held for 3 frames (60 at most); a difference over 16 points is a clamp to the window, not the margin, and takes nothing off. The excess depends a little on the content on show then (a selected item adds a few points), so a window whose content changes later saves off by that difference (a `shortcut:` in the code). A saved W×H is drawn W×H plus the excess and saved as W×H, so a start changes nothing. Test `set_preview_layout::a_saved_window_size_reopens_at_the_same_size`.
  - **HDR tiles.** `model::preview::dds_thumbnail` treats a BC6H, RGBA16F or RGBA32F DDS as float, so `make_thumbnail` maps each colour channel `x / (1 + x)` (a negative is 0 first) and encodes sRGB at 0 EV; alpha is untouched and an LDR tile is as stored. The main preview already did this on the GPU (`tonemap` in `preview.wgsl`; an RGBA32F file previews as RGBA16F); the shader now clamps a negative to 0 before the map, which turned -2 into +2. Test `preview_model::hdr_preview_tone_maps_values_above_one` (1, 4 and 16 give 188, 231 and 248; RGBA16F and RGBA32F; BGRA8 unchanged) and `a_bc6h_tile_is_tone_mapped`; the shader clamp is pinned by `render::exposure_scales_hdr_values_before_the_tone_map`.
  - **Helper rows.** `AppModel::row_move` (the Up and Down keys): from a helper row they step through the shown helper rows and stop at the ends; Up from the first returns to the last tile; from a tile in the last grid row (a partial one included), Down enters the first helper row; from any other tile, and with no helper rows, Up and Down are the grid row moves as before. Shift+arrow and Left/Right stay on the tiles (DY16's re-review). Enter and Space open the active row as before. The row is scrolled into view by the existing `reveal_item`. Tests `helpers_list::{helper_rows_move_with_arrow_keys, down_enters_the_helper_rows_only_from_the_last_grid_row, down_without_helper_rows_is_the_plain_grid_row_move, the_helper_row_the_keys_reach_stays_in_view}`.
  - **Filled analysis.** The fill step keeps each filled target's analysis and source info in the pack map under `session::fill_cache_key`: the target's path and stamp, the helper's `FillJob` (path, stamp, role, read, invert, assigned) and the EXR straight-alpha setting it was loaded under; hints are checked as for a pack. Memory only: the disk store's version pin does not run the fill. A re-plan with no change loads nothing there; a changed target or helper misses; `retain_plan` keeps the keys of the plan's filled jobs. If the straight-alpha loop ran twice, the first key is dropped after the plan and that first load repeats (EXR only). Test `helper_fill_run::filled_analysis_loads_once_across_replans`.
  - **Encode versions.** None moves: no output byte or verdict changes (`identity.json` keeps every key).
  - Supersedes: In part: CE21 (the creep), FC8 (CE21 as a kept shortcut). Whole: DY19's two shortcut bullets (the filled analysis is not cached; helper rows are mouse-only).
- **FM32 Sets column: keyboard and scroll (plan task 21, 2026-11-03).**
  - **Stand-ins.** A card outside the clip rect (CF7) still registers its widget id: a focus stop and an AccessKit node (Button, "Set {id}, position {n} of {N}", selected when the set is, the Focus and ScrollIntoView actions, the bounds of its allocated rect; the drawn card's own node carries the same name, role and selected state through `card_node`). The id is `ui.id().with(("set-card", key, id))`, and the drawn card's `scope_builder` takes the same one through `UiBuilder::id`, so the focus survives the swap from stand-in to card. Tab and Shift+Tab walk the cards in order. A stand-in that takes the focus this frame (the focus was elsewhere at the end of the last one: `DropState::focus`) or gets an AccessKit ScrollIntoView request (egui consumes that action only in a registered widget, so the stand-in reads it with `has_accesskit_action_request`; HP11) sets `nav.reveal_set`, or, for a created set with no key, calls `scroll_to_rect`; the next frame draws the card in view. A Go to set in the same frame wins: the card keeps its focus and the Tab asks for no scroll. A focused card the wheel scrolls off still loses focus (it is no widget that frame). The stand-in is registered by hand (`Memory::interested_in_focus` and `Context::accesskit_node_builder`), not with a `Response` per card: that cost 15% of a frame at 300 sets. Its node's parent is the window's root, not the list's container as for a drawn card: egui 0.36.2 sets a node's parent only through a `Ui` or `Ui::interact`, both of which register a widget rect per card, so it cannot be done at this cost.
  - **Cost.** 300 sets at 1500×900, measured once with a bare `egui::Context` (the benchmark was not kept): 294 µs a frame before, 311 µs after (+6%) with AccessKit off, which is how a window runs without a screen reader; 357 µs before and 430 µs after with it on. The widget rects registered stay 354. Off-screen cards are still not drawn.
  - **Heights.** A change of the column width of 0.5 pt or more (`DropState::card_w`) moves each remembered height by the chip rows it adds or removes times (chip + `DOCK_GAP_Y`): rows come from the set's slot row, missing entries and extra members at `CHIP` wide with the card's margins (`chip_rows`, `reflow_heights`). An id no longer in the views is forgotten. The estimate leaves out the name row, which wraps below the pill when the column is narrow; drawing a card measures it exactly.
  - **Anchor.** The first card in view is remembered with its top in the content, summed from the remembered heights and not from the laid-out rects (egui rounds each allocation, which drifted a pixel over 200 cards). The next frame sets the scroll offset so that card stays where it was on screen while the heights above it change (the mean of the measured ones moves, a width change re-flowed them). A card that comes into view with a height other than its estimate still moves the cards below it for that one frame. A frame where `model.sets_filter` differs from last frame's copy scrolls to the top instead, unless a reveal is pending (HP5); other list changes keep the anchor.
  - **Far jump.** A reveal of a card outside last frame's view lands it at the top of the view, 2 × the item spacing below the edge, not at the least scroll that shows it: an estimated height left it cut off and a second scroll moved it. Once that card has been laid out (`DropState::reveal_seen` holds its key; another target, or a frame with no reveal, takes the jump path again) the reveal scrolls to its measured rect and no longer re-aims from the heights, which moved each frame. Jumps stay instant.
  - **Tests.** `set_cards::{off_screen_set_cards_have_accesskit_nodes_and_tab_stops, card_heights_refresh_after_a_resize, a_far_jump_does_not_shift_the_scroll, a_go_to_set_in_the_same_frame_wins_over_a_tab_reveal}` (the first three failed before: no node for an off-screen card; 124 pt at 520 wide and at the minimum, where the drawn card is 481; the card moved 43 pt between the first frames). `docs/wiki/GUI-Sets-Column.md` drops the Tab limit.
  - **Step 1.** Live: every ruling. The three `shortcut:` comments in `sets_column.rs` are gone.
  - Supersedes: In part: CF7 (the Tab and screen-reader limit, the stale off-screen heights, the shift after a jump, the least-scroll jump) and FC8 (CF7 as a kept shortcut).
- **FM33 TruePBR editors: only active feature cards; Displacement scale under Material (plan task 22, owner 2026-11-01 tune-ups 1 and 2, 2026-11-03).**
  - **Rule.** A feature's group (Coat, Emissive settings, Glint settings, Fuzz settings) is drawn only while the feature is on, blocked included (its header says "on, blocked by {feature}", Note FC9), or while the feature is off but the set's JSON still holds a value of the group (`pgp_fields::kept_unused`). A kept group reads "off", opens by default and shows the line "kept, not used" above its rows, which stay editable: a reset removes one stored value, and an edit still writes nothing to the PGPatcher JSON while the feature is off (review of task 22). Nothing is deleted or rewritten by showing it: the values stay as stored and the writer skips them (AJ4). The group's id carries the on and kept state, so it opens when either changes.
  - **Parallax has no group.** While Parallax is on, **Displacement scale** is a row of Material under Roughness scale; the key, its range, drag feel and number format are unchanged (`displacement_scale`, schema group `Parallax`, so the writer's live-feature rule is unchanged). Parallax off with a value stored: the row stays and "kept, not used" shows under the grid. A pbr_grass set has no row (grass ignores it).
  - **Subsurface and NiAlphaProperty stay listed.** Both hold their own switch: Subsurface's Off / Standard / Foliage selector and NiAlphaProperty's flag, threshold, create and delete boxes are inside the group, and the Features group has no tick for either, so hiding them would leave no way to turn them on. Neither expands into nothing while off (the selector and the boxes show). Subsurface follows the kept rule for its colour and opacity (open, "kept, not used"); NiAlphaProperty's keys are its own switches, so a stored key is always in use and the group reads "set". Hair has no group.
  - **The JSON editor** draws no groups, only the keys an entry has as pills, so it already listed only what is stored. Its pill for a key of an off feature now says "kept, not used", by the same rule.
  - **Tests.** `truepbr_cards::{only_active_feature_cards_show, ticking_glint_shows_its_card, parallax_puts_displacement_scale_under_roughness_scale, a_feature_off_with_stored_values_says_kept_not_used}`, each on the set editor's TruePBR panel and the JSON editor; `set_panel` and `coat_card` take the new listing; a blocked feature with a stored value is pinned too. `docs/wiki/GUI-PGPatcher-JSON.md` and CHANGELOG say it. The exporter and validators are untouched (display only); no NIF-team file changed.
  - Supersedes: In part: AJ1 and AJ4 (the Coat group always listed and its count note), CC9 and CC15 (the feature groups always listed; Parallax in the group order).
- **FM34 Alpha noise near 255 counts as opaque (plan task 24, tune-up 3; the owner delegated the rule on 2026-11-03).**
  - **Rule.** Alpha is classed `Constant255`, the class a fully 255 image gets, when the texels below 251 are fewer than 0.1 % of the image (`ALPHA_SPECK_SHARE`) and no 4-connected group of them is larger than 16 texels (`ALPHA_SPECK_MAX_GROUP`). Otherwise the minimum-251 rule of Note L applies unchanged (`NEAR_OPAQUE_ALPHA`). It is in `analyse_with`, so the policy ask, LOSS-ALPHA-DATA, `mesh_remove` and `suggest_alpha` all read it from `AlphaContent`. The group check runs only when the low-texel count is already under the limit and stops at the first group over 16, so its work is bounded. The rule lives in one place, `analyse::low_alpha_is_speck_noise` over a decoded alpha plane, with `is_low_alpha` as the one low-alpha predicate; the CLI's DDS sub-type inference (`VARYING_ALPHA_BELOW`) decodes mip 0 and calls the same function, so a stored `_m` with a few stray low texels infers as the engine's class implies (review of task 24). The encode path never reads the class: only the inferred alpha policy, rule matching on alpha content and a complex `_m`'s default parallax do, so an explicit `alpha.policy` (`data`, `cutout`, `blend`) keeps its alpha as before and a default-policy file whose only low texels are stray now removes its alpha (a complex `_m` becomes an env mask, parallax off).
  - **Why this rule (task 23 survey of the owner's mods).** No path bug was found. 0 of the 457 mesh-confirmed real alpha textures change class. The three glow maps with a false block (`clutter/birthsignthief01_g`, `birthsignwarrior01_g`, `skeletonkey/skeletonkey_g`) lose their blocking LOSS-ALPHA-DATA, and 135 normals whose minimum is 249 or 250 become opaque. The smallest real cutout is 0.31 % of its image (`weapons/elven/bow/bow`), three times the share limit. Share limits of 0.5 % and 1 % were rejected: they silence 4 and 20 real cutouts. The group cap exists because 0.1 % of a 4K texture is 16 777 texels, which would let a small real cutout through. `sprigganburnt2` is a real cutout (12 alpha-test shapes) and still asks.
  - **Analysis version 2 → 3.** An analysis cached by an earlier build of this branch holds the old alpha class. `tests/analysis_version.json` is re-pinned.
  - **Encode versions.** None moves: `encode_versions` passes with `identity.json` unchanged, and the `encode_version` arms stay "2.0.0" (FM9). No encode fixture's alpha class flips.
  - **Tests.** `analyse::tests::{a_few_low_texels_are_opaque_but_a_real_cutout_or_gradient_is_not, a_connected_group_over_16_texels_is_not_noise}` and `policy_checks::{alpha_noise_near_255_asks_nothing_but_real_alpha_still_asks, a_glow_map_with_a_few_texels_at_250_has_no_loss_alpha_data}` (all failed with the share at 0), `analyse::tests::code_250_is_low_and_251_is_not_at_the_speck_boundaries` and `cli_inspect_validate::validate_treats_stray_low_alpha_texels_as_opaque`. The tests that stood one low texel in for varying alpha now use 8 to 16 texels (`alpha_codes` gives each code 8).
  - Supersedes: In part: L (Rule, Below the cutoff) and AT1 (the test for `complex_material`). BJ1 stands: the gate stays 251.
- **FM35 The alpha question is one dropdown on the resolved type (plan task 25, tune-up 4).**
  - **Which states ask.** Only a texture with no mesh evidence asks. Where a scan covers the texture, `mesh_alpha_policy` decides and `Provenance::MeshAlpha` counts as explicit, so neither `Need::AlphaMeaning` nor LOSS-ALPHA-DATA is raised and the texture is Ready; a non-remove mesh type is then written into the project at once ("Set 1 value automatically", `auto_record`), and a mesh `remove` stays `mesh:alpha` and GUI-only. A mesh-decided texture is not turned back into a question. Its panel shows the Alpha row's dropdown on the mesh's type, with "from the meshes that use this texture" beside it.
  - **One line.** A Problems or Review row that asks a texture's alpha shows `Alpha: <Type> (proposed) — confirm` (`code_text::alpha_confirm_text`; the type names are the five that the old buttons used), as does the preview's strip; the texture panel asks it as an Alpha row with Confirm (HI7, HI13). It replaces three lines: the Undecided sentence, the "carries data the format would drop" line with the engine's "non-constant alpha would be discarded", and the Acknowledge button. A row that asks only its alpha has no `Undecided:` prefix; a row that also asks something else keeps the prefix and joins the parts. Only "(proposed)" exists: a texture whose meshes decide its alpha is Ready and never asked, so there is no "(from mesh)" text.
  - **One dropdown.** `settings_panel::alpha_question` (its private `alpha_dropdown` and **Confirm**, HI7) is the control in the Problems row, Review's inline row and the settings box. It opens on the proposal (`items::alpha_asked`: `suggest_alpha` over the cached analysis, else the profile's own policy). Picking an entry, the shown one too, or Confirm records the answer (on the texture panel's Alpha row, picking the shown value writes nothing, HI14) with the old buttons' write (`alpha.policy` in the texture's own entry through `decide` or `answer_undecided_row`, nothing in `allow`). Enter or Space on a focused Problems row that asks only its alpha confirms the shown type (a row that also asks a sub-type ignores the key, so it never answers just one of the two); Accept all suggestions stays type-only. The `Decide…` menu and the five buttons are gone. The dropdown's accessible name is `Alpha type: <shown>`.
  - **Acknowledge.** While the alpha is asked, `decisions_of` offers no `Acknowledge(LossAlphaData)`: the type answers it (Remove is an explicit drop, any other type is kept), and the settings panel's Warnings list does not repeat it. The row's hover still names the code. After the type is chosen the code can return for a format that cannot store it; that is its own `Acknowledge` row and keeps `ack_label` ("Drop the alpha anyway"), because it records an `allow` entry that no type choice makes. Acknowledge for every other code is unchanged.
  - **Engine text.** The unconfirmed reason reads `alpha meaning is unconfirmed (proposed Cutout: …)` and the proposals no longer end in "the mesh decides", since no mesh did. Policy outcomes, codes, severities and output bytes do not change; `encode_versions` passes with `identity.json` unchanged.
  - **Tests.** `mesh_alpha::{a_mesh_resolved_cutout_shows_no_proposal_text, without_a_mesh_the_short_proposal_shows}`; `problems_rows::{the_problems_row_names_the_type, one_line_replaces_the_three_alpha_lines, picking_a_type_in_the_row_writes_what_the_old_buttons_wrote, a_mesh_remove_is_recorded_and_not_asked}`; `problems_accept::{enter_on_a_proposed_alpha_row_confirms_the_shown_type, enter_on_a_row_with_a_subtype_and_an_alpha_question_does_nothing}`; the old Decide… tests now pick from the dropdown (`ui_smoke`, `bottom_panel`, `no_codes`, `review_tab`). `the_decide_menu_starts_with_alpha_means` is deleted with the menu. No NIF-team file changed.
  - Supersedes: In part: CA11 (Decide… among the fix buttons), FB8 (the hover on the Decide… menu and Review's buttons), FB9 (Decide… rows stay menus), FC9 (the buttons stay Decide…), FI11 (the Decide… menu's lead line) and FI14 (the example row), FK4 (the alpha buttons in the wait test).
- **FM36 `channels.grayscale_source` picks R, G, B, A or luma (plan task 26, tune-up 6).**
  - **Rule.** `GraySource` is `r`, `g`, `b`, `a`, `luma` (the 1.x values `r` and `luma` keep their names; nothing is renamed, so there is no `migrate::REMOVED` row). The default stays `r`, serialised as before, so existing configs, profile hashes and output bytes do not change. Every reader takes the pick through `GraySource::read` (the packed assembly, the grayscale preprocess) and names it through `GraySource::label` (`source:<file>:G`, the `CHAN-NOT-GRAY` text). An `_orm` or `_rmaos` slot's own `channel` and `helper.read` still beat the pick, and an alpha fill is unaffected. The Also derive reads a set's raw maps with the pick of the set's packed `_rmaos` (else `_p`), so Also's `_m` and diffuse agree with the packed output (`DeriveSet::gray`, from `also::derive_sets`).
  - **Alpha.** A grayscale map that picks `a` reads the source alpha even under `alpha.policy = remove` (`preprocess_with` keeps the alpha until the grey is read, then drops it as before), and its alpha is data, so `LOSS-ALPHA-DATA` is not raised for it. A source with no alpha channel reads 255; that is `CHAN-NOT-GRAY` naming the pick ("no alpha channel; keeping A, which reads as 255"; in a pack "<file>: no alpha channel; packing its A as 255"), not a new code.
  - **The check follows the pick.** The default `r` and `luma` assume a grey source: `CHAN-NOT-GRAY` means R, G and B differ by more than 4/255 and names the pick ("keeping R"). A `g`, `b` or `a` pick is never the default, so it is the user's data: it is reported only when the picked channel is flat (range within 4/255, one shared constant `analyse::FLAT_CODES` and `is_flat`, used by `policy` and the pack assembly), or for `a` when the source has no alpha channel. When the picked R, G, B or A is flat and another channel varies, the message adds the first varying channel in R, G, B, A order and the setting that picks it. The pack assembly makes the same test per source, on the channel it packed. The analysis cache key already holds `PackJob::gray`, so no cached entry is read under another pick and `ANALYSIS_VERSION` stays 3. The luma helper moved from `pack::assemble` to `image::luma`, so `config` no longer depends on `pack`.
  - **Per-source pick (tune-up 6).** A raw map's own pick is `helper.read` (Note DY), which already applies to a raw map wherever it lands in a recipe, is set per file in `[files]` or `--set file:<glob>.helper.read`, and beats this setting for that source; the packed output's `channels.grayscale_source` is the fallback for every source without one. So no second per-source field exists and no planner change was needed. A pack warning has no suggestion, as before.
  - **GUI.** The helper panel's "Read from" dropdown (Auto (R), R, G, B, A, Luminance) is the per-raw-map control and writes `helper.read` to the map's `[files]` entry, so no GUI file changed. The texture panel has no `channels.grayscale_source` row; the pick is set in `sdc.toml`, a preset or `--set`.
  - **Encode versions.** None moves: `encode_versions` passes with the existing `identity.json` entries and `profile_hashes.json` unchanged. Two entries are added, `cov/textures/c/gpick_rmaos.dds` (a pack whose roughness holds its value in G, read with `channels.grayscale_source = "g"`) and its default-R twin `rpick_rmaos.dds`; the test asserts the two hashes differ, so the pin fails if the override is ignored.
  - **Tests.** `pack_assemble::a_pick_reads_g_b_or_alpha_and_the_check_measures_that_channel`, `pack_recipes::channel_provenance_strings`, `derive_plan::the_derive_set_reads_the_packed_outputs_grayscale_pick`, `policy_checks::{chan_not_gray_names_the_pick_and_suggests_a_channel, an_explicit_pick_on_a_varying_channel_is_not_warned, an_alpha_pick_needs_an_alpha_channel_and_is_not_a_discarded_alpha}`, `ops::tests::grayscale_picks_g_b_and_the_alpha_a_remove_would_drop` and, end to end through a written DDS, `pack_run::{a_value_stored_in_green_packs_from_green, an_alpha_pick_reads_alpha, a_per_source_read_beats_the_output_pick}`. No NIF-team file changed.
  - Supersedes: In part: M6 (the pick was R or luma) and M12 (the provenance names R or luma only).
- **FM37 A `gray` sub-type: BC4 or R8, red by default, picked by hand (plan task 27, tune-up 5; owner answers 2026-11-03).**
  - **Rule.** `Subtype::Gray` (id `gray`, `Category::Grayscale`, last in `Subtype::ALL`, so last in the picker's Grayscale maps group) is a plain grey map. Its `[subtypes.gray]` row in `sdc-base.toml` is `format = "bc4"`; colour (linear UNORM), alpha (`remove`) and `channels.grayscale_source = "r"` are the base defaults, so a Gray encode of an RGB source writes its red channel, and a `g`, `b`, `a` or `luma` pick (FM36) works as for every grayscale map. The preset version and `docs/presets-changelog.md` stay with the release task (T18-c).
  - **Hand-picked only.** No suffix, no `suggest.rs` entry, no shader role, no analysis suggestion, no PGPatcher or CS PBR mapping, and no slot card (`slot_kind` is `None`). It is chosen in the type picker or with `subtype = "gray"` in a `[files]` entry or `--set`. It has no `alpha_from_mesh`, so its alpha resolves as Height's does: `remove`, with no question, and a varying source alpha raises `LOSS-ALPHA-DATA` unless the pick is `a`.
  - **Formats.** BC4 or R8, UNORM, under both targets, whether or not the source is grey. `policy::encoding_warnings` raises `CFG-INVALID` ("gray is written as BC4 or R8") for any other format; it is the one source of `allowed_encodings`, so the Format dropdown (`fields::encoding_options`) lists exactly the two and a hand edit to another format is blocked. Other grayscale types still list BC1 to BC7, RGBA8, R8 and RG8.
  - **Encode versions.** `encode_version(Gray)` is "2.0.0" (Note DM). `identity.json` gains three keys and no existing key changes: `textures/h/gray.dds` and `vanilla/textures/h/gray.dds` (the forced fixture every sub-type gets; Gray joins the no-alpha source list) and the coverage fixture `cov/textures/c/gray_r8.dds` (the R8 form, from an RGB source). `encode_inputs.json` gains the two forced fixtures and `profile_hashes.json` the twelve `gray` rows (six presets, two targets); both are additions only.
  - **Check of the dropdowns (report only, nothing fixed).** Every sub-type under both targets, a grey and a colour source, every format and tag: the dropdown (`allowed_encodings_for`) against `resolve_file` with that format and tag forced. No pair is accepted by the engine and missing from the dropdown. Pairs the dropdown lists but Resolve blocks: BC1 on every sub-type whose default alpha policy is `data` (`normal`, `landscape_normal`, `detail_normal`, `coat_normal`, `complex_material`, `generic_data`, `skin_wet` colour, and `pbr_subsurface` and `pbr_fuzz` as sRGB under `cs`), `FMT-BC1-ALPHA`, until the alpha is set to `remove`; and BC7 on `complex_grass` (`GRASS-SHAPE`, a source-shape check the dropdown cannot see). Gray has no mismatch.
  - **Compact presets.** `cs-compact` and `vanilla-compact` halve a Gray map like the other grayscale types (`[subtypes.gray] resize = "half"`, Note FM41).
  - **Tests.** `allowed_encodings::gray_allows_bc4_and_r8_only` (failed first: the list held BC1, BC3, BC5, BC7, RG8 and RGBA8), `encode::a_gray_encode_of_an_rgb_source_writes_red` (BC4 and R8), `config::tests::{gray_is_a_grayscale_bc4_read_from_red, subtype_all_order}`, `fields::the_gray_format_dropdown_has_two_entries` and `type_picker::gray_is_in_the_grayscale_group`. `docs/wiki/Texture-Types.md` and CHANGELOG say it; the schemas gain the `gray` id and its `[subtypes.gray]` key. No NIF-team file changed.
- **FM38 Legacy uncompressed DDS: 24-bit RGB is read and written, A8L8 and L16 are read (plan task 27B; owner 2026-11-03).**
  - **Why.** A survey of the owner's 93,234 loose DDS files (2026-11-03) found 476 24-bit R8G8B8 files, 166 of them winning in game across 19 mods (UBE 2.0, COtR, Heartbreaker, Starsight Eyes and others); the vanilla tint masks are 24-bit too, so the game loads the format. It also found 59 winning L16 files (CS terrain heightmaps) and 1 winning A8L8 (a CS shader LUT), and no 565, 1555, 4444, X8B8G8R8 or A8 file. The owner's rule is "output yes if mods can use it", so 24-bit gets write support; A8L8 and L16 stay read only, and the layouts the survey did not find stay unsupported (the existing `Unsupported("uncompressed layout …")` error).
  - **Reader.** `DdsPixelFormat` gains `Rgb8` (24 bits, masks R `0xff0000`, G `0xff00`, B `0xff`: bytes B, G, R, rows unpadded), `La8` (masks R `0xff`, A `0xff00`, at a bit count of 16 or of 8, which some writers put on it; two bytes a texel either way) and `R16` (16 bits, mask `0xffff`). The decode is `[r, g, b, 255]`, `[l, l, l, a]` and `[h, h, h, 255]` from the high byte of an L16 (the decoder has no 16-bit grey path, so it is LDR only). A8L8 means L to RGB and A to alpha in Tessalith's decode; how the game's loader maps it was not verified, so the decode follows the file's meaning. As a DDS input (`load.rs`) they are 8-bit sources: RGB8 has 3 channels, L16 has 1, and A8L8 has 4 channels with alpha, not 2, because `analyse` takes `channels == 2` for a two-channel (Z-less) normal source and an A8L8 file is grey plus alpha (`legacy_uncompressed_layouts_load_with_the_source_info_of_what_they_hold`). `dxgi()` is 0 for all three: they have no DXGI format the reader maps back, and A8L8 must not read as R8G8 (49), which `policy` takes for a two-channel normal. An R, G, B ordered 24-bit file stays unsupported (`malformed_headers_are_rejected_without_panicking` now uses it where it used the B, G, R order).
  - **Writer.** `Format::Rgb8` (id `rgb8`, not block, no alpha, not HDR, 3 bytes a texel) is `pixel_format` `Rgb8`, packed B, G, R by `pack_uncompressed`. It has a legacy header only: `DDPF_RGB`, 24 bits, those masks, no alpha mask, no DX10 block, the pitch one unpadded row (`3 * w`), mips allowed. `resolve_header` returns legacy for it whatever `header` says, and `header_bytes` refuses a DX10 header for it (`DdsError::NoDx10Form`). `as_format` reads a written 24-bit file back as `(Rgb8, false)`, so `validate` judges it like any other format (the PSNR channels are R, G, B).
  - **Policy.** `encoding_warnings` (the one source of `allowed_encodings` and the GUI format dropdown) treats `rgb8` as `rgba8` for every rule, with one more: the `_SRGB` tag is `CFG-INVALID` ("RGB8 has no _SRGB form"), because a legacy header cannot carry it. So RGB8 is offered exactly where `(rgba8, UNORM)` is, cubemaps included (controller ruling T27B-c: the owner's load order has a winning 24-bit file under `textures\cubemaps`; the cubemap list is BC1, BC3, BC7, RGBA8 and RGB8, which amends that list in Note DS). A 24-bit cube writes with the legacy header and the cube caps (`an_rgb8_cube_round_trips_with_a_legacy_header`). `check_profile` adds `FMT-RGB8-ALPHA` (Blocking, `Code::ALL` has 94; "RGB8 stores no alpha, but alpha.policy is <id>; …") for any alpha policy but `remove`, except an empty `data` alpha (FM39), as `FMT-BC1-ALPHA` does for BC1, and `CFG-INVALID` for an explicit `header = "dx10"`. `--allow FMT-RGB8-ALPHA` writes the colour and drops the alpha. `stores_alpha` does not count `rgb8`, so a varying alpha under an inferred `remove` still blocks with `LOSS-ALPHA-DATA`.
  - **GUI.** The dropdown label is `RGB8 · linear (24-bit, uncompressed)` (the label convention is `<name> · <what>`, and a test pins it; controller ruling T27B-d); the tag row is locked with "RGB8 has no _SRGB form". The Problems sentence for `FMT-RGB8-ALPHA` is in `code_text.rs`. A 24-bit DDS previews from the CPU decode, as every uncompressed layout does.
  - **render3d (NIF team crate, `textures.rs` only; controller ruling).** A 24-bit body texture failed to load in the 3D view, so the three layouts upload: L16 as `R16_UNORM` (56) as stored, 24-bit RGB and A8L8 expanded per level and face by `expand_legacy` (pure; B, G, R becomes R, G, B, 255; L, A becomes L, L, L, A, the engine's decode) and uploaded as `R8G8B8A8_UNORM` (28). `load_dds_as` still refuses a format that maps to 0. The line is in `nifparseref\plan\HANDOFF.md`.
  - **Encode versions.** Every arm stays "2.0.0" (no existing output changes). `identity.json` gains two keys and no existing key changes: `cov/textures/c/opaque_rgb8.dds` (a 64 x 64 opaque diffuse) and `cov/textures/c/wide_rgb8.dds` (32 x 16, a 2:1 mip chain). `encode_inputs.json` and `profile_hashes.json` are untouched. Schemas: the new code and the `rgb8` format id.
  - **Tests.** `dds_read::{a_24_bit_bgr_file_reads_with_its_rows_unpadded, an_a8l8_file_reads_as_grey_with_alpha, an_a8l8_file_with_an_8_bit_count_reads_the_same, an_l16_file_reads_as_grey, other_legacy_layouts_stay_unsupported, rgb8_output_round_trips_through_the_reader}`, `dds_read::{an_rgb8_cube_round_trips_with_a_legacy_header, an_rgb8_dx10_header_is_refused}`, `load::legacy_uncompressed_layouts_load_with_the_source_info_of_what_they_hold`, `out_size` (RGB8 is 3 bytes a texel; the estimate reads `pixel_format`), render3d `legacy_layouts_expand_to_rgba8_as_the_engine_decodes_them`, `policy_checks::{rgb8_is_refused_when_alpha_is_kept, rgb8_is_refused_with_an_srgb_tag}`, `allowed_encodings::rgb8_is_allowed_where_rgba8_unorm_is`, `fields::the_dropdown_offers_rgb8_where_rgba8_unorm_is_offered`, the `encode` pack unit test, the `config` classification test and the `encode_versions` coverage fixtures. The first run failed to compile (no `Rgb8`, `La8`, `R16` or `FmtRgb8Alpha`). Main-team files only except render3d's `textures.rs`.
- **FM39 BC1 and RGB8 on a `data`-alpha type when the alpha is empty (plan task 27C; owner 2026-11-03, controller ruling T27C-a).**
  - **Why.** The Task 27 dropdown audit found 31 refusals, 9 of them `FMT-BC1-ALPHA` on `data` types (normals, `complex_material`, `generic_data`, `coat_normal`, `skin_wet`, `pbr_subsurface`, `pbr_fuzz`). An opaque BC1 or RGB8 decodes alpha as 255, so an alpha that is already 255 everywhere is kept exactly. The owner: "bc1 on rmaos and _m should be supported if the alpha maps are empty, _m would need to be 0, rmaos could just be rgb".
  - **Rule (controller rulings T27C-a and T27C-b).** With `alpha.policy = data`, BC1 and RGB8 are accepted when the alpha the run writes is empty: the analysis says `none` (no alpha channel), or its 8-bit histogram has `eq255` equal to the texel count (255 everywhere). The near-opaque class of FM34 (`constant_255` with a few low or stray texels) is not empty here: under an explicit `data`, a stray texel is data, so one texel at 200 keeps the refusal. Any other alpha keeps `FMT-BC1-ALPHA` and `FMT-RGB8-ALPHA`, so the two formats agree. The dropdown is unchanged: it lists BC1 and RGB8 per sub-type and Resolve decides.
  - **The pick is kept (ruling T27C-c).** The profile stays `alpha.policy = data`, so the GUI shows what the user picked; `check_profile` only skips the refusal. BC1 and RGB8 never store the alpha, and `data` builds no alpha-weighted mips, so the file has the bytes of the `remove` encode (golden `bc1_with_empty_data_alpha_matches_the_remove_encode` compares them). There is no encoder flag.
  - **Which alpha is judged (ruling T27C-a).** The one the run writes. A packed output is analysed after assembly, so an `_rmaos` whose specular took its neutral default (255, DD9) is empty and accepted. A target filled by a helper (`_specular`, `_opacity`, `_cnr`/`_f`/`_s` weights, `helper.read`) is analysed again with the fill applied (`api.rs` fill step), so a fill that writes anything but 255 is not empty and BC1 refuses it, while a fill that is solid 255 is accepted.
  - **`complex_material` keeps the refusal.** Its empty alpha is written as 0 (`ops::complex_alpha_zero`, AH1/AH2), which BC1 cannot hold with colour (a transparent BC1 texel is black), and an opaque `_m` would be read as an env mask (the CS rule in Note AT). The message says so: "BC1 cannot keep a complex material's alpha 0; an opaque `_m` is read as an env mask. Use BC7/BC3, or the envmask type." The sentence is for an empty alpha (the rule above); a `complex_material` with varying alpha gets the generic `FMT-BC1-ALPHA` message (ruling T27C-d). RGB8 on it keeps the FM38 sentence. The owner confirmed keeping this refusal.
  - **Encode versions.** Every arm stays "2.0.0". `identity.json` gains `cov/textures/c/rmaos_bc1_data255.dds` (an `_rmaos` with solid 255 alpha as BC1, `alpha.policy = data`) and no existing key changes.
  - **Tests.** `policy_checks::{bc1_accepts_an_rmaos_whose_alpha_is_constant_255, bc1_accepts_a_normal_with_no_alpha, bc1_still_refuses_a_varying_data_alpha, bc1_refuses_a_data_alpha_with_one_stray_texel, bc1_refuses_a_complex_material_with_empty_alpha_and_says_why, bc1_refuses_a_varying_complex_material_alpha_with_the_generic_message}` (the accept and stray tests cover RGB8 too), golden `bc1_with_empty_data_alpha_matches_the_remove_encode` (byte for byte) and `a_packed_rmaos_with_a_default_specular_is_accepted_on_bc1` (real files, decoded alpha), `helper_fill_run::a_helper_filled_alpha_decides_whether_bc1_keeps_data_alpha`, the `encode_versions` fixture. The first run failed: `data` with an empty alpha was `FMT-BC1-ALPHA`. `bc1_blocks_every_alpha_policy_but_remove`, `bc1_with_alpha_still_blocks_without_the_setting` and `rgb8_is_refused_when_alpha_is_kept` now use a varying alpha for their `data` rows.
  - Supersedes: FM20 (in part: "`data` always blocks") and FM38 (in part: RGB8 refuses every policy but `remove`).
