- **FM42 Perceptual BC1 (plan task 28A; owner 2026-11-03, amends Note CP's scope).**
  - **Why.** The owner wants perceptual BC1, which he declined on 2026-11-01 (FJ7 Q3, edited). Weighting rgbcx's BC1 colour error by Rec.709 luma trades RGB PSNR and ΔE76 for luma PSNR and luma SSIM at the same encode time; the measurements, first on the Ideal model and again with cBC1Dual, are in `docs/research/encoder-benchmark.md#luma-weighted-bc1-error-2026-10-31`.
  - **One switch.** `encoder.perceptual` now reaches BC1 and BC3's colour block as well as BC7; there is no new field and the base default stays `false`. BC7 keeps bc7e's own weights (Note CP, unchanged). BC1, BC1 with 1-bit alpha and BC3 colour weight R, G, B by 54, 183, 19 (Rec.709 luma of 256) in every colour error sum rgbcx compares, the cBC1Dual sum included, so the weights and Dual combine (README-VENDOR.md, patch "rgbcx BC1 perceptual"). Other formats ignore the flag. With it off every output is byte-identical: `identity.json` keeps every key and gains `cov/textures/c/perceptual_bc1.dds`; `encode_inputs.json` and `profile_hashes.json` do not change (`PresetRegistry::profile` applies no rules, so the vanilla-compact rule edit does not reach the pins; no fixture runs vanilla-compact). Every `encode_version` arm stays "2.0.0".
  - **Per thread.** The weights are `thread_local` in rgbcx and the shim sets them around each call, because the engine encodes block rows of different files in parallel.
  - **Refusal.** `FMT-PERCEPTUAL-DATA` is unchanged in rule (colour without data alpha only) and now reads "perceptual BC1/BC3/BC7 is allowed only for colour without data alpha".
  - **Presets.** `vanilla-compact` sets `encoder.perceptual = true` in its two BC1 colour `[[rules]]`, not in `[defaults]` (data types would hit `FMT-PERCEPTUAL-DATA`); Note FM43 adds the `diffuse`, `glow` and `skin_albedo` tables of `vanilla-quality` (inherited by `vanilla-compact`), so those are perceptual in BC3 and BC7 too. `cs-compact` and the other presets stay off. The preset version stays "2.0.0" (2.0 is unreleased); `docs/presets-changelog.md` has a line in its 2.0.0 entry. The `rgbcx_patch` tag gains `+bc1-perceptual`, so a 2.0 manifest from before this change re-encodes its BC1 to BC5 outputs once.
  - **GUI.** The BC7 weighting dropdown keeps its Perceptual option for BC7. For BC1 and BC3 a **Perceptual** tick (`encoder.perceptual`, Compression group, hidden on other formats) edits the same field; its lock is the dropdown option's (colour without data alpha). Two surfaces, one field: the formats never show both.
  - **Tests.** `encoders-sys` `bc1_perceptual::{perceptual_bc1_differs_and_off_is_byte_identical, perceptual_is_per_thread}` (off is pinned to the pre-patch bytes of BC1, BC1 alpha and BC3), engine `encode::perceptual_bc1_raises_y_psnr_on_a_colour_gradient` (BC1 and BC3), `policy_checks::perceptual_bc1_on_a_normal_is_refused`, `golden::vanilla_compact_bc1_colour_is_perceptual_and_data_is_not`, the `compact_and_fast_preview_resolve_as_designed` pin, `encode_versions`, GUI `bc7_weighting::the_perceptual_tick_shows_for_bc1`. The first run failed to compile in `encoders-sys` (no flag) and failed the rest.
- **FM43 BC6H defaults to the peak mode; vanilla colour is perceptual (plan task 28C; owner 2026-11-03, amends FF1 and FM42's Presets bullet).**
  - **Why.** The owner: "since were already changing goldens, we might aswell role this in Bc6hLevel::VeryslowPeak to the default pick, keep very slow selectable". Later: "bc3 and bc7 should both be on perceptual for vanilla diffuse for presets", then "glow and skin albedo too".
  - **BC6H effort.** `bc6h_level` is unchanged (FF1). `layered_profile_with` sets `encoder.effort = slowest` when the final format is `bc6h_uf16` and `encoder.effort` has no layer above the hidden base (provenance `Builtin` or none). A user or project preset, a config, a rule, `[files]` or `--set` that names the effort wins, `veryslow` included, and so does `fast-preview` (a built-in preset above the base). It is code, not a `[subtypes.pbr_emissive]` table, because a table cannot see the final format: an 8-bit emissive is BC7 (`emissive_hdr_choice`), and the table's `slowest` survived that, so `identity.json`'s `textures/h/pbr_g.dds` (BC7) changed; a table also loses to nothing but sub-type and file scope, so a user preset's `defaults.encoder.effort` could not reach BC6H. A rule cannot match a resolved format either. The step also covers a file whose format is forced to BC6H by hand. The step is the helper `presets::bc6h_peak_default`. `PresetRegistry::profile` (the pin) runs no resolve step and still says `veryslow` for the BC6H sub-types, but `sdc presets show` and the preset editor call the helper too (`PresetRegistry::effective_profile`, the editor's row model), so they show `slowest` with the reason (`builtin (BC6H default)` in `presets show`, "(BC6H default)" on the editor's base hint). The stored preset value is unchanged, and an explicit `veryslow` shows as itself.
  - **Vanilla perceptual.** `vanilla-quality` gets `[subtypes.diffuse]`, `[subtypes.glow]` and `[subtypes.skin_albedo]` with `encoder = { perceptual = true }`, merged into the base encoder table (effort and radius stay); `vanilla-compact` inherits them and keeps its BC1 rules' flag. These sub-types are perceptual whether they resolve to BC1, BC3 or BC7. `subsurface_tint`, `backlight_tint`, `ui_sky`, `landscape_diffuse` and the PBR types stay off (controller ruling T28C-b). `skin_albedo` is unavailable under the vanilla target, so its table only shows in `presets show --target cs` and the pin; it has no encode effect. `cs-*` and `fast-preview` are unchanged.
  - **Data alpha.** `FMT-PERCEPTUAL-DATA` refuses perceptual on data alpha, so a built-in preset's `perceptual` is dropped at resolve when `alpha.policy` is `data` and no explicit layer set `perceptual` (a diffuse whose alpha the user calls data would otherwise be blocked). An explicit `perceptual = true` with data alpha is kept and refused as before.
  - **Pins.** The arms are all "2.0.0" and `RELEASED_ENGINE_VERSION` is already "2.0.0", so `bumped()` accepts nothing this cycle; the stale keys were deleted and re-pinned by hand. `profile_hashes.json`: `vanilla-quality/*` and `vanilla-compact/*` for `cs` and `vanilla`, `diffuse`, `glow`, `skin_albedo` (12 keys); no BC6H key (the pin does not run resolve). `encode_inputs.json`: `textures/h/hdr_sky.dds` (effort) and `vanilla/textures/{h/diffuse,h/glow,pbr/rock,rnm/iron}.dds`. `identity.json`: those four vanilla files; `textures/h/hdr_sky.dds` keeps its bytes (the 64 px fixture encodes the same in both BC6H modes), so no BC6H identity key changed. Version stays 2.0.0. `if-changed` skips a file only when both the recorded `encode_version` arm and the SHA-256 of the resolved job profile match (`api::is_up_to_date`). The arms do not move, so what re-encodes is a file whose profile hash moved: a BC6H output (effort) or a vanilla diffuse, glow or skin albedo (perceptual) written by an earlier 2.0 build. A 1.x output has no `encode_version` and re-encodes on its first 2.0 run whatever this note does.
  - **Also export.** The Vanilla phase copies a plain set from the PBR folder only when its profile equals the PBR one but for the target. A diffuse now differs (perceptual under `vanilla-quality`, not under `cs-quality`), so the Vanilla phase encodes it instead of copying; the other plain types still copy. The `also_support::request` helper sets `subtype:diffuse.encoder.perceptual=true` so the copy tests keep a copyable plain set.
  - **Mip PSNR (fix round 1, controller ruling).** Perceptual encoding lowers RGB PSNR: on a smooth 64 px gradient (`golden`'s `rgb(64)`) the 8x8 and 4x4 mips of a vanilla diffuse read 28.95 and 23.00 dB against the 30 dB floor of `VAL-MIP-ERROR`. When `encoder.perceptual` is on for a BC1, BC3 or BC7 texture, the check now scores Rec.709 luma PSNR (`validate::psnr_luma`, the FM42 weights 54/183/19 of 256) against the same floor, and the message says "luma dB". Other formats and non-perceptual files are scored as before. The three golden clean-fixture tests (`vanilla_diffuse_bc7_unorm_no_srgb_full_chain`, `bmp_diffuse_is_bc7_unorm`, `vanilla_g_is_glow_and_m_with_alpha_is_envmask_with_loss`) run with the preset default again and pass. No bytes change; the verdict moves for perceptual files only, which re-encode anyway (Pins).
  - **Tests.** `golden::{bc6h_subtypes_default_to_the_peak_mode, an_explicit_veryslow_on_bc6h_stays_veryslow, vanilla_colour_types_are_perceptual_on_bc1_bc3_and_bc7, cs_quality_diffuse_is_not_perceptual}`, `presets::the_vanilla_presets_mark_three_colour_types_perceptual`, GUI `bc7_weighting::the_bc6h_effort_dropdown_still_offers_veryslow`, `encode_versions`. The preset-table design failed `encode_versions` on the BC7 emissive key first; the vanilla tests failed before the table edit.
  - **Fix round 1 (review of task 28C).** (1) `sdc presets show` and the preset editor show the effective BC6H effort and why (see BC6H effort); tests `cli_presets::presets_show_gives_the_bc6h_default_effort_and_its_reason` and `presets_window::the_editor_shows_the_bc6h_default_effort_and_says_why`, written after the code, so they have no red run. (2) Luma mip PSNR (see Mip PSNR); test `validate::perceptual_mips_are_scored_on_luma`. (3) `also_export::a_perceptual_vanilla_diffuse_is_encoded_not_copied` pins the Vanilla phase re-encoding a perceptual diffuse (the run without the `also_support::request` override). (4) The resolve-step placement (T28C-c) and the hand re-pin (T28C-d) are accepted by the controller.
- **FM44 `resize` relative to a slot of the set (plan task 28B; owner 2026-11-03, amends FM29).**
  - **Why.** Under the compact presets a normal already exported at half its diffuse's size was halved again. The owner asked for the size to follow the set's diffuse, with the reference a choice of slot.
  - **Setting.** `resize_relative_to` (`ResizeRelativeTo`): `self` (default, not serialised, so every existing profile hash and identity holds) or `slot1` to `slot8`. It is in `FIELD_PATHS`, so rules, `[files]` and `--set` take it.
  - **Slot table.** `Subtype::txst_slot()`, an exhaustive match: 1 diffuse, landscape_diffuse, pbr_albedo, skin_albedo, complex_grass; 2 normal, landscape_normal, pbr_normal, msn; 3 pbr_emissive, glow, skin_sk, subsurface_tint; 4 height; 5 cubemap; 6 envmask, complex_material, rmaos, skin_rfaos; 7 coat_normal, pbr_fuzz; 8 pbr_subsurface, backlight_tint, msn_specular; none for the rest (ui_sky, flow, generic types, hdr_sky, skin_wet, projected_noise, detail_normal, gray). It is the engine's own table because the engine cannot read the GUI's `slot_kind` (controller ruling T28B-e). GUI test `resize_field::the_engine_slot_table_matches_the_slot_cards` holds it equal to `slot_kind` and `SlotKind::slot_number` both ways; the sub-types with no slot card (cubemap 5, envmask and complex_material 6, subsurface_tint 3, backlight_tint and msn_specular 8) are listed there by name.
  - **Halving rule.** own = `pot_dims(own)` (own when `pot_exempt`); cap = the longer side of `shrink(pot_dims(reference), resize, resize_floor)`. Both axes halve while the longer side is above the cap and its half is still ≥ `resize_floor`. The map never grows, keeps its aspect and stays a power of two. A 2048 diffuse with `half`: a 2048 normal → 1024, a 1024 or 512 normal keeps its size.
  - **No reference.** `resize = "none"` reads no reference and adds no code, so the compact BC1 colour rules' `resize = "none"` still means full size. A map whose own slot is the named slot is treated as `self`, with no code (controller ruling T28B-c: a slot-1 map "relative to slot1" is the flat half the owner asked for). A set with no map in the slot, more than one, or one whose size cannot be read falls back to `self`: `resize` applies to the map's own size, still stopped by the floor, and the map gets **`RESIZE-NO-REFERENCE`** (Info, data `{slot, maps}`, "…so this map was resized from its own size"). Controller ruling T28B-f: the compact presets must keep shrinking lone maps as 1.x did; the relative rule only protects a map that has a reference to compare with.
  - **Where the reference lives.** The planner writes it into the job's profile, `Profile::resize_reference: Option<(u32, u32)>` (`None` for `self`, `(0, 0)` for no single map, skipped when `None`; controller ruling T28B-d accepts this over a `target_dims` parameter), so every reader of `target_dims` (pipeline, DIM checks, bake plan and its `_n` profile copy, admission, `estimated_bytes`, the GUI size estimate and preview signatures) and the profile hash agree with no new parameter; a changed reference size moves the hash, so `if-changed` re-encodes. It is not a setting: a `[files]` entry, rule or `--set` naming it is refused as an unknown field, and a value in a preset table is overwritten by the planner for every job. `schemas::partial_profile_schema` drops it from the config and preset schemas (the partial profile and the by-product `Profile` definition there); the profile, manifest and event schemas keep it, since plans and records carry it.
  - **Planning.** `resize::plan_references` runs in `plan_sets` after the derive step, before shaders, bakes and `FilePlanned`. Sizes are pass 1's (`cube_facts`, else `input_dims`). A packed or derived map's own size, and its size as a reference, is its sources' common size (`api::job_dims_by`, the FM30 size before `target_dims`); a cube's is its source cell (`cube::layout::source_cell`). The pass recomputes `DIM-RESIZED` only for jobs with a reference (`policy::resized_warning`); a `self` job's warnings stay exactly as `check_profile` made them (unit test `resize::a_self_jobs_dim_resized_is_left_alone`). `DIM-SMALL` stays from `check_profile`, which saw the size without the reference; only a `resize_floor` below 4 could make it stale, since the relative size never falls below the floor.
  - **Presets.** `cs-compact` and `vanilla-compact` keep a plain `resize = "half"` on `diffuse`, `landscape_diffuse`, `pbr_albedo` and `skin_albedo` and add `resize_relative_to = "slot1"` to every other halved sub-type (25). A map in a set with no slot-1 job (a lone normal, `generic_data`, `flow`, a set whose diffuse is a skipped DDS) still halves its own size, as in 1.x, and gets `RESIZE-NO-REFERENCE`.
  - **GUI.** A **Relative to** Advanced texture field after Resize ("Own size", then "Slot N · <slot name>" by `sets::slot_name`), and a preset-editor row under *Resize & mips*. It is never locked, like Size floor (FM29), so Copy settings carries it with `resize`.
  - **Pins.** No arm moves (all 2.0.0). `identity.json`: two new keys, `cov/textures/c/relhalf.dds` (64 px diffuse) and `cov/textures/c/relhalf_n.dds` (128 px normal, `half`, floor 16, `slot1` → 32 px); no existing key changes. `profile_hashes.json`: the 25 sub-types under `cs-compact` and `vanilla-compact` for both targets (100 keys), re-pinned by hand (T28C-d).
  - **Tests.** `engine/tests/resize_relative.rs` (14: halves a full-size normal and writes it, keeps an already halved one, never enlarges, keeps the aspect, slot 4, no map and two maps in the slot fall back to own size, a lone compact normal halves 2048 → 1024 with the Info, own slot is self, `none` ignores the reference, `self` is byte-identical, `resize_reference` is not a setting, packed map sized from its sources, compact presets, slot words); `resize::a_self_jobs_dim_resized_is_left_alone`; `presets::compact_and_fast_preview_resolve_as_designed`; GUI `resize_field::{the_size_row_offers_relative_to_slots, the_engine_slot_table_matches_the_slot_cards}`, `advanced`. The lone-map compact tests (`golden::{compact_2048_normal_plans_1024, compact_1024x512_plans_512x256}`, `cli_convert::cs_compact_writes_bc1_colour_and_half_size_normals`) pass unchanged.
- **FM45 Final review fixes (main team, 2026-11-03; rulings FR-a to FR-e).**
  - **Removed keys, all at once (FR-a).** `migrate::removed_in` and `removed_set` list every hit, one per line in key order (an `allow` list gets one line per removed code; per key the first `REMOVED` row still wins, so `nvtt` names the value), at most 10 lines and then `and N more`; a single hit reads as before. `sdc` reports every bad `--set` and `--allow` in one error, and the GUI banner keeps the list one key per line. No auto-remove: some removed keys change output (`drop_top_mip`, `pot-down`), and nothing may change output silently. Tests `migrate::every_removed_key_is_listed_in_one_error` (20 `[files]` hits plus two other keys), `cli_basics::every_removed_set_and_allow_is_named_at_once`, GUI `project::every_removed_key_of_a_1x_project_is_named_at_once`.
  - **Skipped user presets (FR-b).** Every CFG-PRESET-SKIPPED file that is not an alias clash gets a Warning row "Preset file <name> was skipped: <reason>" with Open folder (the reason's first line; the hover has the file and all of it). When the project's missing preset is named after a skipped file (its stem, ASCII case folded), the Blocks row and Review's refusal read "Preset <id> was skipped: <reason>" instead of "is not installed"; Choose preset stays. `bottom::skipped_reason` strips the engine's `preset parse error: <file>: ` lead-in (a `shortcut:`, it parses the message). Test `presets_creator::a_skipped_user_preset_names_its_reason`.
  - **Cubes ignore `resize_relative_to` (FR-c).** `resize::plan_references` reads no slot for a `cubemap`, since `target_dims` leaves a cube to `cubemap.face_size`, so no RESIZE-NO-REFERENCE claims a resize that never happens. Test `cube::a_cube_ignores_resize_relative_to`. The GUI says "no effect on a cubemap" on a cube's Resize rows and "no effect while Resize is None" on Relative to and Size floor (`fields::no_effect_reason`), in place of the provenance; the rows stay unlocked (FM44).
  - **Pin record (FR-c).** `identity.json` key `cov/textures/c/cutout_bc3_t200.dds` was re-pinned by hand in ac5e4fff: its fixture changed from `rgba64()` to `rgba_near_cut()`, alpha near the cutoff, so threshold 200 changes its bytes (`t200_pins_the_cutoff`); no encoder byte changed. `encode_inputs.json` was rewritten in b9a8bb1c (all 61 keys): each key hashes its encode input, which lost the removed `validation.*` fields (FM16). Neither changes a 1.x output.
  - **GUI.** The preset editor has a Perceptual box for BC1, BC3 and BC7 with the texture panel's lock; the closed Relative to combo shows "Slot N", with the slot's name in the list and on hover (it held the Advanced drawer about 90 px past its edge); the 1-bit alpha box is named for AccessKit; the alpha dropdowns of Problems and Review are named "Problems alpha type: …" and "Review alpha type: …" (the texture panel keeps "Alpha type: …"); the FMT-BC1-ALPHA sentence offers 1-bit alpha only for see-through alpha.
  - **Schema.** `resize_floor` is `1..=16384` in the schemas, as the loader checks. `[defaults] packing.fill` still validates in the schema: the `[defaults]`, sub-type and `[files]` tables share one partial profile, so refusing it there needs a definition of its own. The loader refuses it (FM16).
  - **Docs.** CHANGELOG: the `--strict-cubemaps` and 2D cube-check lines go (a 2D `_e` file fails validation, as in 1.1.0), the power-of-two rule is the 95 % / 85 % one, the byte-change, vanilla perceptual and compact height lines give the final state, and the FM12 event change is listed. The Known issues page is a section of Troubleshooting. The broken `CHANGELOG.md#unreleased` anchor in `GUI-NIF-Edit.md` is the NIF team's (HANDOFF line, FR-d).

## Note FN — User-facing audit leads: generate sizes, fill boxes, read-only reasons, report, feature clash (main team, 2026-11-04)

Leads 32, 2, 17, 24, 10 and 27 of `F:\ai\dds\reports\debloat-2.0\main-audit-leads.md`, plus an owner ruling on the feature clash row. No encoded byte changes: `encode_versions` pins do not move.

- **FN2 A pack Fills box takes typed text once (lead 2).** `advanced::settled_drag` uses `DragValue::update_while_editing(false)` and settles on `drag_stopped || lost_focus || (changed && !dragged && !has_focus)`, and only when the value differs from what came in. Typing "200" is one edit and one undo step ("Fill value"), applied on Enter, Tab or a click away; Esc keeps the old value. Arrow keys on the focused box change the value at once (egui), and that value is held in egui memory while the box keeps focus, as a drag's is, so leaving the box writes it as one step. The box carries an AccessKit name ("AO fill" and so on). Tests `typing_into_a_fill_box_is_one_edit` (RED on the old rule: 255 was written on the first keystroke) and `an_arrow_key_on_a_fill_box_is_written_when_the_box_is_left` (fix round 1; RED before the hold: the arrowed value was lost).
- **FN3 A greyed set control gives the model's own reason (leads 17 and 24).** `AppModel::sets_read_only_reason()` is `None` exactly when `sets_editable()`, else "{READ_ONLY}: {error}", or `READ_ONLY` with no project doc. Every set control greyed for that reason shows it: the CM card, the XPMF card, the PGPatcher fields, the Sets column, slot commands, the JSON editor, the skin card, the set editor and `remove_missing`. Controls that write `sdc-project.toml` settings use `AppModel::toml_read_only_reason()`; the input name box uses that, then the set reason. The status banner is never a disabled reason (it can hold recovery and startup lines). The pass-through `settings_panel::toml_read_only_reason` wrapper is deleted. Tests `a_read_only_project_greys_the_card_with_its_reason_not_the_banner` (`cm_json.rs`) and `a_read_only_project_greys_the_card_with_its_reason` (`skin_card.rs`).
- **FN4 The JSON Lines report leaves out per-mip progress (lead 10).** `ExportModel::on_event_at` drops `EventKind::Progress` before it is stored in `events`, so the GUI's JSON Lines report is the engine stream without progress lines, and a long export no longer keeps one event per mip in memory. Test `progress_events_are_not_kept_for_the_report`.
- **FN5 A feature clash offers both features (owner, 2026-11-04; supersedes FC3's single P26 fix in part).** A PGPatcher "X and Y can't both be on" row carries `Fix::FeatureClash(set id, X, Y)` and draws two buttons, **Turn off X** and **Turn off Y** (the label was "Turn X off"), in the sentence's order, neither a default. Each asks for `Fix::TurnFeatureOff` on its own feature only, applied by `AppModel::turn_feature_off` as one undo step of its own ("Turn off X"). It does not use `set_json_value`'s per-set-and-key merge, so a click right after turning the same feature on by hand stays a separate step and one undo brings the feature back. FC3's rule that the row turns off the later feature in enum order no longer applies; the rest of FC3 stands. Test `a_feature_clash_offers_both_features_and_each_turns_off_only_its_own` (it turns both features on by hand first; with the merge, undo left the feature unset).
- **FN6 The Also project name needs no change (lead 27, checked).** `also_project` already calls `engine::also::project_name(&[root])`, so the GUI and CLI name the project the same way by construction (CV12). Nothing changed.

## Note FO — trace-debug fixes 2026-11-04 (main team, 2026-11-04)

Items are numbered by the trace-debug report's finding (`.superpowers/sdd/trace-debug-2026-11-04`); FO6 and FO7 are not here (findings 6 and 7 were delegated to the NIF team, owner, 2026-11-04).

- **FO1 A renamed set's input name moves its entry (finding 1).** Restates the digest rule (a rename or input name moves each renamed set's entry in the same undo step): `auto_retarget` takes the set's `renamed_from` as the old automatic id when the entry is a renamed, non-created one, so `SetsDoc::retarget_auto` finds it. Test `gui_tweaks::input_name_on_renamed_set_moves_its_entry`.
- **FO2 Export on a missing preset opens Review (finding 2).** `export_stopped` opens Review when `missing_preset_text()` is set, as it does for `export_blocks`, so the header and palette Export never write with the fallback preset (FC1). Review's own Export stays off through `review_gate`. Test `auto_accept::a_missing_preset_export_click_opens_review_and_writes_nothing`.
- **FO3 Number boxes show a stored value as it is (finding 3).** Ruling V6: `clamp_existing_to_range(false)` on the CS PBR `drag` (every Num and Glint row), the skin card, the complex-material card and the PGPatcher RGB boxes, so drawing never rewrites an out-of-range stored value (a template's `densityRandomization` 300 stays 300). A drag or a typed value is still clamped. shortcut: an AccessKit SetValue skips the range; clamp what is written if that matters. `advanced.rs`, `presets.rs`, `marker_dialog.rs` and `viewport3d.rs` edit values that are not stored project values and keep the default. Test `cs_pbr_ui::a_draw_keeps_an_out_of_range_template_value`.
- **FO4 A bulk or copied BC7 weighting pick completes every texture (finding 4).** FC8's follow-up is a list, one entry per picked texture; `follow_weighting` writes each one that still does not show the pick, joined to its own pick's step, and the writes share one re-plan. A bulk pick is two undo steps (the bulk, then the follow-up): the bulk's step has no merge key to join. Test `bc7_weighting::bulk_linear_overrides_every_hidden_inherited`.
- **FO5 A packed bake member is sized by the common canvas (finding 5).** `bake::plan::post_resize_dims` starts from `api::job_dims` (FM30's common size of a pack's sources) instead of the first source's own size, so the planned level shift equals the run's. Every pack source is sized by content (`load::image_size`, as Load reads it; a PNG named `.jpg` counts as its content; a cube DDS counts 6w, DS7). Same-size sets are unchanged: goldens and `encode_versions` pass without a diff. Test `bake_plan::packed_member_bake_shift_uses_the_common_canvas_size`.
- **FO8 The Auto wetness guess binds in the preview (finding 8).** GN1 says the Auto wetness map is meant to bind at t74; both GUI texture-map builders (`meshes::texture_sources`, `SetPreview::add_vfs`) now add `wet_candidates` beside the RFAOS ones through `meshes::skin_map_guesses`, as optional sources. render3d is unchanged. Test `nif_edit::the_auto_wet_guess_is_in_the_mesh_texture_map`.
- **FO12 The cube marker writer replaces any 152-byte 1x1 cube DDS (won't fix, owner, 2026-11-04).** Trace-debug minor 12: the user must target that exact path, and the file is easy to recreate.

## Note GQ — NIF trace-debug fixes (NIF team, 2026-11-04)
Fixes for the NIF-team items of the 2026-11-04 trace-debug report (owner delegated, 2026-11-04).
- **GQ1** A source change makes an exported edit pending again. When the source NIF's bytes change (`reload_source`, or a reopen on a changed source), `export.ops_hash` is set to an empty sentinel (not `None`, which would hide the PGPatcher warning), so the edit is pending and Review exports it again; the export records a real hash again. A reopen saves the document at once only for an exported edit; a never-exported one is saved with its next change. The exported file was built from the old source, so it would otherwise override the mod's update.
- **GQ2** The Shader tab's colour picker ends its undo step when its popup closes (or a drag in it is released), as the key-table swatch does. Before, only the R/G/B fields flushed, so picks from two popups merged into one undo step.
- **GQ3** `pick_source` also skips any folder that holds Tessalith's edit document for this NIF (`<root>\.sdc\nif-edits\<path>.sdc-nifedit.toml`), extending GD28 from `sdc NIF Edits` to every earlier export folder; a folder with a document for another NIF is still a source. Before, an old export folder under another name was picked as the source and the edit applied to its own earlier output.
- **GQ4** Meshes reads never land across a scan start or a reload, extending GC14, GC15 and GC19. `MeshesModel::store_settled` (no scan running and the store not unsettled) gates `select_nif`, the mod picker (greyed) and the texture-path click (`show_users`), so after a cancelled or failed scan they read nothing from the disabled store. `start()` drops a This-project pill read still out, and `reload()` drops a Controllers read still out, so neither lands an answer for the old store or file. A reload while the store is not settled keeps the selected NIF in `reselect` for the next completed scan, and an edit by path (`show_nif_tab`, a worklist fix) opens nothing and says to wait or scan again.
- **GQ5** A set member edit in the set preview no longer pushes the whole scene: the member re-encodes and its new bytes are swapped in (`sync_textures`), as a cap change already was. Only a member the edit newly blocks pushes the scene, so the game's copy of its texture comes back: `Encoder::sync` sets `scene_dirty` and `SetPreview::check` takes it in the same frame. Before, every member edit pushed the scene again.
- **GQ7** A skin-card pick (any `report_missing` request) reloads a path the texture cache holds as an absent Auto guess (the resolver found no file, and no request reported it missing): `reuse(entry, report_missing)` gates the cache hit in `Renderer::texture`. Before, the pick bound the cached nothing even after the file appeared, until the cache was cleared. An entry already listed as missing, one with a texture, or one whose file failed to decode (its error line is not repeated) stays cached.
- **GQ8** `esp::mato_data` takes the last EDID and the last DATA of a record, as the scan (`records::read`) does, so a name the scan stored finds its record (a good DATA followed by a short one gives None in both). The orbit pitch clamps to +-1.52 rad, below asin(0.999) where the view's up vector switches; the pan basis is unchanged.
- **GQ9** `decode_head_at` reads inside its block: the cursor is bounded to the declared block end, so a head that runs past its declared size reports `BlockSize` (block, kind, declared, bytes needed; a scan problem for that block, not "file ends early") instead of taking its shader and alpha refs from the next block. The header gives an implausible block count its own `NifError::TooManyBlocks` (modscan now stores such a file as a failed parse, not `not_skyrim`) and a type index past the type list `NifError::BadTypeIndex` naming the block; no modscan schema change, so an existing store keeps its old `not_skyrim` answer for an unchanged file (rows are reused while size and mtime match) until the store is rebuilt: a modscan schema bump, a corrupt scan.db or a deleted one.
- **GQ10** Gate fix for GQ9: the render3d `skipped_shape_messages_use_a_short_label` fixture had zero-size blocks that relied on the head reading past its block into the footer; each block now carries its own 116-byte head, so the test still skips 2000 shapes under the bounded read.

## Note FQ — backlog batch B2: engine planning (main team, 2026-11-04)
Fixes for trace-debug minors 17, 18, 9, 10, 11, 19 and 13 (owner delegated, 2026-11-04). No encoded byte changes.
- **FQ1 A failed or cancelled packing recipe ends every source (T17).** Only the source that carries the `file_failed` ended before; each other source of the recipe now gets a `DERIVE-NOT-CONVERTED` skip ("<file> is not converted: its <recipe> recipe failed"). `plan_packing` (a source failed to load or was cancelled) and the pass-2 loop (`api.rs`) only list the sources (`PackPlan::lost`); `end_lost_sources` runs once after pass 2 and skips any source a job packs, a failure or cancel ends, or a skip already ends, so each source ends once whatever the recipe order. The skip counts as intentional, so it does not make the batch partial beyond the failure that caused it, and it puts the source's `[files]` key among the planned files.
- **FQ2 A six-file cube's faces and output stem are planned `[files]` keys (T18).** The unmatched-key collector reads `j.cube`: every face file, and the folder of the first face plus the output's name (`textures/cubemaps/chrome_e`), so `CFG-FILE-UNMATCHED` no longer flags five faces of a cube.
- **FQ3 A built-in preset rule's `perceptual` is a default (T9; follows FM43).** At the FM43 drop of `perceptual` under `alpha.policy = data`, a value set by `Provenance::Rule` of a built-in preset (`vanilla-compact#0`) counts as not explicit, as the preset's own default does; `field_explicit` itself is unchanged. `vanilla-compact` with a user's `alpha.policy = data` on a colour map is Ready with `perceptual = false`, not FMT-PERCEPTUAL-DATA. A user rule or `--set perceptual` still wins and is reported.
- **FQ4 `subtype`, `target`, `category` and `resize_reference` in a defaults table are load errors (T10, T11).** `check_chain` refuses them in `[defaults]` and `[subtypes.X]` of every preset and config, naming the table and the key and saying why (it has no effect there; for `resize_reference`, that the planner sets it and a hand-written value skews the size checks). Before, the first three were overwritten by `finish_profile` and a hand-written `resize_reference` skewed the DIM-* checks. The planner's own in-memory `resize_reference` (FM44) is not a parsed table and is not refused. The built-in presets keep their top-level `target`. CHANGELOG has the line; Project-Config-Fields no longer says `subtype` is accepted there.
- **FQ5 An `[inputs]` alias is matched against the folder case-insensitively (T19).** `read_as_target` strips the folder the file was found in from the alias path component by component, ignoring case and keeping the remainder's case, so a project config named in another case than the input folder no longer gives IN-ALIAS-INVALID.
- **FQ6 A legacy cube DDS that lists only some faces is a partial cube (T13).** `read_dds` returns `DdsError::Unsupported("partial cube: …")` for a header without DX10 whose `caps2` lacks any of the six face bits, before the length check, instead of "truncated DDS file". A legacy cube with no face bits at all (`caps2 = 0x200`) is a partial cube too; it was read before, and is refused now.

## Note FS — backlog batch B3: GUI input and keys (main team, 2026-11-04)
- **FS1** The Advanced drawer's Mipmaps and Advanced texture fields rows gate on `AppModel::fix_off` (read-only reason first, else "Wait for the plan to finish"), as the texture settings panel's rows do (FK), so a pick during a pending plan is not dropped as "unchanged" against the stale view. (Backlog T32)
- **FS2** The Fills box (`settled_drag`) keeps a settled value on show while a re-plan is out, until the model hands the same value back or the plan is idle, as `cube_section::num` does; the held value is keyed to the box's set, so another set's box never shows it. Before, it showed the old value after Enter until the plan landed. (Backlog R2)
- **FS3** The header Undo and Redo go through `commit_click`: a focused text field commits first, so its step is the one undone and Redo is not cleared (FC8). (Backlog T20)
- **FS4** An Explorer file drop is ignored for the frame when a modal was open at its start: `model.dialog`, a pending sets-column prompt (Rename, Replace, Refused and the rest), the Slot commands dialog, or egui's top modal layer. Before, a drop over a slot raised a Replace? prompt or an error line under the modal, and a drop elsewhere replaced the open dialog. (Backlog T21)
- **FS5** The Preview key actions (`]`, `[`, 1-6, L, T...) are live only while the Selection is in front of the centre (`centre_front() == Selection`), as the Grid and Sets keys are (FB7); a docked Presets or JSON tab owns the keys. (Backlog T22)

## Note FP — backlog batch B1: gui model fixes (main team, 2026-11-04)

Items cite the trace-debug report's minor findings (`.superpowers/sdd/trace-debug-2026-11-04`) by number; each has a test.

- **FP1 The header says Review while a tick is on (T29).** `export_is_clean` is false while an Also tick is on, as `export_checks` already sent the click to Review (Note CN), so the button no longer reads `Export N files` and then opens Review. Test `also_export::a_tick_makes_the_header_read_review`.
- **FP2 Every export ends its rows (T25).** `export_ended` ends the Queued and Running rows of a plain export too (the `also_pass` guard and field are gone; `also_error` is None for a plain export). An export that dies after discovery leaves no row Queued. Test `export::a_failed_plain_export_leaves_no_row_queued`.
- **FP3 Cancel does not keep the automatic types marked written (T36).** `cancel_open` clears `auto_written` of the history it restores, because the folder's documents were re-read (FI4) and the next plan records the types again. Test `open_cancel::cancel_does_not_keep_the_automatic_types_marked_written`.
- **FP4 Undo steps recorded under a blame keep the stashed `sdc.toml` (T37).** `ProjectDoc::snapshot_text` is the stashed text while `blamed()`, else `text()`; `snapshot_of` uses it, so a step taken while the engine's rejection holds `sdc.toml` no longer stores an empty file for Undo and Redo to restore. Test `history::a_step_recorded_while_sdc_toml_is_blamed_keeps_its_text`.
- **FP5 Clearing the read-only flag refreshes Review's check (T31).** `refresh_read_only_flags` sets `review.toml_unwritable` again when the flags change, so Export is not left disabled with a reason that no longer holds. Test `also_export::clearing_the_read_only_flag_reopens_reviews_export`.
- **FP6 Orphan notes go with a written JSON (T23).** The "the PGPatcher JSON is now ..." notes about an older file are kept only when the export writes the JSON (`Written`, or an Overwrite that wrote); an export that writes none (vanilla with a borrowed slot, a refused file) says nothing about it. Test `pbr_export::no_orphan_note_when_the_export_writes_no_json`.
- **FP7 Save writes the edits a blame stashed (T38).** While the retry plan after a rejection is running (`tentative_edits()` is Some), `save()` writes the stashed text with `ProjectDoc::save_stashed` (same changed-on-disk check and atomic write), so Save in the Open, Close and Reload prompts keeps the user's edits. `sdc.toml` may then hold a config the engine rejects; it opens read-only with that message, as any rejected file does. Test `history::save_in_the_open_dialog_writes_the_stashed_sdc_toml_edits`.
- **FP8 Review's Blockers lists a missing preset and a read-only `sdc.toml` (T30).** Both refuse the gate, so both are blockers: a red row each in column 0 and in the count, the preset's row with `Choose preset…` (opens the header's Preset menu, as Problems' button does). Test `auto_accept::review_lists_a_missing_preset_as_a_blocker`.

## Note FU — backlog batch B6: GUI per-frame cost (main team, 2026-11-04)

Each item pins its cost with a counter or a call count, not only "tests stay green".

- **FU1 The JSON editor builds a set's context only for an open entry (T33).** `pgp_fields::context_of(model, set, view)` takes the view the editor already holds; `set_entry` builds it after the `!open` return, and reads `active_matches` and `json_rename` once per entry. A folded entry costs nothing per frame. Test `json_errors::only_an_open_entry_builds_its_context` (test-hooks counter `pgp_fields::context_builds`).
- **FU2 Review's Undecided row uses the Problems fix buttons (L64).** `undecided_row` draws the name link and `fix_buttons` on one line (the button texts are unchanged, including `Acknowledge for this texture`), then the sentences (alpha confirm line, acknowledgement line) on a wrapped line of their own when it has any, so `Set type: X ✓`, the picker, the Alpha type dropdown and Acknowledge are one widget set on both surfaces. The row's own sub-type combo, ✓ and temp state are gone. Test `problems_accept::review_undecided_row_offers_the_problems_accept`.
- **FU3 The bulk panel reads the re-encode split from the cached view (L14).** `BulkView.reencode` holds `reencode_split(keys)`, computed with the view. Test `bulk_ui::the_reencode_split_is_computed_with_the_bulk_view_not_per_frame`.
- **FU4 The header's VRAM line and the selected sets come from the cached column stats (L15).** `ColumnStats` carries `sets` and `vram`; `StatsKey` has a sizes revision that moves when header dims land, so the text follows with the selection unchanged. Test `out_size::the_header_vram_line_is_cached_and_follows_landing_dims`.
- **FU5 The input-name box scans the path list once per text (L16).** The ghost and the live rows are cached in egui memory by (the path list's `Arc`, the text); the cache holds the `Arc`, so a reused address cannot match. The match stays an ASCII case-insensitive prefix scan (the list's order is not sorted for it). Test `input_name_edit::a_focused_box_scans_the_paths_once_per_text`.

## Note FV — backlog batch B5: Also derive, RNM AO, PSD (main team, 2026-11-04)
Engine fixes for trace-debug minors 26, 27, 28 and 40 and backlog R1. `golden` and `encode_versions` are unchanged. No `encode_version` arm is set: each change reaches only inputs handled wrongly before, and the identity of those outputs changes by itself (FV2: the cavity source carries `invert`).
- **FV1 A PBR member that fails to load leaves its set out of the Also derive (T26).** `FailedFile::set` holds the key of the failed file's set, filled once the final sets are known (joins and `[sets]` edits applied). `derive_sets` drops the members of each set with a failed file and adds a SetNote "not derived: N map(s) failed in the PBR export", as an undecided set does (CN99). Before, the Vanilla and CM outputs were built from default metal, AO and roughness. Tests `also_export::derive_sets_leaves_out_a_set_with_a_failed_member` and `derive_sets_finds_a_renamed_set_with_a_failed_member`.
- **FV2 The derive honours `helper.invert` and `helper.read` on raw `_curves` and cavity maps (T27).** The derived set's `curves` and `cavity` sources take the member's `HelperReport` invert and read channel, as the pack loop applies them, so the derived job and its identity carry them. Test `also_export::derive_sets_applies_a_cavity_maps_helper_invert`.
- **FV4 A negative PSD layer count keeps merged transparency when the global layer mask length is missing (T28).** `merged_transparency` returns what it found instead of false. Test `psd::negative_count_survives_a_missing_global_mask_length`.

## Note FT — backlog batch B4: gui model 2 (main team, 2026-11-04)

- **FT1 An input rename moves the set's Also export opt-out (T34).** `set_input_name` calls `rename_skip_also` in the same edit as `retarget_auto`, as the set rename in `model/sets.rs` does, so `[export] skip` follows the new automatic id. Test `backlog_b4_input_name::an_input_rename_moves_the_skip_entry`.
- **FT2 The set's own input name is refused in any case (T35).** `input_name_plan` reads `\` as `/` in the chosen path (a backslash path no longer fails the prefix check) and returns "the set already has this name" when it equals the diffuse's game path ignoring case and `.dds`, so no case-only alias is written and no undo step is taken. Test `backlog_b4_input_name::the_sets_current_name_in_another_case_is_refused`.
- **FT3 A blocked item shows its current reason (T15).** When a re-plan leaves an item blocked for a different reason, `preview_after_plan` sets `preview.source` to the new `Failed(reason)` as well as the notice. Test `backlog_b4_preview::a_blocked_item_shows_its_new_reason`.
- **FT4 A skipped cube DDS previews as a cross (T16).** `dds_preview` keeps all six faces' levels from `first_level` (face-major, from `DdsFile::surfaces`) and sets `faces: 6`; `decode_faces` is shared with `decode_levels`. An array DDS (or an array of cubes) still shows its first slice, with the line `Array of N: only the first slice is shown` (`cube` for cubes) added to the preview notice through `PreviewOut::note`. A cut-off cube is an error, not a panic. Tests `backlog_b4_preview::a_cube_dds_previews_all_six_faces`, `a_cube_dds_preview_from_a_lower_mip_keeps_each_faces_own_levels`, `a_truncated_cube_dds_is_an_error`, `an_array_dds_says_which_slice_it_shows`.
- **FT5 No original-textures read while the scan store is unsettled (T43).** `ask_original_textures` returns at once while `scan_store_unsettled`, so a store a running or cancelled scan left empty caches no false "no mesh" (as `used_by_for` does). Test `pgp_lock::no_original_textures_read_starts_while_the_scan_store_is_unsettled`.

## Note FY — backlog batch B8: gui refactors and dead code (main team, 2026-11-04)

- **FY1 One commit-once-per-gesture widget (L29).** `advanced::held` holds the in-flight and the sent value in egui memory (pending, sent, busy: R2) and settles on a drag end, on leaving the box (FN2: typed text and arrow keys on a number box are one edit), or on a click. `settled_drag` (Advanced fill boxes, busy), the cube section's `num` and `held_value` (busy false) wrap it; `colour` and `settings_panel::commit_slider` stay. A number box defers an arrow key to leaving the box. `held_value` also settles on `lost_focus` now, which changes nothing a caller sees: its boxes took an arrow key at once and still do. Tests in `ui/advanced.rs` and `cube_section.rs` (a typed value and an arrow key commit once in both; a slider click commits once in `advanced.rs`).
- **FY2 The preview decodes each mip on its first read (L12).** `PreviewOut.decoded` is `Decoded`, one `OnceLock` per mip, filled by `PreviewOut::decoded_mip` for the readout, the heatmap job and the no-BC fallback upload; an encode with the heatmap off and no readout decodes none. A DDS input is lazy too, in its own pixel layout. Trade-off: where the upload takes the CPU path whatever the GPU says (a cube, an uncompressed layout, a base mip that is not a multiple of 4), `decode_if_cpu_path` decodes every mip on the worker before the hand-off, as before. A BC encode stays lazy, so on an adapter without BC support its fallback upload decodes on the UI thread, and a pixel readout's first read of a mip decodes that mip there; a UI read of a mip that a heatmap job is decoding waits for it (`OnceLock`; `shortcut:` note on the method). `decode_levels` and `decode_faces` (named in FT4) are gone: `decode_mip` decodes one mip of one face or of a cube's six (the cross, DT16). Test `preview_model::encoding_is_off_by_default_and_encode_now_encodes_once` counts decoded mips (test-hooks).
- **FY3 `PresetsModel::refresh` layers each branch once and reads the aliases once (L59).** The branch rules' overrides are read once per refresh too (`scope` per branch cloned the rules and re-keyed each). The #46 cross-preset check stays in `validate`.
- **FY4 One unset-default table (L62).** `fields::unset_default` serves the field views and the preset editor's `value_of`. `value_of` now also answers `mips.edge` (`wrap`) and `encoder.bc1_alpha` (false), which no preset row shows yet. Test `ui::presets::tests::value_of_shares_the_unset_defaults`.
- **FY5 One auto-flag entry point (L57).** `checks` passes `auto_flags::hint` to `resolved_for`, as the PGPatcher export and Parameters panel do; `auto_flags` and `resolved` (no scan evidence) exist only under `test-hooks`. A PBR grass set's checks now read the grass-only auto keys as its export does (test `auto_flags::a_pbr_grass_sets_checks_read_the_keys_its_export_writes`); the visible result of those readers (`height_neutral`) is the same, because a grass shader is `Half` either way.
- **FY6 The cube section's warning-text parser carries a `shortcut:` note (L76).** `Cx::errors` (CFG-INVALID); the upgrade is structured warning data, which needs an engine event change.
- **FY7 `SetsDoc` shares one table lookup; an equal output-name write is no edit (L55).** `table(&self, id)` serves `set_shader` and `set_output_name`; writing the name a set already has leaves the revision and the dirty flag alone. Test `sets_doc::tests::an_equal_output_name_write_is_no_edit`.

## Note FW — backlog batch B7: gui model 3 (main team, 2026-11-04)

- **FW1 Each JSON finisher gates on its own phase (T24; owner, 2026-11-04; supersedes the "no failures" wording of the digest's AT3 bullet in part, and the CN64 sentence "the PBR folder's JSON finishers still run" for CM and XPMF).** A cancel or an error holds every JSON back. After that a plain export needs `failed == 0`, as before. An Also export no longer reads the combined summary: the complex-material JSON needs `failed == 0` in the CM phase (the PBR phase when no CM phase ran) and the XPMF profiles in the PBR phase, so one failed Vanilla file writes them and a failed CM phase skips the complex-material JSON with "Complex material JSON not written: the CM phase failed". The PGPatcher JSON and the CS PBR JSON stay cancel-only. The note names the failed phase. The XPMF Vanilla-folder copies are not gated on the Vanilla phase: each skips a profile whose texture is missing. A plain (non-Also) export keeps `failed == 0` on its whole summary. `AppModel::also_phases` holds each phase's failed flag. Tests `cm_json::a_failed_vanilla_file_does_not_hold_back_the_cm_json`, `a_failed_cm_phase_skips_the_cm_json_and_names_cm`, and for XPMF `xpmf_export::a_failed_vanilla_file_still_writes_the_pbr_folders_profile` (a real Also export with a failing Vanilla folder) and `a_failed_pbr_phase_skips_the_profile_and_names_it` (a failed count and an engine error in the PBR phase).
- **FW2 One finisher for the CS PBR, complex material and XPMF JSON (L1).** `model/json_finish.rs`: `finish_guarded` runs the gate, writes, records the hashes and lists the files for the Also walk; each kind implements `Guarded` (its name, its `GATE` phase or `None` for cancel-only, its writer and its hash record). `finish_cs_pbr`, `finish_cm_json` and `finish_xpmf` stay as one-line entry points. No behaviour changed beyond FW1.
- **FW3 The CS PBR gate is pinned (L7).** Test `cs_pbr_export::the_cs_json_gate_is_cancel_only`: a cancelled summary writes no file and says "CS PBR JSON not written: the export did not finish"; failed files do not stop the write.
- **FW4 The view cache has one get-or-build (L9, L65).** `model::cached(m, get, put, build)` replaces seven copies (views, export blocks, set asks, problems, export files, delete hits, the XPMF card values); the build runs with no `RefCell` borrow held. `pbr_export::export_blocks` and the private `set_asks` return the stored `Arc<Vec<ExportIssue>>`; every caller, the UI included, reads that list without copying it. Test `pbr_export::the_cached_blocks_are_handed_out_without_a_copy`; the problem, export-file and view build counters are unchanged.
- **FW5 Tests wait for a counted drop, not a sleep (L54).** Under `test-hooks`, `AppModel::stale_replies_dropped(kind)` counts plan, source and encode replies `poll` dropped for an old generation or token, and `poll_until_stale_drop(kind, timeout)` polls until one more of that `StaleKind` (plan, source or encode) is dropped; each test waits for its own kind. `plan_progress` and `preview_model` use it instead of sleeping 500 ms; each fails (times out) when the drop path stops counting. `review::REVIEW_JSON_TTL` is the Review JSON cache's lifetime and `expire_review_json` ends it, so the review test no longer sleeps 1.1 s.
- **FW6 One file-name check (L8).** `pbr_export::file_name_problem` keeps its own empty and `..` rules and otherwise returns `pgp::prefix::check_file_name`'s reason, so the JSON name, the EDID, the XPMF file name and the output name refuse the same separators, trailing dots and spaces, forbidden characters and device names. Wording changed: a separator is "a name only, no folders" (was "must be a single file name, not a path"), and `check_file_name`'s device reason is "is a name Windows reserves for a device". Test `pbr_export::tests::both_file_name_checks_refuse_the_same_names`.
- **FW7 `SlotFill::item` and `is_filled` (L60).** They replace the hand matches over `Own`, `Borrowed` and `Empty` in `model/sets.rs`, `pbr_export.rs`, `xpmf_json.rs`, `auto_flags.rs`, `checks.rs` and `ui/set_editor.rs`; no behaviour changed. (`ui/pgp_fields.rs` and `used_by.rs` use them too.)
- **FW8 Looking one set up clones one view (L56).** `set_of_item`, `input_name_plan`, `original_source` and the rename retargets read the cached `views` and clone only the set they return. Test `model::tests::looking_up_one_set_deep_clones_no_whole_view_list` (the `SET_VIEWS_CLONES` probe stays at 0).
- **FW9 Dead and test-only model functions are gone (L58).** `known_slot_bits` (no callers) and `rename_plan` (tests only; `tests/rename.rs` calls `plan_rename` itself) are deleted.

## Note HA — README encoder charts: NVTT back as a reference, GPU-decoder scoring (main team, 2026-11-04)

- **HA1 The README charts and Research tables keep NVIDIA Texture Tools 3 as a reference encoder (owner, 2026-11-04).** Tessalith does not run NVTT (FM14), but it is the strongest outside encoder on most formats, so the comparison stays. The text says it is a reference. Supersedes in part: FM14 (its "NVTT 3 rows of the README charts" clause; old wording archived).
- **HA2 The formats chart scores what GPUs decode.** BC1 is shown on rgbcx's NVIDIA and AMD models, BC3 alpha, BC4 and BC5 decoded rounding to nearest. Values are bench round 3 (93 to 96 images per set), and the rgbcx column is Tessalith's patched build: BC1 Dual (FM17), the wider BC4 block search (FJ1, FJ2). The BC1 row comes from a rescore of Tessalith 2.0's BC1 encode against the round-3 files ([BC1 Dual rescore](../docs/research/encoder-benchmark.md#bc1-dual-rescore-2026-11-04)). BC6H keeps its 4-HDRI source.
- **HA3 Rows of one chart share their sample (BC6H aside, HA2).** bc7e `max` is charted at its round-1 Δ (+0.15 dB RGB, +0.04 dB premultiplied alpha) like the other BC7 rows, not the 150-texture tuning sample (+0.32); the README states its gain in place of the "separate sample" note. Colour follows the encoder in every chart (bc7e and rgbcx blue, ISPC orange, NVTT aqua, Compressonator violet, texconv magenta) in solid fills, and every bar has its value label; the stripe patterns are gone (owner, 2026-11-04). `tools/readme-charts.py` asserts that every full-width line fits the 780 px chart.
- **HA4 The formats chart plots each format against Tessalith's encoder (owner, 2026-11-04).** Each format is scored on its own image set, so absolute dB on one shared axis invited comparisons across formats that mean nothing. Each bar is an encoder's PSNR minus Tessalith's default for that format (rgbcx for BC1 to BC5, ISPC for BC6H), which is the 0 line; the absolute values stay in the Research table, one row per format.

## Note HB — CS Advanced Skin material files (main team, 2026-11-05)

- **HB1 An Advanced skin set writes a `.csskin.json` on every export (owner, 2026-11-05).** Community Shaders' Advanced Skin keys a material on the diffuse texture: `<diffuse>.csskin.json` beside the exported diffuse names its parameters and its `controls` (`_rfaos`), `wet` and `detail` textures. The file is `{format: "CommunityShaders.SkinMaterial", version: 1, parameters?, textures?, authoredFor}`. `parameters` holds only the keys the user set on the card, all from the 22-name contract and in range; `textures` holds lower-case `textures/...dds` paths with `/` separators, re-based from the output under the export root; `authoredFor` is the exported diffuse's size and FNV-1a 64 hash. It is written automatically like the PGPatcher JSON: no button and no opt-out (the disabled "Export JSON…" placeholder is gone). Before writing, the recorded hash is checked with a guarded write shared with the CM JSON: an edited file is kept and a note says so. A file no skin set writes any more is left in the export folder and listed as a note, never deleted. A value outside its range (`skin::skin_value_problem`, the one validator for the red block and the writer) blocks the export with a red Problems row; it is never clamped or written. Writer: `crates/gui/src/model/cs_skin_json.rs`. Closes the OPEN item of AG90.
- **HB2 Only the set that exports the diffuse writes the file; wet and detail slots can be borrowed (owner, 2026-11-05).** A set with no diffuse, a borrowed diffuse, or a host that is not an Advanced skin set writes no file, and gets an amber Problems row (NoDiffuse, BorrowedDiffuse, HostNotSkin). A texture slot borrowed from a host that does not produce the file is left out of `textures` with an amber row (HostMissing); an empty slot is left out silently, with no row. A path is never written for a file that does not exist. Share brings a `_wet` or `_detail_n` only into an Advanced skin set (final review M4): only those show cards 9 and 10, so another set could neither see nor clear the borrow. `_wet` and `_detail_n` are `SlotKind::SkinWet` = 9 and `SkinDetail` = 10, past `LAST_GAME_SLOT` = 8, so the existing locks, borrows and Share apply to them; game-facing writers use `game_slot()` and never see 9 or 10, and a borrowed slot's menu offers **Clear** (`MenuAction::ClearBorrow`).
- **HB3 A grey `_wet` is not written (owner, 2026-11-05).** Community Shaders removed the grey/height wet form (CS commit 176a0083), so `skin_wet` is BC7 only (a normal with a mask in alpha) and the `skin-wet-form` fact and the BC4 exception are gone. The engine moves a grey `_wet` to `plan.skipped` with the warning `SKIN-WET-GREY`, so the CLI and GUI agree and the rest of the batch still writes. `skin_rfaos`, `skin_wet` and `skin_detail` accept only the formats CS's texture loader reads (BC1, BC3, BC7, RGBA8; BC5 too for `skin_detail`); any other explicit format is `FMT-CS-SKIN-LOADER`, a Blocking code that `--allow` cannot downgrade. The grey wet's skip keeps `SkipKind::PackingInput` (controller ruling, final review M10): the code `SKIN-WET-GREY` names the reason, and a new `SkipKind` would churn the event schema.
- **HB4 `skin_detail`, suffix `_detail_n` (owner, 2026-11-05).** A new sub-type, recognised only in an Advanced skin set on `cs` (elsewhere `x_detail_n` groups as before, a normal of base `x_detail`). Its source becomes BC5 when every alpha value is 255 or it has no alpha, else BC7; an explicit format wins within the loader list. It is exempt from `FMT-BC5-NORMAL`, since BC5 is the intended form for CS's detail normal.
- **HB5 Old skin card keys are dropped (owner, 2026-11-05).** The card edits the 22 contract parameters in six groups (Specular, Physical, Edge & detail, Transmission, Fuzz, Wet). Keys outside them (`SkinMainRoughness`, `F0`, `UseSSS`, `sssWidth`, …) are not translated and are removed from a set's `skin` table on its next edit (`SetsDoc::retain_skin`); nothing reads them.
- **HB6 Version 2.1.0 (MINOR, DM8).** The workspace version is 2.1.0 and `RELEASED_ENGINE_VERSION` stays 2.0.0 until release. The `SkinWet` and `SkinDetail` arms of `encode_version` return `ENGINE_VERSION`: the first changes bytes and verdicts for a grey source and the loader formats, the second is new. The `SkinRfaos` arm stays 2.0.0 although explicit BC4/BC5/R8/RG8 on it are now `FMT-CS-SKIN-LOADER` (controller ruling, final review M7): that verdict only blocks explicit formats CS cannot load, and a blocked job writes nothing, so no output's identity changes. No other arm changes; the encode-version pins changed only for those two types.

## Note HC — 2.1 bug batch (main team, 2026-11-05)

- **HC1 A specular fills the flat alpha of a pre-packed `_rmaos`/`_rfaos` image (owner, 2026-11-05).** Before, the image won under `packing.prefer = prepacked` and the specular read "not used", even with a hand-set `rmaos.a` fill. Now, when the set's only other loose map is a specular (an `_ao_generated` file the image's empty B takes may join it) and the image's alpha is flat, the image joins the recipe as CO104 does: A from the specular, the other channels from the image. "Flat" is the alpha verdict `plan_fills` reads (`AlphaFacts::empty`), so any constant counts, not only 255. An alpha with content, or another loose map, keeps the M9 result. A pre-packed DDS is never joined (images only, as CO103). No `encode_version` arm moves: the recipe and its bytes for given sources are unchanged, and the new source list re-encodes that set by itself. Tests `generated_files::a_specular_fills_the_flat_alpha_of_a_prepacked_rmaos` and its neighbours.
- **HC2 A row about a missing or unused map says which (owner, 2026-11-05; amends FB8).** "Add the missing map" never named the map, and the name was only on the hover. `PACK-DEFAULTED`, `PACK-CONFLICT`, `PACK-UNUSED-INPUT`, `SHADER-EXTRA-MAP` and `SHADER-MISSING-MAP` show the engine's text, without its code: "x_rmaos.dds A: no specular source; neutral default 0.04". Every other code keeps its action sentence, and the table keeps these five for lines with no engine text (`code_line`, banners). Test `code_text::a_row_about_a_missing_map_names_the_map`.
- **HC3 The input name box shows the name without the diffuse's suffix (owner's screenshots, 2026-11-05).** Every member keeps its suffix under any input name (`member_tail`), but the box showed the diffuse's whole path. So `body_albedo` showed whole, typing `body` while the file was `body_albedo` asked for the same name and snapped back, and typing `body_albedo-` wrote `body_albedo-_albedo`. Now the box shows `body`, a typed name loses one copy of the diffuse's suffix, and the amber "no known texture" also checks the name with the suffix. A picked path that holds a different suffix (`rock_d` on an `_albedo` diffuse) still gains the diffuse's: not reported, left as it is. Test `backlog_b4_input_name::the_box_shows_and_takes_the_name_without_the_diffuses_suffix`.
- **HC4 `Kinds ▾` hides kinds of Problems row (owner, 2026-11-05).** One missing specular filled the list. The menu ticks each kind (`Code`) with its row count; a hidden kind leaves the Problems list only, for this project, and `gui.toml` keeps it (`problems_hidden`, by project root). My calls: Warning and Info rows only, as a block or a question must stay in reach; the tab count, the ready counts and Review ignore it, as Review's safety rules read every warning (a hidden row is not acknowledged); no Clear button, since the rows come back on every plan. Tests `bottom_panel::a_hidden_kind_leaves_the_list_but_not_the_ready_count`, `a_question_is_no_kind_to_hide`.
- **HC5 A PBR pick without a PBR albedo is not called vanilla (owner's screenshot, 2026-11-05).** The shader pick reads any PBR map (a lone `_f`), while `SetView::pbr` and the JSON need a `pbr_albedo` diffuse, so the card said "PBR (auto)" over a "vanilla" pill. On Community Shaders such a set is a JSON set (HL5) and the pill says "PBR → JSON"; on another target it says "PBR, no JSON". Test `pbr_landscape::a_pbr_pick_without_a_pbr_albedo_is_not_called_vanilla`.
- **HC6 A texture only a TXST names is a known path (owner, 2026-11-05).** `known_paths::read` read NIF shapes only, so the input name box and the slot pickers never offered it, and it read amber. It now also reads `txst_slot`, for winning records whose master is loaded (as `txsts_matching` does), each in its TXST slot. Read only: `modscan` is unchanged. Test `pgp_lock::a_texture_only_in_a_winning_txst_is_known_in_its_slot`.
- **HC7 From this set and Clear drop a borrow (owner's screenshots, 2026-11-05).** Both only wrote `slotN`, so on a borrowed slot the thumbnail stayed and each pick only greyed one entry and lit the other. On a borrowed slot both are now on, and both remove the borrow and any `slotN` stored with it, as one undo step that plans again, because borrows gate the bake. My calls: the two do the same there, since the set has no file of its own in that slot; a `slot2 = ""` left by the old behaviour goes too, as it would blank the slot in a PGPatcher entry; **Path…** on a borrowed slot is unchanged and keeps the borrow; a slot that is not borrowed keeps FI11. Also confirmed for the owner: an Advanced skin `_rfaos` bakes from a borrowed `_n`, only the host's `_n` is written, and `.csskin.json` names no normal, so the game reads the normal the mesh names. A vanilla or DDS `_n` lent to a skin set does not bake and says so (`SET-MISSING-COMPANION`). Tests `slot_menu::clear_and_from_this_set_both_drop_a_borrow`, `cs_skin_json::a_borrowed_normal_bakes_the_skin_rfaos_and_exports_one_normal`, `bake_plan::a_skin_rfaos_bakes_from_a_borrowed_n_with_one_normal_planned`, `bake_plan::a_vanilla_n_lent_to_a_skin_rfaos_does_not_bake_and_says_so`.

## Note HD — Variance bake: mip 0 and any size pair (owner, 2026-11-05)

- **HD1 Nothing is added to mip 0 of any member (owner, 2026-11-05; amends R5's mip 0 at shift > 0).** Before, a member smaller than its `_n` (shift > 0) had its mip 0 widened by the `_n`'s lost detail; now mip 0 stays as authored, and mip 1 onward bake as before (`NormalVariance::add_at` returns 0 at level 0; `build_chain_with` makes no level-0 bake call). At shift <= 0 mip 0 was already untouched. The `Rmaos`, `SkinRfaos` and `ComplexMaterial` arms of `encode_version` move to 2.1.0, so each re-encodes once. The `_cnr` self-bake already starts at level 1. Test `bake::tests::nothing_is_added_to_mip_0_of_a_smaller_member`.
- **HD2 The variance bake accepts an `_n` of any size and shape (owner, 2026-11-05; amends R5, CL40's size rule and S-mips-7's refusal).** For `Rmaos`, `SkinRfaos` and `ComplexMaterial`, a pair whose post-resize sizes are powers of two of one aspect keeps the level shift (`BakeJob.shift`, `fit: None`; its identity is unchanged). Any other pair (another aspect, or a size that stays non-power-of-two) bakes from `area_resample(written mip 0, member, false)` with `BakeJob.fit = Some(member size)` and shift 0: the mean of the written vectors over each member texel, not renormalised, then the usual length pyramid. The member's own chain has a level for every mip, so mip `m` of the member reads the `_n` footprint of the same UV area. Equal sizes always pair. The bake reaches members that have mips (HD5): an NPOT `_n` over a member with mips bakes, while an NPOT member keeps its size only with `mips.levels = "none"` (`resize::pot_exempt`) and so has no mips. `SET-BAKE-RATIO` is never raised again; its code and GUI sentence stay so an `allow` list naming it still loads (semver, DM8). A shared roughness slot and an sRGB member still never bake (R7, R10). Admission adds `w * h * 23` bytes for the fit's resample and pyramid. Tests in `bake/mod.rs`, `bake_plan.rs`, `bake_run.rs` and `gloss_bake.rs`.
- **HD3 Nothing is dropped from the mip tail (agent ruling, 2026-11-05; the owner suggested dropping tails, and there is no spare tail to drop).** The DDS chain length is unchanged and every member mip below 0 has a pyramid level: both chains halve each axis (floored at 1) and reach 1x1 together, so there is no spare tail to drop.
- **HD4 No row or note says a bake was fitted (agent ruling, 2026-11-05).** `set_resolved.bake` shows the set baked, which is all the user needs. The fit assumes the `_n` and the member share UVs, as every borrowed `_n` already does.
- **HD5 A member with one mip level does not bake (agent ruling from HD1, 2026-11-05).** HD1 leaves mip 0 as authored, so a member whose chain is only mip 0 (`mips.levels = "none"`, which also exempts it from the power-of-two resize, or `levels = 1`) would gain nothing. The planner gives it no `BakeJob` (`bake::plan::attach`), it does not record the `_n` as a source or decode it, and it does not make `set_resolved.bake` true. Test `a_member_with_no_mips_does_not_bake`.

## Note HE — 2.1 leftovers (main team, 2026-11-06)

- **HE1 A slot holding an RFAOS says RFAOS (owner, 2026-11-06: "Skin tiles and rows saying RMAOS when the recipe is RFAOS: yes, fix").** The Rmaos slot's chip, empty-chip hover, `owns` line, slot-menu title (`Slot 6: RFAOS`), card title and Assign/context menu titles said RMAOS for a skin set. `SetView::rfaos` is true when the slot's item (own or borrowed) has an `Rfaos` pack or the `skin_rfaos` sub-type; with an empty slot it follows the shader (Advanced Skin). `SetView::slot_text` and `SetView::slot_label` give the name; the coat rule of `slot_label` is unchanged. My calls (agent ruling): the Matching header's slot name follows it too; `SlotKind::label` itself stays RMAOS, so the Kind filter chips and the refusal messages (not per set) keep it; the recipe picker already says "RFAOS (skin)". Tests `cs_skin_json::an_rfaos_slot_reads_rfaos_everywhere`, `a_borrowed_rfaos_reads_rfaos`.
- **HE2 A set line naming a deleted file clears itself (owner, 2026-11-06: a red "?" chip "was a deleted map, those should just get cleared, with an info in console").** At the end of a full plan (`AppModel::clear_deleted_lines`, called from `apply_plan`), each `add` or `remove` line whose `Missing::File` is gone from disk is dropped from `sdc-project.toml` and logged in Messages as Info (`{set}: {file} no longer exists, so it was cleared from the set`); one re-plan follows. Guards, so a drive or folder that is briefly away never wipes lines: the project file must be editable, the plan must list items, the root must exist, and the file must be absent from a folder that exists (an extension-less key counts as present while a sibling with that stem is there; a line whose folder is missing or unreadable is kept, see HE3). The re-plan is a plain `replan`, not a user edit: it keeps the encoding and type notes, and a queued export starts on the plan after the clear. A file that exists but no plan lists (not a texture this project reads) keeps its chip, Problems row and **Remove from set**, which is what DU1 and DU3 now test. The re-plan clears nothing more, so there is no loop. My call (agent ruling): the unparsable-file case needs no test of its own beyond the read-only guard, because a read-only document holds no entries. Tests in `set_cleanup.rs` (`a_deleted_add_line_is_cleared_with_an_info_line` and the five after it).
- **HE3 The clear is no undo step (agent ruling).** It writes through `SetsDoc::drop_entry` directly, not `edit_docs`: an undo that restored the line would clear it again at once. Undo and redo of the user's own steps are untouched; redoing a step whose text names the deleted file brings the line back and the next plan clears it again, with another Info line, and the redo stack is not lost. A line whose folder is missing is kept (agent ruling): a renamed or unplugged folder must not lose every line under it, with no undo; the chips stay and **Remove from set** works, and with the folder back the set reads as before. Borrows and `NoFilesLeft` keep their chips (agent ruling): a borrow names a set that may come back, and an entry with no files left holds settings the user may want, so neither is cleared by itself; **Remove from set** and Problems still remove them.
- **HE4 No missing-map warning for a map a mesh or TXST already supplies (owner, 2026-11-06: "use existing: yes, and no need to confirm it's actually used").** `SHADER-MISSING-MAP` is dropped from a set's view (`SetView::set_warnings`, so the card badge, the pill and Problems follow) when a live mesh's texture set, or a winning TXST, that names the set's diffuse has that map's slot filled. `KnownPaths::supplies` (diffuse path with `.dds`, lower case, to 0-based slot bits 1..=7) is read from the scan with the conditions of HC6 (the winner and `owner_ok` joins) and of `LIVE_LIGHTING_TEXSET`; `sets::map_slot` gives each required role's slot (`_n`, `_n/_msn`, `_n (model-space)` 2; `_g` 3; `_p` 4; `_rmaos`, `skin_rfaos`, `_m complex` 6; `_i` 7; the diffuse-like roles none). The scan is not asked whether the game uses the map (the owner's word). `AppModel::known_rev` is part of `ViewKey`, so a scan landing after the plan removes the warning with no new plan. Agent rulings: GUI only, the CLI has no scan and still warns; the lookup is the name the set writes (`mesh_key` of the diffuse's game path and its output stem, as Used by does), because the meshes name the output, not the name the file is read as: an `output_name` or an exporter-named diffuse (`rock_albedo.png` writing `rock.dds`) is found by the written name, and an input name (alias) is not asked (final review I2, agent ruling); it is an exact path, not a stem or folder match; `PACK-DEFAULTED` stays, because it is about the channels of a file this set writes. Tests `a_map_a_mesh_supplies_raises_no_missing_map`, `an_aliased_set_looks_up_the_written_name`, `a_renamed_output_looks_up_the_written_name`, `no_scan_warns_as_before`, `every_required_role_has_a_slot` in `sets_model.rs`; `read_lists_the_slots_a_mesh_or_txst_fills_beside_a_diffuse` in `pgp_lock.rs`.
- **HE5 Which rename moves the input name box (agent ruling; the owner's question was whether bulk rename "looks like only the set").** `Rename inputs…` (`RenameMode::Inputs`) moves the box, as it writes the `[inputs]` aliases the box shows. `Rename…` (the set) and `Rename outputs…` do not: they change what is written, not the name the files are read under. Pinned by `rename_inputs_moves_the_input_name_box` and `rename_outputs_and_rename_set_leave_the_input_name_alone` in `input_name_edit.rs`; no code change.
- **HE6 The helper panel's Fills rows (owner, 2026-11-06, on a screenshot: "this looks sloppy"; the layout and the naming are agent rulings).** The Fills block is one Grid row per line: `Fills` and the hint on the first line, one target per line with its ✕, then the Add combo with `Reset` (shown only when the list is hand-set). The ✕ and `Reset` sit in the same column as the Role row's `Reset`; the target name is as wide as the combos (`COMBO_W`), inset to the combo's text, and truncated with the full name on hover; the hint is truncated the same way. A target is named by the planned output that ends in its suffix (`bake_1001_rmaos R`, the name on the `Show …` button), else by the set's base name when no output landed (`helpers::target_label`); the Fills rows, their ✕ names, the Add options, the `Skipped …` line and the `fills to …` line all use it. Pinned by `fills_rows_line_up`, `the_hint_column_says_set_by_you_or_suggested_on_the_first_fills_line` and `a_packed_target_is_named_by_its_output` in `crates/gui/tests/helper_panel.rs`. At 1100 px the Advanced strip overlaps the centre's reported rect by 18 px (seen once the over-wide row no longer widens it); `panel_controls_inside_panel_at_1100` skips the strip and the overlap is left as it was.
- **HE7 Two raw maps of one name are not duplicates (owner's report, 2026-11-06, `mouth_ao.psd` + `mouth_ao.png` in one Advanced Skin set: "cannot use either, is stuck using fill even when one is removed from the set"; the ruling is an agent ruling).** A raw map and a skin `_f` write no file at their own output (final review M5, agent ruling): pack planning consumes them or reports `PACK-UNUSED-INPUT` "not converted". So `prepare` leaves such an image out of the `IN-DUPLICATE` comparison (AF7 already compares only the jobs that write): `x_ao.png` + `x_ao.tga` give no `IN-DUPLICATE`, the first by path feeds the channel (M13) and the other is `PACK-UNUSED-INPUT`, which the AO source picker can swap. The rule follows the writer, not the name: a file whose sub-type is requested, or whose `helper.role` is `none`, writes and is compared as before, so two of those are still `IN-DUPLICATE`; two files with no raw name (`wall.png` + `wall.tga`) stay duplicates; a `_f` outside an Advanced Skin set is a PBR map that writes. My calls (agent ruling): a `[[rules]]` entry that types a raw name is invisible to `prepare` (rules need the analysis), and the BV2 writer dedupe is its backstop, so two rule-typed twins never both write; a file removed from the set by `SetEdit::Remove` is a set of its own that packs the same default output, so the BV2 dedupe still flags that one file; a `SetEdit::NoSet` file writes nothing and is left out of the comparison (HF1). The fast re-plan runs the same rule (`api::waiting_images`) on the old and the new state over every set and returns `NeedsFull(DUPLICATE_CHANGE)` when a file moves in or out while another file shares its output, so a skin `_f` typed by hand, or a neighbour's sub-type that unmakes the skin set, is seen; on a cs PBR set `PBR_SUBTYPE` already forced a full plan for such edits. Tests in `crates/engine/tests/raw_twins.rs`, `a_sub_type_edit_on_a_twin_plans_in_full` in `fast_replan.rs`, `picker_can_switch_between_twins` in `helper_picker.rs`.
- **HE8 Final-review fixes (agent rulings).** (a) A twin that writes nothing is skipped in the rename dialog's shared-output check (`input_collisions`), so a set with raw twins takes an input name and `Rename inputs…`; test `a_set_with_raw_twins_takes_an_input_name_and_a_bulk_rename`. (b) The set preview's member chips name the slot with `SetView::slot_text`, so a skin set's chip says RFAOS (HE1); `a_skin_sets_rmaos_chip_reads_rfaos`. (c) A failing `supplies` query leaves `KnownPaths` otherwise whole: the supplies map is empty and only the HE4 warning stays (`a_supplies_query_error_keeps_the_picker_paths`). (d) `helpers::target_label` (HE6) names a target by the base name of any output when none ends in its suffix, and names the colour (`Alpha(Diffuse)`) target the same way, so a renamed set reads `steel` beside `steel_rmaos R`; the old "else the set's base name" holds only when the set has no output. (e) `Skeleton::shared_output` is true when two files fold to one output; the HE7 duplicate check in `patch` runs only then (it cannot change a verdict otherwise), so an edit on a project with no shared outputs no longer clones the sets and predicts them twice. Pinned by the existing HE7 tests; `a_recipe_edit_that_flips_skin_plans_in_full` pins that a recipe edit that unmakes a skin set plans in full. (f) Left, with the reason: HE4 for a kept DDS diffuse (no job) is not done. `slot_of` gives a slot only to an item with a job, so such a set has no own Diffuse slot, and building the lookup key needs a game path the item does not carry; it needs the slot rule changed, not a few lines.

## Note GR — Clip editing removed (owner, 2026-11-05)

The owner removed clip editing from NIF Edit; clip playback and clip key edits stay (owner, 2026-11-05).

- **GR1 The Controllers tab has no clip editing.** There is no New clip, Remove clip, clip rename, clip timing (cycle, frequency, start, stop) or Convert to clips. The Preview picker stays, inside an open "Clips (N · showing X)" section at the top of the tab, and so do the "In clip" key tables. Reverting an op drops only that op.
- **GR2 A saved edit document drops its clip ops on load.** `EditDoc::from_text` removes the five clip ops (AddSequence, RemoveSequence, RenameSequence, SetSequenceTiming, ConvertChainToClips) and counts them; opening the NIF puts "<path>: N clip edit(s) dropped: NIF Edit no longer edits clips." in Messages. An exported document with only clip ops opens empty and pending, and the file on disk changes at the next normal save. The document version stays 1.
- **GR3 The nif crate has no clip edit ops.** `Op` has no AddSequence, RemoveSequence, RenameSequence, SetSequenceTiming or ConvertChainToClips, and `ClipTrack`, `convert_check`, `clip_key` and `timing_problem` are gone. The clip handling the spec lists under "Stays" is kept: `SetKeys` with a clip, SplitAlpha on clip-driven controllers, `nif::clips`, playback and binding in `anim`, the empty-manager check on source files and compaction's clip layouts. `anim::CONVERT_NAME` ("SpecialIdle") is the clip that plays by itself under Free chains.
- **GR4 The user docs describe no clip editing.** GUI-NIF-Animation.md documents the open "Clips (N · showing X)" section at the top of the Controllers tab (Preview picker only; a file with no clips has no section), the "In clip" key tables and Split alpha on a clip-driven alpha (in GUI-NIF-Edit.md). The 3.0.0 CHANGELOG entry records the removal.
- **GR5 Rows that depended on a dropped clip op, and the retarget path (agent ruling, 2026-11-06).** A clip key edit that named a clip a dropped op made (an AddSequence or Convert) is not applied: its row turns red (Conflict or Refused), the bytes stay the source's, the export waits, and Revert clears it.
