The Messages line counts only the dropped ops, because the dependent rows cannot be counted without applying them; GUI-NIF-Edit.md ("Older edits") says such a row turns red and Revert clears it. An export-folder change reaches Messages through the same open path when an untouched session reopens from the new folder; a touched session loads nothing into its own document (the new folder's document is kept aside whole at the next save), so it has no line to push.

## Note HK — Guards from the 2.1 retro (owner's "fix 1 - 7", 2026-11-06; wording is agent rulings)

- **HK1 `txst_slot.slot` is the raw TXST index.** A schema comment says so and `modscan::plugin_store::TX_TO_NIF` is now public, because `model/known_paths.rs` read the raw index as a lighting slot. A comment doesn't change `SCHEMA_VERSION`, so no scan is lost. Changed in a NIF team crate with the owner's OK.
- **HK2 `.githooks/pre-commit`** checks fmt when `.rs` files are staged, control bytes in added lines (vendored objects and test fixtures excepted), and duplicate note headings in `DECISIONS.md` and `DECISIONS-ARCHIVE.md`. CI already runs fmt; the hook catches it before the commit. It's off until a clone sets `core.hooksPath`.
- **HK3 `CODING_STANDARDS.md` holds judgement rules for reviewers;** anything a machine can check goes to a check, not there. Every review dispatch names it.
- **HK4 `tools/scan-query.py` opens the scan database with `mode=ro`.** Its TXST listing prints the mapping key from Note HG's spec: defining plugin plus `0x` FormID from `rec.local`.

## Note GS — Guards from the v3 chunk A retro (owner's "implement all", 2026-11-06; wording is agent rulings)

- **GS1 `tools/gate.ps1` is the one full gate.** CLAUDE.md points to it instead of listing commands. It runs the doc checks too, stops before the tests when fmt or clippy fails, and refuses to start below 20 GB free. The full gate runs after the final review's fixes, because a fix wave invalidates an earlier run.
- **GS2 CI runs `tools/doc-dupes.py` and `tools/doc-markers.ps1`.** Both ran only by hand before a push, so a failure could sit unseen. `doc-dupes.py` exits 2 when it reads no sentences, which is what a wrong working folder looks like; it used to report 0 failures and exit 0.
- **GS3 `cargo fmt --all --check` works in worktrees again.** The os error 206 workaround (fmt per package) didn't reproduce on 2026-11-06, including on a 51-character worktree path, so the gate, CI and the hook all use the same command. If 206 comes back it fails loudly, and the per-package loop is the fix.

## Note GT — Bundled Community Shaders source (owner, 2026-11-06)

The 3D preview uses a pinned CS tree embedded in the exe, with CS's default settings; it never looks for the user's CS (owner, 2026-11-06).

- **GT1 The pinned tree is CS 1.9.0 (AIO build 2026-10-03), from jiayev's development fork, 57 files (481 KiB) in `crates/render3d/shaders/cs/` plus `SettingsDefault.json` (owner, 2026-11-06).** It holds only the include closure of `Lighting.hlsl`, `DeferredCompositeCS.hlsl` and `DynamicCubemaps/SpecularIrradianceCS.hlsl`, copied byte for byte by `tools/cs-bundle.py`, which exits non-zero on an unresolved include. `.gitattributes` marks it `-text`. Its licences are in `THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md` and `LICENSES/`, and CLAUDE.md names it as the one exception to "no game or mod assets in the repo". Updating the pin is a commit that also refreshes the goldens. `d2026.10.11.0`, in the capture folder name, is the version MO2 holds for that mod, not a CS build. The tree matches no public commit found in `community-shaders/skyrim-community-shaders`; the fork was not searched.
- **GT2 Shader text is read through `render3d::ShaderSource` (`Bundled`, the default, or `Folder`).** `build.rs` embeds `shaders/cs` as a table sorted by lowercased path; `bundled::read` folds ASCII case and `\` to `/`. `includes::expand` and `SourceSet` take the source and keep the includer-folder-then-root order, the depth cap of 64 and root-relative `#line` names, so a bundled expansion is byte-identical to the folder it came from. A release build always reads `Bundled`; `Folder` exists only under `test-hooks` (GT4).
- **GT3 `cs_settings::defaults()` is the bundled `SettingsDefault.json` with every feature loaded.** It parses the embedded file once, takes `Linear Lighting`, `gameGamma` and the other values from it, and counts the 48 `Shaders/Features/*.ini` stems in `FEATURE_NAMES` as loaded, so `preview_defines` gives `CS_HAIR, CS_SKIN, DYNAMIC_CUBEMAPS, ENABLE_LL, EXTENDED_MATERIALS, EXTENDED_TRANSLUCENCY, VANILLA_FRESNEL` and Linear Lighting is on. A test checks `FEATURE_NAMES` against the pinned `Features` folder when `SDC_CS_SHADERS` is set. The `coverage` drift report, `read_settings`, `SettingsPaths` and the change hash are gone (GT4).
- **GT4 The preview compiles the bundled tree with `cs_settings::defaults()` and never locates the user's CS (owner, 2026-11-06).** `render3d::locate`, `Fingerprint`, `Change`, `SettingsPaths`, `read_settings`, `Info.ini` and `feature_inis` handling, the change hash, `Renderer::set_shaders`/`shaders()`, the GUI locate flow ("Shader folder", the not-found state, the moved popup, the settings re-read on focus) and the `gui.toml` `render3d.shaders` key are gone, with the `walkdir` dependency; an old `[render3d] shaders` key is ignored on load and dropped on the next save. `ShaderSource::Folder` exists only under the `test-hooks` feature, through `Renderer::with_source` and `Viewport3d::use_shader_folder`. The preview no longer follows the user's `SettingsUser.json`, the MO2 overwrite copy, `Shaders/Features` inis, their enabled features, or Game Gamma: it draws CS's defaults, so a user who changed them sees the default look. This supersedes GQ6 (the Game Gamma define); `preview_defines` keeps the code path but defaults give 1.6, so no `CS_GAME_GAMMA` is added. All 48 features load by default, so the "Off in this preview" line is long: it is drawn on one row, cut with an ellipsis, with the full list on hover and in the accessible name.
- **GT5 Tests that only need the shader source read the bundled tree and no longer skip; tests that need owner data stay gated (agent ruling, 2026-11-06).** The `Lighting.hlsl` compile, expand, draw, deferred, debug-view, composite, prefilter and missing-fill checks in `crates/render3d/tests` use `ShaderSource::Bundled` with no `SDC_CS_SHADERS`; they add 7.6 s to `--skip render_` and run in the default parallel pass, so none is renamed `render_`. The shared cache was waived. Golden and replay tests (`golden`, `golden_material`, `replay_real_fixture_smoke`) compile the bundled tree too, since the captures are of the pinned build, but still need `SDC_GOLDEN` (and `SDC_ACHERUS` for the meshes); they passed on the pinned captures under the new default defines; `SDC_CS_SHADERS` stays only for the two folder comparisons (`bundled_expansion_equals_the_folder`, `feature_names_equal_the_pinned_features_folder`). The docs state the pin and the defaults once, in `docs/wiki/GUI-Set-Preview-3D.md`, with a CHANGELOG line for the removed folder flow.
- **GT6 Luma Framework's terms are not plain MIT (owner, 2026-11-06).** `Common/Color.hlsli` calls the code it adapted MIT, but Luma's licence adds that commercial use needs the authors' permission. `THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md` quotes the real terms and says compatibility with GPL-3.0-or-later is not confirmed with the author. The code stays in the pinned tree; the owner may ask the author.

## Note HG — Texture set records in the sets model (main team, 2026-11-06)

- **HG1 The scan's TXST slots are raw `TX0n` indexes; `known_paths::read` converts them (agent ruling, a bug fix).** `txst_slot.slot` holds the record's `TX0n` index, not the lighting slot. `Store::txst_textures` and `txsts_matching` convert it through `TX_TO_NIF`; `known_paths.rs` read it as a lighting slot, so a record's `_rmaos` (TX02) listed under slot 3, its `_sk` (TX03) under slot 4 as a height, and `supplies` (HE4) put the wrong bits on a diffuse. `tx_to_slot` (TX 0..=7 to slots 1, 2, 6, 3, 4, 5, 7, 8; else none) maps it in the GUI, and a row outside 0..=7 is skipped. `modscan` is unchanged, and `the_slots_match_txst_textures` pins the GUI table to `txst_textures`. This corrects HC6 and HE4.
- **HG2 A colourless set the user explicitly made Advanced skin is shared (agent ruling; created and renamed sets by the controller after the final review).** On `cs`, a set whose explicit shader (`set_shaders` or `set:x.shader=`) is Advanced Skin, with members, every one of them `_n`, `_sk`, `_wet` or `_detail_n` and at least one `_n` or `_sk`, has `TextureSet.shared_skin`: other skin sets use its maps. `pack::plan::set_is_advanced_skin` is true for it, so its members type `pbr_normal`, `skin_sk`, `skin_wet`, `skin_detail`; it has no diffuse and builds no `_rfaos`, and `SHADER-MISSING-MAP` and `SHADER-EXTRA-MAP` are not raised for it. Narrower than the retired shared-skin plan: there is no same-name rule, and a lone `_sk` or `_n` with no explicit shader stays as before (`normal`, `backlight_tint`, the `skin` pick). `group::mark_shared_skin` runs twice. (a) **Before set edits**, so the joins of HG3 see the flag, with `api::pre_edit_shaders`: a shader stored under a `SetEdit::Rename`'s `to` also names its `from`, unless `to` already exists (that Rename goes stale). A fast re-plan's rerun of the joins uses the same call, so it agrees with a full plan. A Rename's `to` is checked against the pre-edit sets, not in edit order, so a hand-edited project whose renames free and then take a name (`a` to `c`, then `b` to `a`) lends the shader to the wrong set and leaves its `_wet` typing undecided (`shortcut:` on `pre_edit_shaders`; walk the edits in order if the GUI ever writes such a sequence). (b) **After set edits**, on the post-edit sets and their `explicit_shaders`, it decides the flag: a created or renamed set is shared by the same rule, and a set given a colour map or any other kind of member, or emptied, is not; a set emptied by a hand move keeps its joined `_wet`. (c) **The member rule reads names only** (`group::shared_member_tails`: the members' stems and the suffix aliases, never the set's key or a plan's slots), so a rename never changes it. A `*_wet` or `*_detail_n` is a tail, hand-added or joined, unless its whole stem is another member's base, which makes it that base's colour map (`road_wet` beside `road_wet_n`); a `*_detail_n` whose base is another member's base is that base's normal (`x_detail_n` beside `x_detail_sk`). A lone `road_wet` or `x_detail_n` has no `_n` or `_sk`, so it stays a colour member or normal in a set of any name. A set whose members are all joined `_wet` and `_detail_n` (the set lists them) stays shared. A shared set lists its tails, so they type as skin maps: a hand move rebuilds the set's suffixes through `member_split`, which reads those tails only in a set already listing them. (d) `txst_map::record_shared_sets` (R7) asks the same rule of the set's input files (by their `[inputs]` alias), never the plan's Diffuse slot, so it gives the same answer in every plan and settles; it looks for the colour path's writer among the other sets, since the plan before R7 may write a hand-added `_wet` at the record's slot 1. (e) A file patch changes no member's name and no shader, so it cannot gain or lose the flag. A shader change is part of the request signature, so the fast re-plan answers GLOBAL_CHANGE and plans in full; the flag comparison in `skeleton.rs`'s `same` is a defensive check only, which no user path reaches. Off `cs` nothing is shared.
- **HG3 The `_wet` and `_detail_n` joins follow a shared set or the set's maps (controller ruling on the final review; the spec's "Engine: only R7").** `join_members` asks `shared_skin` (HG2), or else `set_is_advanced_skin` by the set's maps, as before this note. An explicit shader has no other say in the join: a set skin only by its chosen shader keeps its `_wet` in a set of its own (the limit in DV7 stands, with its `shortcut:` above the join), and an explicit shader other than Advanced Skin on a set with a real `_rfaos` still joins its `_wet`. Existing projects keep their outputs.
- **HG4 `shared` is derived in the GUI; a shared set shows only its maps (agent ruling).** `SetView.shared` is set by `finish_view` (`sets::is_shared`): the pick is Advanced Skin, the set has members, and every filled slot and every member is Normal, `_sk`, `_wet` or `_detail_n` (a member in no slot, such as a kept DDS with no job, is judged by its name through the engine's `member_split`). That follows the engine's member rule (`group::mark_shared_skin`); the plan's narrower test (no Diffuse, no Rmaos) called a set shared after a hand-added `_p` that the engine had already unmarked, and hid that map's row. The statics choice ("PGPatcher entry for statics") on a shared set is disabled with the reason on hover and as a line, and its status line is not drawn: a shared set has no diffuse, so it writes no PGPatcher entry; the rest of its CS PBR records section and the Advanced tab stay. `TextureSet.shared_skin` is not carried over `SetResolved`, which is a serialised report. A lone `_rfaos` is not shared and keeps its missing-diffuse row. A shared set's card pill reads "shared" (hover "shared skin maps (no JSON)"); `shared_cells` keeps only Normal, `_sk`, `_wet` and `_detail_n` cards in the set editor, the set card and the card height. It writes no `.csskin.json` and raises no skin skip row (`cs_skin_json::build_plan`), takes a `_wet` or `_detail_n` drop (`choice_allowed`), and the name-tail readers (`member_tail`, `renamed_output`, the Rename dialog) read `_wet` and `_detail_n` as tails for it as for a skin set. `input_name.rs` reads a set's diffuse first, so a shared set never reaches it; it takes the same argument for consistency.
- **HG5 A mapped set writes to its texture set record's paths (owner for R1, the stored block and the key; agent ruling for the rest).** `[sets.txst]` holds `plugin`, `formid`, `edid` and `slotN` strings (the record's 1-based lighting slots, as `Subtype::txst_slot` gives; `TxstMap` in `sets_doc.rs`), with `no_fill = ["slotN"]` beside it; both are reserved keys, so they never reach the PGPatcher JSON. (a) **R1, owner:** a file in a slot the record names writes to that path; the `.csskin.json` follows its diffuse because it is built from the diffuse's output. (b) **The key is plugin + formid (owner, 2026-11-06: "edid within a plugin itself is not a forced unique"); `edid` is a label.** (c) **Path rule:** `txst_map::record_rel` works from `item.key` and the root's `textures` component, mirroring `discover::game_path`: `record` lower-cased, `textures/...dds`, no `..`; a `textures` folder in the key gives the components before it plus `record`, else the root's last `textures` folder must prefix `record`, else the rel is `record`. A record that fails it is never written (agent ruling). (d) **R5, agent ruling:** a file with no record slot (`_wet`, `_detail_n`, a slot the record leaves empty) keeps its path, with no warning. (e) **R10, agent ruling:** record path, then `output_name`, then the default; input names are untouched. `engine_inputs` extends the record paths after `named` off `cs` and, on `cs`, inserts them last under the same `mirrored` and `seen` checks. (f) **R11, owner:** two sets on one record path give the existing duplicate row (`path_collisions` reads the record paths too). (g) A PBR set on `cs` ignores its mapping (agent ruling; its prefix names its outputs). Skin behaviour stays `cs`-only. No `encode_version` arm changes.
- **HG6 Record paths win per output key and collide on every target (agent rulings; overrides the brief's insertion order, from the Task 4 review).** (a) `engine_inputs` applies precedence per key first: a key with a record path drops its `output_name` and prefix paths, and only then do the `mirrored` and `seen` duplicate checks run, on final paths. The brief inserted the record last, so a name it had replaced still reserved its path and blocked another set's record there. A record path that loses the check leaves its file on its default path (its `output_name` too). (b) Record paths take those checks on every target, so a second set on one record path keeps its default path and its job and planning is stable; `path_collisions` reports record collisions on every target (off `cs` only record rows) and `export_blocks` stops the export. Off `cs` with no mapping nothing changes. (c) An `output_name` takes its folder and suffix from the file's default output as it is now (`sets::DefaultOutputs`, built once per pass): every file goes through the engine's `apply_read_as` with the whole `[inputs]` table, so an alias the engine refuses leaves the file where it is, then `canonicalise_outputs` applies the suffix aliases; for a packed output `pack::plan::virtual_input` on its first source. It never reads `job_key`, which a record mapping moves, so no mapping can reach it: removing or undoing a mapping restores `textures/a/steel.dds` and `textures/a/steel_rfaos.dds`, and an input renamed into another folder takes its name there. (d) `record_rel` normalises the record's spelling: lower-case, `/`, empty and `.` components dropped, `..` refused, and the final relative path is checked; `TxstMap.slots` keeps the text as stored. (e) Containment is by path components only; a junction the user made inside the export folder is followed, as for every other output (`shortcut:` in `record_rel`; upgrade when the engine's write boundary resolves real paths).
- **HG7 Empty record slots of a mapped set fill from the project (R2, R3, R8 owner; R6, R7, R9 and the rules below agent rulings; Task 5).** (a) **R2, owner:** an empty slot (n >= 2) of a mapped set whose record path another set writes is `SlotFill::Borrowed` from that set, amber, with nothing written to `sdc-project.toml`; the bake reads that normal through `borrowed_normal`, so one `_n` is written. `txst_map::fill_from_records` runs after `entry_view` and before `finish_view`, so an explicit `borrow`, a `slotN` setting, a pin and the set's own file already hold the slot and win. `SetView.filled` (slot to host id), `fill_off` and `record_supplied` carry the result. (b) **R3, owner ("no need to confirm it's actually used"):** a slot the record names that no set writes is supplied, and the `SHADER-MISSING-MAP` for it goes with no scan; this also covers a path `record_rel` refuses. (c) **The writer rule, agent ruling:** a set writes a path through its file's record path when its set maps that file's slot, else through the last plan's `job_key`, both ASCII-folded, the first writer in display order keeping a path; a set never fills from itself, and a PBR set on `cs` (whose mapping is ignored) counts by `job_key`. A slot fills only from a host file of the same slot number. (d) **R6, agent ruling:** slot 1 is never filled or counted supplied; the colour is the set's own. (e) **R7, agent ruling:** `record_shared_sets` names the sets that have a record, no `shader` and members that pass HG2's member rule, and whose slot 1 path another set, a skin one, writes (the writer's `v.skin`, from the last plan); `set_shaders` adds each as Advanced skin, a `shader` the user chose winning. That is read from the last plan, so it settles through the `EngineInputs` compare, as `borrowed_normal` does. (f) **R8, owner ("when existing at that path yes, but shouldn't override another set that supplies it's own"):** a skin set whose Normal was filled from a colourless host also takes that host's `_wet` and `_detail_n` when the host has them as its own; the set's own file, an explicit borrow, a pin and a turned-off fill win, and a host without them adds nothing. (g) **R9, agent ruling:** **Clear** and **From this set** on a filled slot (and `clear_slot_borrow` on 9 and 10) write `no_fill = ["slotN"]` and re-plan, never `slotN = ""`, which is a PGPatcher command that 9 and 10 do not have. `restore_fill` removes the key; the slot menu shows **From <host>** after Clear (the only command on 9 and 10) while the fill is off. Turning the normal's fill off also drops the wet fills that came with it. (h) `splice_views` rebuilds every view when any entry has a record, because a fill reads other sets' paths (`shortcut:` there; index writers by path if a project with many mapped sets shows it in a profile). (i) **Game paths, agent ruling (fix round 1):** the writer index and the consumer's record path are compared as game paths (`txst_map::game_key`: `\` to `/`, ASCII lower case, everything before the first `textures` folder cut; whole when there is none), so a host in another mod folder still fills a consumer; where a file is written (`record_rel`, `record_entries`) stays under its own mod folder. In a mapped project a landed plan that moves only an item's `job_key` counts as a change of its set (`full_plan_changes`), since a fill reads other sets' output paths.
- **HG8 The picker, the record read and the record-change rows (Task 6; the key is the owner's, the rest agent rulings).** (a) **Key, owner (2026-11-06):** "Key is plugin + EDID, not FormID. Should probably have formid somewhere in there, written as 0x (no header, first two digits, no leading 0's)... edid within a plugin itself is not a forced unique." The settled reading is plugin + FormID with the EDID as a label: `0x05000814` shows as `0x814` (`formid_text`). It is computed from `rec.local`, never `rec.formid`, whose load-order digits move (a light plugin's `FE4CD814` would keep `4CD`). (b) **The defining plugin, agent:** the plugin in a record's key is `masters_json[local >> 24]`, or the winning plugin itself when the index is at or past the master count (`clamp_local`), so an override finds the same record when the winner changes; `TxstRecord.winner` is shown beside it when they differ. The read is one query over `rec` / `rec_winner` / `plugin` / `txst_slot` with `owner_ok = 1`, ordered by EDID; a record with no path is not listed; `txst_slot.slot` is converted by `tx_to_slot` there (HG1). Like `supplies` (K1) it sits behind `unwrap_or_default`, so a failing record query empties only the picker. (c) **The picker, agent:** **Map to target…** (HL4) is greyed with "Scan your mods first" when the scan holds no record and no target mod is set, and with "PGPatcher PBR sets name their outputs in the JSON; use Rename outputs" for an albedo PBR set on Community Shaders with no target mod, the same set `record_entries` ignores (HG5); the scan reason comes first. The dialog writes nothing until **Map**; Enter maps the chosen row only while no widget has focus (HL4); a click outside does not close it (it holds the typed words). The list is a `show_rows` list of one fixed row height, filtered again only when the words or the scan change (`record_hits`, kept in `Pending::MapRecord`). **Change record…** opens on the set's current record when the scan has it. (d) **Record changes, agent:** a mapped set whose stored slots differ from `record_of` (plugin + FormID, ASCII-folded, the winner not compared) gets a Warning row "Record {EDID} changed" with **Use new paths**, which copies the record's paths and EDID in as one undo step. A record missing from a non-empty scan gets an Info row (no fix, no Review block), and the mapping and stored paths stay. An empty scan says nothing, so the mapping survives a scan that has not run. A PBR set on Community Shaders gets neither row: its mapping is ignored. (e) **A supplied slot shows where the shader has a row for it, agent:** the set editor writes "From record: {file}" in an empty slot row the record supplies. The Advanced skin editor has no `_s` row, so a supplied slot 8 shows nowhere there; its warning is still dropped. The card's "Record:" line is small, not weak, and its hover lists every slot path, marking each the set does not write itself with "(from the record)". (f) **One rule for what a member writes, agent:** `member_write` says what a member of a mapped set writes: the output key, the record's path and where it goes under the export folder. `record_entries` (the plan) and the picker's preview both call it, so a packed output with a job on a record slot is previewed, and a kept DDS with no job, a refused path, a packing source and a helper say "keeps its own path". Path collisions are not predicted in the preview. (g) **A new record starts with every fill on, agent:** `map_record` to a different key (plugin ASCII-folded + FormID) and `unmap_record` clear the set's `no_fill` in the same edit, so one undo restores it; the same key (**Use new paths**, **Change record** to its own) keeps it. (h) **Arrow keys, agent:** Up and Down in the picker move the highlight (no highlight to the first row on Down) and scroll it into view; they never map (Enter does, HL4). (i) **`record_ignored`, agent:** `AppModel::record_ignored(&SetView)` is the one test for "a PBR set on Community Shaders, whose mapping is ignored"; the plan, the preview, the menu and the Problems rows all use it.
- **HG9 Small record rules from the final review (agent rulings).** (a) A `no_fill` slot the record names, not otherwise filled, with no set writing its path: the slot's menu offers **Use the record's slot** (`fill_off` holds an empty host id for it) and `restore_fill` removes the key; the slot is then supplied. **From {set}** stays when a writer exists. A key for a slot the record doesn't name, or for 9 and 10 with no host, changes nothing and gets no command. (b) `[sets.txst]` `slotN` keys outside 1..=8 are dropped on parse. (c) A PBR set on Community Shaders shows its card's record line with " (ignored: PGPatcher PBR set)". (d) `set_txst(None)` removes a table that then holds nothing but its `id`; `unmap_record` clears `no_fill` first so the table can go. (e) `txst_map::formid_eq` compares FormIDs as hex numbers, `0x` optional and any case (`0x0814`, `0X814`, `0x814`); one that doesn't parse matches only its own text, ignoring case. `record_of` and `map_record`'s same-record test use it.
- **HG10 Known limits of R7 and the shared member rule, parked (agent rulings, final re-review).** R7 reads the plan it changes, so these cases don't settle or don't match the engine; each is safe because Export waits for a settled plan and refuses one that never settles ("the sets changed while planning"), and a doubled colour writer raises the duplicate row. (a) A set whose membership changes once R7 gives it Advanced Skin (`x_sk`, `x_wet_n` moved into `x`: the shader joins `x_wet`, whose stem is then another member's base, so R7 drops the shader and the next plan joins it back) re-plans until the bound and Export refuses. (b) A `*_detail_n` whose whole stem is another member's base (`x_detail_n` with `x_detail_n_sk`) is read as that base's normal, not as a colour, so such a host is shared. (c) R7 reads a member through its stored `[inputs]` alias even when the engine refused it (`IN-ALIAS-INVALID`), so it can give a colour set Advanced Skin; the alias warning and the duplicate row show it. (d) The GUI's `sets::is_shared` (HG4) reads slots and the engine's rule reads names, so a lone `x_detail_n` with explicit Advanced Skin shows "shared" in the GUI while the engine types it `normal`, and a set holding a member hand-typed `diffuse` under a shared name shows not shared. (e) A shared host emptied of its `_n` and `_sk` by hand moves keeps its joined `_wet` shared, as before this rule. Upgrade any of them if a project meets it: carry the engine's flag to the GUI (`SetResolved`), and give R7 the engine's accepted names and membership instead of the last plan's.
- **HG11 The owner's answers on the open record questions (owner, 2026-11-07).** (a) A record path many records share (`textures/black.dds`) raises no warning when mapped. The owner would like a "used by (N)" count beside a record path in the picker if it is cheap; it is not built yet. (b) `docs/wiki/GUI-Sets-Column.md` stays one page, over the ~15 KB budget. (c) Clear on a normal's fill also turns off the `_wet` and `_detail_n` that came with it (HG7g) stays. (d) A supplied `_s` on an Advanced Skin set shows only in the card's hover stays.

## Note GU — v3 cube sources and removed-key versions (owner, 2026-11-06)

- **GU1 Each removed key names its version.** Every `REMOVED` row in `engine::migrate` carries `since`, the version that removed it, and the message reads `<dotted path>: removed in <since>; <hint>`. Rows from 2.0 say `2.0` and their messages are unchanged.
- **GU2 A cube source is six faces, a cross or a strip.** A cube source is six faces, a cross or a strip (exact 4:3, 3:4, 6:1, 1:6 proportions), or a cube DDS; a square or 2:1 image typed as a cube is Unresolved with "not a cube source: {w}x{h} is not six faces, a cross or a strip. Change this file's sub-type.", and the GUI shows that sentence with the cube section's Layout combo disabled (the sentence on hover), no **Set layout** menu, and **Set type…** on its Problems and Review row. `cubemap.layout` = `equirect`, `mirror_ball`, `angular` or `photo` is refused in config, presets and `--set` with "removed in 3.0; convert it to six faces, a cross or a strip first".
- **GU3 Ground fill and horizon are gone.** A cube's lower half is the source's own pixels: the engine and the GUI no longer fill below a horizon. `cubemap.ground_fill` and `cubemap.horizon` are refused in config, presets and `--set` with "removed in 3.0", and a project that has neither key loads as before.
- **GU4 The 3.0 cube removals reverse the 2.0 debloat review's "kept on purpose: cubemap knobs" (item 14; not in the repo), owner 2026-11-07.** GU2 and GU3 supersede that pick: the panorama importer is gone, and the archive table lists each digest line and sub-bullet it cut. DS13, DT13 (the layout box never offers six face files) and FC12's digest line stay.

## Note GV — v3 generate from normal removed (owner, 2026-11-06)

- **GV1 The GUI makes no height, AO or curvature map.** The Generate dialog, its set-card, slot-row and Problems entries, the "generated" marker and hover, the stale-map row and Delete generated file are gone; a `_generated` file is an ordinary source row, and GEN-REPLACED, GEN-AO and Also export's read of `_curves_generated` are unchanged. A `[generated]` table in `sdc-project.toml` loads without a message and is saved as it was, and a `generated` key in `_sdc/generated.json` is ignored on load and dropped by the next write to that file; every hash field there stays. Undo no longer protects the `[generated]` table text, since it is no longer Tessalith's record.
- **GV2 The engine has no solver.** `engine::from_normal` (height, AO and curvature from a normal) and the `GEN-STALE` code are gone. An `allow` list that names `GEN-STALE` is refused with "removed in 3.0"; `_generated` files are still read as ordinary sources, and the Also export still reads `_curves_generated`.
- **GV3 The docs say what a `_generated` file still is.** `docs/wiki/GUI-Generate-From-Normal.md` and every link to it are gone; `Set-Shaders.md#generated-files` is the one home: a `_generated` file is an ordinary source that a real map beats (`GEN-REPLACED`), `_ao_generated` feeds `_rmaos` B (`GEN-AO`) and `_curves_generated` is read by Also export. Rulings wholly about the solver, the dialog or `GEN-STALE` moved to the archive; mixed ones were edited to this rule (archive table `2026-11-07-gv.tsv`).
- **GV4 The 3.0 generate removal reverses the 2.0 debloat review's "kept on purpose: AO/curvature" (item not in the repo), owner 2026-11-06.** GV1-GV3 supersede the frozen 1.x CO rulings where they describe the solver, the dialog, its offers, provenance or the stale flag, FC3's GEN-STALE row, FB13's AO box and DP1-DP3; `tools/decisions-archive/2026-11-07-gv.tsv` lists each cut. CO1-CO10, CO75, CO89, CO90, CO92, CO94, CO102-CO106, CO108, CO115 and CO116 stay, as edited in the digest.

## Note HM — UI-proof workflow: headless driver, frame checks, journeys (main team, 2026-11-07)

- **HM1 One app harness for the doc shots and the UI driver (crates/gui/tests/app_harness/mod.rs).** harness, settle, rect_of, the demo project, the CPU-first adapter choice and redirect_app_data moved out of doc_shots/scenarios.rs unchanged; tools\doc-shots.ps1 gave the same 45 results before and after (39 unchanged; main-window, problems-tab, set-card-menu, review-tab, presets-tab and helper-panel already differed from the committed shots). It is a directory module so it is not a test target of its own; each binary that includes it declares manifest at its root.
- **HM2 The UI driver is a harness = false test target (crates/gui/tests/ui_drive/main.rs).** Replay, not a daemon: every run starts from a fresh fixture; measured warm cost for the demo plus 2 steps: 2214 ms (start: fixture, app, first settle, 925 ms), under the design's 10 s line. With no --fixture it exits 0, so the workspace test run passes through it. Pointer steps aim at the visible centre (clip ∩ rect) and scroll a hidden target into view first. Drags use the house press, 8 moves, release algorithm (kittest has no widget drag). Not steps (gaps, named, not faked): drops from Explorer, IME text, touch; the 3D view's Copy image writes the real clipboard.
- **HM3 The driver opens no real window.** A fake PickerBackend answers file dialogs (a pick step queues a path inside the fixture folder; none queued = Cancel), and the test-hooks field SdcApp::shell_log records Explorer requests instead of spawning explorer.exe. Release builds have neither.
- **HM4 Model facts for the driver.** Each frame's state.json holds dialog, review_open, selection, selected_sets, expanded, dirty, counts, sets_filter, textures_filter, sets, items and problems, all read through public model functions (no AppModel accessor was added). An expect step names a fact by JSON Pointer into that file (`~1` for a `/` inside an id). A filter's predicate is Rust in oracles.rs (a script can't hold one) and runs by name through a check step; filter_matches reports an option that hides everything or changes nothing as a note. Journeys (`journey!` in crates/gui/tests/ui_proof.rs) replay a script without wgpu (plain_harness), so the gate's test run creates no GPU device outside render_ tests; the app-data redirect runs once per binary, and the binary's other tests (tiny UIs for steps and checks) never read the environment, so sharing the binary with journeys is safe.
- **HM5 Frame checks (crates/gui/tests/ui_drive/checks.rs).** Seven mechanical checks run on every frame and are written as `NN.checks.json` (run.json `findings` counts them per check, a check step's note is a `filter` finding with rule `oracle <name>`); each finding carries its rule text: `names` (egui-ui-rules 8.2), `clipped_text` (3.4), `off_window` (3.1/3.3), `overlap` (3.1), `alignment` and `lonely_wrap` (owner 2026-11-07 design, Why), `painted_only` (8.2 and design §2). Siblings are nodes with the same parent and the same size (within 1 px, at least 8x8), not the same role: the set card's empty chips are `Label` nodes and its filled ones `Button` nodes. `alignment` accepts a matching top-left or centre offset, so left-aligned names of different widths pass; a sibling with no painted text is flagged only when most siblings have some; one row whose tops differ by more than 1 px is flagged too. `painted_only` matches whole words, skips glyph-only text and checks each piece of a middle-elided text separately; the naming node has to touch the text's rect, not hold its centre, because the rect of rotated or right-aligned text is wider than the glyphs. `clipped_text` and `off_window` list only left and right cuts (rule 3.4 is about truncation; a row half scrolled out at the top or bottom is not a defect); the rest are counted in one `summary` finding per frame, with the number of Labels whose clip is unknown (egui's widget rect and the AccessKit rect disagree by 1 px or more, as in a scrolled ScrollArea; those are checked against the window only). `names` skips thin drag-only handles (8 px or thinner, no role) and scroll bars; `overlap` skips scroll bars. Clipping comes from egui's interact_rect and hover text is not probed (a hover would change the replay). On `demo` with an empty script (frame 00), 1055x770: alignment 7, names 16, overlap 7, summary 1; at the default 1600x900: alignment 8, names 20, overlap 10, painted_only 0, summary 1. The Sets-column finding names the empty slot chips G, CNR and S (centred letter) against the filled chips (bottom-left letter); the other findings are the Textures tiles (unnamed, 3 px vertical overlap, no letter on some).
- **HM6 The ui-proof workflow (.claude/workflows/ui-proof.js, tools/ui-drive.ps1).** Two modes with the owner's approval of goals.md between them. Mode run uses at most 8 agents (fixtures 1, explorers up to 5, visual 1, verify 1), because the wrapper's -Collect merges the frame checks in PowerShell and the verifier writes the report. The verifier returns the report as text and the caller saves it as REPORT.md, because the harness refuses report files written by subagents. Explorers read only NN.png and NN.tree.txt (state.json is the model, not the screen); their blindness is a prompt rule, since workflow agents get CLAUDE.md, which maps the repo but not the UI. The wrapper runs a copy of the built exe (.claude/scratch/ui_drive.exe), so explorers do not queue on cargo's lock and a build in another worktree cannot replace it mid-run. No CHANGELOG line or wiki page: nothing user-visible changed.
- **HM7 First run: the Sets column (2026-11-07).** The owner approved 7 goals; the fixtures commit adds 7 oracles and 7 journeys, in test code only. The report is `REPORT.md` under `.superpowers\ui-proof\sets-column`. A goal's check names the outcome, not one example of it: g1's check was pinned to `set:armor/gold`, so it scored "not reached" when the explorer opened `actors/body`. A journey fails on any note from a check step, so its check must decide (reorder the journey, never weaken the runner). run.json `size` is the window size at the start of the run (what `-Replay` and `-Collect` use), `final_size` the size at the end. `NN.tree.txt` prints up to 400 children per level. The app findings are a separate plan.

## Note HO — Tool scripts read and write UTF-8 on PowerShell 7 only (main team, 2026-11-07)

- **HO1 tools\publish-wiki.ps1, doc-shots.ps1 and package-release.ps1 start with `#Requires -Version 7`, and their text reads name UTF-8.** Windows PowerShell 5.1 read the BOM-less pages as cp1252 and published every “…”, “—” and “→” as `â€¦`-style mojibake (147 lines on 2026-11-07; the sources were clean). publish-wiki reads pages strictly as UTF-8 (invalid bytes stop the run) and refuses to write a page whose converted text holds a UTF-8-read-as-cp1252 sequence; `-SelfTest` covers the check.

## Note HN — Sets column polish: docked helpers and the first UI-proof findings (main team, 2026-11-07)

- **HN1 Helpers are docked on the chips they fill (Decisions 3-5; crates/gui/src/ui/sets_column.rs).** A helper is drawn as a 16 px badge (`BADGE`) centred on the top-right corner of each slot chip it fills, half outside the chip (HP1), on a filled chip and on a packed output chip (HN2), with a 1 px panel-colour outline and a 2 px stroke while selected; chips sit `DOCK_GAP_X` 16 px apart across and `DOCK_GAP_Y` 8 px down, `DOCK_INSET` 2 px in from the card edge, so the badge lies in the gap. The hit rect starts at 20 px (`BADGE_HIT`) and `clamp_hits` cuts it to the card and to the shared edge of any other badge, chip, tile or combo (never under 18 px at 1055x770 and 1400x790). A second helper takes the top-left corner, and K helpers that do not fit fold into one `+K` cell (hover lists them). A padlock (kept by PGPatcher, locked pin) keeps its corner and the badge uses the next free one. A badge is a Button named "<helper file>, fills <slot> <channel>" (HP2: `fill_text`, "RMAOS R"); hover shows the file and the helper panel's fills line; click selects the helper (opens the helper panel); right-click is the member menu. A chip with docked helpers is named "<letter>, <item>, filled by <helper files>"; a chip without helpers keeps its bare letter. Only a helper that got a badge leaves the trailing row; one folded into `+K` or with no free cell stays there as well. This amends CD8 ("Chips are named by their letter") for chips with helpers; CD8's plain `_m` thumbnail look is kept.
- **HN2 The empty-chip reading (Decision 2).** The "empty target chip" of the spec is a packed output with no source file of its own, such as RMAOS built from helpers only. The planned output is a member of the set, so that chip is an `Own` chip and takes its badges. A `SlotFill::Empty` chip (the slot has no file at all) draws only its letter, bottom-left and dimmed (HP9), and takes no badge.
- **HN3 The trailing row holds what no chip shows, and names each member (M1).** It holds a helper that fills nothing (dimmed by the disabled alpha, name and hover "unused: …", HP12), a helper whose target has no chip on the card, a `_m` env mask and a clashing second member of a kind. A helper reads "<file>, <fills text>" (the HP2 wording), any other member "<file>, <role>", also in its hover. Each tile also paints a short tag bottom-left (HP9): its slot letter when the slot is taken, "unused" for an unused helper, the first fill ("M G") for a helper whose fills land on no chip, otherwise the member's tail ("_m", or "—" when empty); a tag wider than the tile is cut with `…`. This replaces "Members in no chip follow as plain thumbnails" with a row that says why each member is there.
- **HN4 The width change reveals the open card (F1).** When the Sets column's width changes from a known width and exactly one set is selected, that set is revealed on that frame (the FM32 reveal). The set editor's room comes from Textures first, then Sets (HP6).
- **HN5 The hidden-set line wraps and Clear filter sits below it (F2).** "{id} is hidden by the Sets filter" wraps over as many rows as it needs and the **Clear filter** button is on the next row. The wording stays. This supersedes FC13 in part (FC13, which extends CD13, set the truncated line with the button on the right): the line is no longer truncated with the whole text on hover and the button is no longer on the right (the rest of FC13 and CD13 stands).
- **HN6 Card titles are elided in the middle (F3; the pattern of CD4).** The title is shortened to what the column's width holds, measured on the laid-out text: the whole id, then the middle elided (`header::elide_middle`: first segment and leaf), then `…/<leaf>`, then the end of the leaf. When the leaf does not fit beside the position and handle, the title takes its own line. While a search is on and its folded text lies inside one middle segment (not the first, not the leaf), the title tries `…/<that segment>/<rest>` right after the whole id (HP7). The full id is the label's AccessKit name and, when shortened, its only hover line.
- **HN7 Filter names, shader counts and the drop hint (F4, F5, F6).** The search box is named "Search sets"; the shader menu "Filter, Sets shader filter", and "Filter (1), Sets shader filter: <shader>" while one is chosen (the name holds the painted text, HP12). The popup lists "<shader> <n>" with the keyed sets that pass the filter as it would be after that click (HP4); a zero entry is weak but clickable, so every Mode shader stays listed (extends CD11, A9 kept). "Drop here to start a new set" is `weak_text_color()` and the selection accent while a drag is over it.
- **HN8 The `helpers` driver fixture and journeys d1-d4 (Decision 1; test code only).** `helpers` is the demo plus `props/crate` (a diffuse, a `_cnr`, and the helpers roughness (fills RMAOS R and CNR alpha), opacity and an unused metallic), so the RMAOS chip is empty and still gets a badge. It is a second fixture because changing `demo` would move every count the goals pin. d1-d4 and g10 replay it; oracle `one_set_open` decides a goal on the selection. Owner's answers (2026-11-07, "yes yes go"): goal g1 accepts any opened set, checked by `one_set_open`; goal g10 (find which file fills the roughness of props/crate) was added and has its journey.

## Note GW — v3 RNM removed; detail names (owner, 2026-11-06)

- **GW1 `_detail`, `_detailnorm` and `_detail_n` are one detail map on landscape and skin sets.** In a Landscape or PBR Landscape set the three names are `detail_normal`, in an Advanced skin set `skin_detail`, written under their own spelling (`x_detailnorm.png` writes `x_detailnorm.dds`) and never an RNM layer; `x_detail` and `x_detailnorm` join set `x` when it exists or the path is landscape and stay undecided elsewhere, while `x_detail_n` joins only a skin set, a landscape path or an explicit Landscape or PBR Landscape shader, so a `_detail_n` on a set that is Landscape only by a mesh pick, outside a landscape path, needs an explicit shader. Two of the names in one detail host (a skin set, a landscape path, an explicit Landscape or PBR Landscape shader, or a Landscape picked from a mesh, which `plan_sets` checks after the pick) are the blocking `IN-DUPLICATE`; in a plain set the files are undecided members. A saved sub-type wins, and this supersedes GI13 and GI22 in part.
- **GW2 The GUI has no RNM section, blended preview, normal-card outline or "Blends into" row.** A normal's texture settings panel and its card draw nothing for layer files, the set editor and Problems show no `RNM-*` notes, and a helper panel never offers a layer target; an old layer file is a plain file with no special row.
- **GW3 RNM is removed from the engine.** `engine::rnm`, `RnmSpec`, `Profile.rnm`, the four `RNM-*` codes (`RNM-NO-BASE`, `RNM-IGNORED-EYE`, `RNM-MASK-ORPHAN`, `RNM-LAYER-SHADOWED`; the 2.0 migrate row for `RNM-AO-MISSING` stays), `VocabRole::NormalLayer`, `HelperRole::Layer`, `FillTarget::Rnm` and the manifest `rnm` input are gone; a project that names an `rnm` key, an `RNM-*` code in `allow` or a layer `helper.role` is refused as removed in 3.0, and a file called `rnm` or `_bump` is just a file. No `encode_version` arm moved, because only normals that had layers change and they re-export anyway when their input list changes; a no-layer bake keeps its `identity_sha256`. The `iron_n` (cs and vanilla) and `iron_rmaos` keys in `encode_versions/encode_inputs.json` and the `iron_n` (cs and vanilla) and `iron_rmaos` keys in `encode_versions/identity.json` changed because the fixture's normal is no longer blended.

- **GW4 The 3.0 RNM removal reverses the 2.0 debloat review's pick that kept RNM (item not in the repo), owner 2026-11-06.** GW1-GW3 supersede the frozen 1.x RNM rulings W1 to W12 and W14 to W18 (W13, the `_norm` alias, stays), Y5, AL4, BB1-BB3, BQ9, GI22, AG115-AG117 and FV3, the RNM-outline halves of FC9 and FC13, and DY17's layer target; `tools/decisions-archive/2026-11-07-gw.tsv` lists each digest line and note item they cut, and the CO90 identity sentence. AL6, BC8, DY2, DY13a, X8, GI13, CO115, CO116, AV4, BB4, BB5 and DY10e stay, as edited in the digest; FM12, FY1, HE7, the FM44 reference and planning sub-bullets and the FV preamble are edited the same way. No live digest line carries RNM wording any more, the W line at 58 (plain normals) included.
- **GW5 The docs say what is left.** `Texture-Types.md#detail-names` is the one home for the three detail names and the set each becomes; `GUI-Helpers-and-Cubemaps.md` (renamed from `GUI-Helpers-RNM-and-Cubemaps.md`) and `Texture-Packing.md` lost their RNM sections, `Project-Config-Fields.md` and `Diagnostic-Codes.md` lost the `rnm.*` rows and `RNM-*` codes, and the CHANGELOG says layer files are no longer blended, so those normals change on the next export. The RNM panel screenshot, its `doc_shots` scenario and the workflow section tables' RNM paths are gone.

## Note GX — Preview scene depth at t17 (NIF team, 2026-11-07)

- **GX1 A depth pre-pass feeds t17 on frames with a scene-depth reader.** When a shape the frame draws has a pixel shader that reflects `SharedData::DepthTexture` at t17, `Renderer::render` first draws the opaque shapes (forward and DEFERRED programs) with depth write on into the depth alone, each with its own programs and depth state, so `discard` applies as in the Lighting draw. It then copies that depth into `Target::depth_copy` and binds its R32_FLOAT view at t17 for every t17 reader in the opaque and blended passes. A frame without a reader does neither. The main pass draws as on any frame: LESS_EQUAL passes the equal depth. This follows the game's Utility pre-pass before the Lighting draws (G3 in `G1-G8-results.md`). The one reader is CS hair without TRUE_PBR (`CS_HAIR_SHADING`, the self-shadow raymarch at `Hair.hlsli:289`): no other bundled program reads t17. The test `only_vanilla_hair_reads_the_scene_depth` reflects every Lighting.hlsl pixel program for every shader type, forward and DEFERRED, with the flag-driven defines, under the default features. Lighting.hlsl is the only shape shader the preview compiles (no Effect, Water, Sky, Grass or Utility programs); the backdrop and the composite read their own registers. `reads_depth` matches the two names exactly, because PhysicalSky's `TexVolLum` is t17 too.
- **GX2 Both targets' depth is R32_TYPELESS, with a D32_FLOAT DSV and a copy made on demand.** Both depths are R32_TYPELESS, as is the copy, so `CopyResource` stays in one format family. A target makes the copy and its R32_FLOAT view on its first pre-pass frame (`Target::ensure_depth_copy`) and drops them with itself, so a resized target starts without one and a frame with no reader allocates nothing. The game binds an R16_UNORM depth at the frame's size there (capture b9-tex frame50765 ev5718 and cs-1.9.0 frame14158 ev33358: 2176×1224, BufferDim the same). The preview keeps 32-bit float: both hold the same 0-at-near depth, and the raymarch reads only `.x`.
- **GX3 The pre-pass draws only the shapes the game's pre-pass draws.** It skips blended shapes, shapes whose pipe has z-write off, and DEFERRED programs on a forward target, which never draw (D-P12); a DEFERRED reader on a forward target does not count as a reader either. A pre-pass draw's PS reads the t17 default, which is harmless because it writes no colour. After the pre-pass, an opaque shape is tested against the final opaque depth rather than the depth so far, and the image is the same: the pre-pass writes depth in the same order with the same states, so each pixel's last depth writer passes again at equal depth and overwrites every earlier fragment, and later shapes see the depth they would see without a pre-pass. That holds for z-write-off and z-test-off shapes too. It rests on one precondition: a reader's discard and depth output do not read t17, because a reader sees the default there in the pre-pass and the copy in the main pass. The hair reader meets it. Lighting.hlsl's discards (1742, 3024, 3038, 3043) test the projected weight, the alpha mask and `alpha`, which comes from the colour texture's alpha, `MaterialData.z` and the vertex alpha (2986-3045). The hair branch changes only `baseColor.xyz` (1559-1568), `hairShadow` feeds only the light contexts (2360, 2427, 2559), and Lighting.hlsl writes no `SV_Depth`. In the mixed case of the `render` shortcut comment (a non-DEFERRED opaque draw on a deferred target), a hidden DEFERRED fragment fails the depth test on a pre-pass frame, so its RT4/RT5 do not reach the composite there.
- **GX4 The raymarch's other inputs match the projection.** `CameraData` is (far, near, far − near, far · near) from the View's own frustum, the near and far its projection's q = far / (far − near) uses (`View::new`), so `GetScreenDepth` returns view z for the 0-at-near, 1-at-far projection. `BufferDim` is the target size, `DynamicResolutionParams1/2` are 1 (as captured), and `CameraView`/`CameraProj` are the frame's own matrices (camera-relative, as `WorldPosition` is). The hit test `sampleDepth < rayDepth` therefore compares view z with view z. The depth buffer is cleared to 1.0 (far) and tested LESS_EQUAL.

## Note GY — Unknown suffixes wait for a type (NIF team, 2026-11-07)

- **GY1 A lone file with an unknown suffix waits for a type.** A file waits when it has no `[files]` sub-type and no rule gives one, its stem has no recognised suffix (§6.1, a preset alias or a vocabulary term) and contains an underscore, its set has no other member after joins and set edits, no scanned mesh or GRAS model uses it as a diffuse (GY6), and its path is not under `textures/cubemaps` and has no `landscape`, `sky` or `interface` component. `suggest` gives it no suggestion (`suggest::unknown_suffix`), so it is unresolved with `SUG-LOW-CONFIDENCE`, `Need::Subtype` and the reason `engine::policy::UNKNOWN_SUFFIX`, writes nothing, and `ResolvedJob::waits_for_type` is true. `iron.png` stays a colour map, and so does `iron_ore.png` beside `iron_ore_n.png`; a saved `[files]` sub-type counts as assigned. This supersedes U1, U3 and FC1 in part (a no-suffix file is the colour member at High only when it does not wait). Tests in `crates/engine/tests/unknown_suffix.rs` and `cli_convert::an_unknown_suffix_exits_1_and_names_the_fix`.
- **GY2 A set shader assigns no type.** Only a `[files]` sub-type or a rule's sub-type does: a lone `road_wet.png` in a set with an explicit Advanced Skin shader waits, gets no `skin_wet` suggestion (`_wet` is a suffix only for a member joined to an Advanced skin set) and its set is not shared. Tests `shared_skin::a_set_named_after_its_wet_file_waits_alone` and the lone case of `shared_skin::renaming_a_colour_set_keeps_its_colour`.
- **GY3 `--allow SUG-LOW-CONFIDENCE` skips a job with no suggestions.** It accepts a suggestion, so an undecided job with none, waiting or not (an `x_detail` in a plain set too), stays unresolved with `Need::Subtype` and writes nothing. Test `unknown_suffix::allow_does_not_convert_a_waiting_file`.
- **GY4 A kept DDS never waits.** A DDS that is not re-encoded is never planned, so the rule does not apply to it: in the shader's map check (`map_members`) a lone kept `rock_bump.dds` still fills the colour slot by its name, so its set gets the map warnings a lone `rock.dds` gets (SHADER-MISSING-MAP for the normal, no SHADER-EXTRA-MAP). The shader pick never reads it. Test `unknown_suffix::a_lone_kept_dds_keeps_its_map_check`.
- **GY5 In the GUI a waiting file is undecided and has no set card.** `TextureItem::waiting` is `ResolvedJob::waits_for_type`; the file has one Decide row in Problems and one Undecided row in Review, an amber tile, no `[files]` sub-type from the first open, and Export skips it as undecided. A waiting file's set has no card (nor set rows, "← set" link, set selection or a place in the status line's and the Sets pills' counts) unless the user created it or set something on it: its sets-doc entry holds more than its id, such as GY2's explicit shader. An entry left with only its id (the shader back to Auto, the last lock or borrow removed) or a `[project] order` listing is no choice, and shows no card, not even a missing one. Problems builds every set row from the views, so a hidden set has none. Assigning a type brings the card, Undo takes it away and Redo brings it back. Tests in `crates/gui/tests/unknown_suffix.rs`.
- **GY6 Mesh evidence says what a lone file is (controller ruling R2 on the final review).** The scan reaches the engine only as `BatchRequest::mesh_shaders`, `mesh_alpha` (both slot 0, keyed by `shader::colour_mesh_key`) and `gras_diffuses`, and before this item it typed a file only through the `member_subtype` insert over an existing suggestion, which a waiting file never has. Now `decide_subtype_with` counts a `mesh_alpha` key for the file (any slot-0 shape) or `gras_diffuse` as saying it is a diffuse, so it gets the ordinary colour suggestion (`SuggestContext::may_wait` false) and the GUI records it as any High pick; `suggest_grass` needs no earlier suggestion, so a GRAS diffuse that passes the grass tests is `complex_grass`. Only slot 0 counts: the engine gets no other slot's evidence, and `sdc` reads no scan (a saved project carries the recorded type). Tests `unknown_suffix::a_mesh_diffuse_does_not_wait`, `complex_grass::a_lone_underscored_gras_diffuse_does_not_wait` and the GUI's `unknown_suffix::a_mesh_that_uses_the_file_settles_it`.
- **GY7 An Error wins over waiting (controller ruling on the final review).** A waiting file with an Error-severity warning (an invalid `[files]` field) has status `Error`, so `waits_for_type` is false: it shows as an error with its set's card and writes nothing. Tests `unknown_suffix::an_error_wins_over_waiting` (engine) and `unknown_suffix::an_error_keeps_the_card_and_writes_nothing` (GUI).

## Note HP — Sets column, second UI-proof round (main team, 2026-11-07)

Source: the second UI-proof report `.superpowers/ui-proof/sets-column-hn/REPORT.md` (findings SC1-SC9, M1, M2) and the design spec `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-11-07-sets-column-hp-design.md`. Owner answers (2026-11-07): badge spacing option B; the card combo stays on its own row under the title; every count means what its click shows. Each item names what it amends; the replaced wording is in DECISIONS-ARCHIVE.md. Code: `crates/gui/src/ui/{columns,sets_column,mod}.rs`.

- **HP1 Badges straddle the chip corner (SC6).** A badge is drawn 16 px (`BADGE`) centred on its chip's top-right corner (position 0) or top-left corner (position 1). Its hit rect starts at 20 px (`BADGE_HIT`) and `clamp_hits` (a pure function: compute all rects, then split each overlap at its midline) cuts it to the card and to the shared edge of any other interactive rect, never under 18 px at 1055x770 and 1400x790. A selected badge has a 2 px stroke. Chips are `DOCK_GAP_X` 16 px apart across and `DOCK_GAP_Y` 8 px down, `DOCK_INSET` 2 px from the card edge, and `SETS_MIN` = 2 × `MEMBER` + `DOCK_GAP_X` + 2 × `DOCK_INSET` + `PILL_CHROME` + `SCROLL_ALLOWANCE` + `PANEL_CHROME` = 133 (was 121); a 260 px column still holds 5 chips per row (measured: five chips of 32 px and four gaps of 16 take 224 px of the 243 px card). The numbers follow from the badge: facing badges across 16 px overlap 4 px (18 wide each), a badge on a lower row reaches 2 px into the chip above (18 tall), at the card edge 1 px would leave the card (19 wide). **Amends HN1** (12 px, top-right inside), **FM32** Heights ("chip + item spacing" is now "chip + `DOCK_GAP_Y`") and FB2's "~120 px" Sets minimum (FB2 is in DECISIONS-1.x.md, which stays frozen; the digest line cites HP).
- **HP2 One wording for a fill (SC7).** `fill_text(t)` is the target's chip letter (the suffix in capitals; "D" for the colour map) + a space + `fill_channel(t)`: "RMAOS R", "RFAOS A", "CNR alpha", "D alpha", "M G" (3.0 has no layer fills, GW3). Badge name, badge hover, trailing-row name and the helper panel's Fills rows all use it. The output file is `target_file(h, t)` (the planned output's stem, "crate_rmaos"; the base name for the colour map); plain text joins them with two spaces ("RMAOS R  crate_rmaos"), and the helper panel paints the file in the weak colour. "fills to" is gone. **Amends HN1** (badge name) **and HN3** (trailing role).
- **HP3 The card combo keeps its own row under the title (SC1, SC9; owner answer 2).** It is right-aligned and sized to its value (`combo_w` returns the capped and the full width, measured in `TextStyle::Button` because the ComboBox lays out in Button), capped at the card's inner width less 48 px (`COMBO_FREE`; owner, 2026-11-08, HP12) and cut with `…` only past that; the full value is in the hover and the AccessKit value. At least 48 px on the left of that row is free card area and opens the set. The combo is named "Shader for <set id>". The titles keep their row. **Amends CD7** ("the shader combo at full width").
- **HP4 Every count in the column is what its click would show (SC5; owner answer 3).** A pill's count and a shader-menu entry's count are the keyed sets that pass the filter as it would be after that click (the search, the other pills, the shader), through one predicate. A zero pill is weak (greyed) only while it is off and stays clickable; a selected zero pill paints as a normal selected pill, because weak text on the selected fill risks 4.5:1 (HP8). A selected pill counts the sets shown with it on (the list itself), not what turning it off would show: a selected facet whose number jumps to the wider total reads as wrong (owner, 2026-11-08). **Amends CD12** ("Not ready plus Ready count every keyed set") **and HN7** ("keyed sets per shader").
- **HP5 A changed filter starts the list at its top (SC3).** On the frame `model.sets_filter` differs from last frame's copy (`DropState::filter`) the list scrolls to the top. A pending palette reveal (`nav.reveal_set`) wins over the reset; any other change of the list (a set added, a rescan) keeps FM32's anchor. **Amends FM32** Anchor for that case only.
- **HP6 The set editor's room comes from Textures first (SC2).** When the editor needs its floor (`set_editor::ROWS_MIN`), the shortfall comes from Textures down to its minimum, then from Sets. `TEXTURES_W` 380 and `SETS_W` 260 replace the literals in `ui/mod.rs`. Opening a set at 1055 narrows Sets only by what rounding Textures up to whole tile columns takes (HP12). In a narrow window opening a set moves the Sets column left once, by the room Textures gives up (owner accepted, 2026-11-08; 121 px at 1055x770 after HP12's rounding, 141 px before). **Amends FB2** (the split) **and HN4** ("FB2's narrowing is unchanged").
- **HP7 A cut title keeps the search match (SC4).** While a search is on and its folded text lies inside one middle segment (not the first, not the leaf), the title tries `…/<that segment>/<rest>` right after the whole id; a match in the first segment or the leaf, or across a `/`, uses HN6's order. **Amends HN6** while a search is on.
- **HP8 Readable and named (SC8, M1).** The status pill text uses `widgets.inactive.text_color()` (at least 4.5:1 on the card fill in both themes). The empty chip's dim letter (`dim_letter_colour`) is the same colour, not `weak_text_color()`: it measures 9.55:1 dark and 11.03:1 light on `extreme_bg_color`, so it reads at 4.5:1 or more in both themes at the cost of a smaller step from a filled chip's letter. The texture settings checkboxes are named by their row label (follows FM42, as "Mipmaps" is).
- **HP9 Labels sit in one place (M2).** An empty chip draws its letter bottom-left, dimmed (HP8), where a filled chip draws it, and its AccessKit name is "<letter>, empty" ("G, empty"). A trailing-row tile paints a tag in the same spot: its slot letter when the slot is taken, "unused" for a helper with no fills, the first fill (`fill_text`, "M G") for a helper whose fills land on no chip, otherwise the member's tail ("_m"), or "—" when the tail is empty. A tag wider than the tile is cut with `…` ("CNR alpha", "RMAOS R"); the tile's hover keeps the whole text. **Amends HN2 and HN3.**
- **HP10 Test support.** The `overlap` frame check spares a badge centred on its chip's corner: a pair is not a finding when the smaller rect is at most 24 px a side and its centre is within 1.5 px of a corner of the larger on each axis; a badge centred mid-edge is still flagged. `tests/ui_proof.rs` has `replay_with(fixture, before, lines)` and `replay_mid` (narrows the window between a set's open and the later lines, because narrowing before the click leaves the card unclickable for the driver); `sets_column_pins.rs` has `sets_width(h, w)`, which persists the Sets column's chosen width and settles 3 frames, for pins that need a narrow column (opening a set at 1055 narrows Sets only by the 20 px of HP12's tile rounding).
- **HP11 An off-screen card honours ScrollIntoView (SH1).** A card outside the clip rect answers an AccessKit ScrollIntoView request as if it had taken the focus, and its node lists the action (a screen reader or the test driver can bring it into view). No `Response` is added. **Amends FM32** Stand-ins.
- **HP12 Proof fixes (UI proof `sets-column-hp`, SH3, SH4, SH6; owner answers 2026-11-08).** (a) SH4 (owner): when an open set takes Textures' room, Textures' shrunk width rounds up to whole tile columns (`columns::round_up_to_tiles`, called from `ui/mod.rs`), the extra room coming from Sets and never taking Sets below `SETS_MIN`; when the next whole column is out of Sets' reach the width is left as it is. At 1055x770 with a set open Textures is 259 px and holds 2 tiles per row (it held 1 beside a blank strip at 239 px); Sets is 240 px (it was 260). HP1's "5 chips per row" holds only for a Sets column of about 259 px or more, so a card with five chips wraps 4 + 4 at 1055x770 with a set open (it was 5 + 3). **Amends HP6** (the split). (b) SH6 (owner): the card's shader combo is capped at the row's inner width less 48 px, not at half; "Environment Map (auto)" shows whole on a 260 px column, and the 48 px on the row's left stay free card area. **Amends HP3** (owner answer 2's "half"). (c) SH3 (controller): a card's one-row heading needs `NAME_MIN.min(<the title's whole width>)` for the name, so a whole title that fits keeps its status pill on the title row (props/crate at 1055x770: pill y 251 below the title's 229.5, now on its row). **Amends FI13** (80 px, or the name's whole width when that is less). (d) Names match the painted text (egui-ui-rules 8.2; controller): the Sets shader filter button is "Filter, Sets shader filter" ("Filter (1), Sets shader filter: <shader>" with one chosen), and the trailing-row helper tile's name and hover say "unused: <reason>", the word its tag paints. The Textures helper rows and the helper panel paint "not used: …", so they keep it. **Amends HN7** (the filter's name) **and HN3** (the tile's hover).

## Note GZ — MATO layer: PBR coat shapes and rotated statics (NIF team, 2026-11-08)

Source: the owner's "fix 1 and 2 on v3" on two of GG's OPEN items, settled from the pinned CS source, with no capture. Code: `crates/render3d/src/material.rs` (`projected_eligible`, `write_projected`, `write_material`); tests in `crates/render3d/tests/projected.rs`. GG's third OPEN item (replay of blended subjects) stays open.

- **GZ1 A PBR shape with SLSF2.24 gets the layer.** On a PBR shape SLSF2.24 is not multi-layer parallax: CS's TruePBR.cpp turns it into `PBRFlags::TwoLayer` (the coat) at load and clears the flag, and its PBR permutation list has TruePbr | VC | ProjectedUV with technique None. The coat reads `PBRParams2` and `MultiLayerParallaxData`; the layer writes `MaterialObjectRGBScale`, `ParallaxOccData` and `SparkleParams` and samples t3, t8, t10 and t11, so nothing collides. `projected_eligible` now skips SLSF2.24 only on a non-PBR shape (`m.is_pbr() || !SLSF2.24`); a PBR coat toggle stays a cbuffer edit. **Amends GG6** (DECISIONS-1.x.md, frozen; the digest line cites GZ1).
- **GZ2 `TextureProj` row 2 is the shape's model row 2.** Lighting.hlsl:254-255 computes `TexProj = mul(World3x3, TextureProj[2].xyz)` and the PS projects along `normalize(TexProj)`. With row 2 = the model's row 2 (nifly 3×4, the rows `World` gets; the camera shift changes only the translation), World3x3 · row 2 = s² · world up for any rotation and uniform scale, so a rotated static takes snow on its world-top faces. Rows 0 and 1 stay as captured (they feed TexCoord0.zw, which the triplanar path does not read). An identity model writes the captured matrix unchanged. `write_material` takes the layer with the model (`Option<(&ProjectedLayer, &Mat3x4)>`), from `ShapeDraw::model` on an edit and from the shape's world at build. **Amends GG2** (DECISIONS-1.x.md, frozen) where it fixed the whole captured matrix.

## Note IA — Model-space normal baker conversion (NIF team for the owner, 2026-11-08)

Source: the owner's MSN convention table and "add a small msn conversion feature … reusing the existing invert and channel swapping; no automated checks". Code: `engine::config::MsnSource`, `ChannelSpec::msn_source`, `engine::ops` (`Domain::Msn`); GUI `model::fields::MSN_SOURCE`, `ui::settings_panel::options_of`. Tests: `ops::tests::msn_source_reaches_the_skyrim_layout`, `crates/gui/tests/msn_source.rs`.

- **IA1 `channels.msn_source` turns a baker's model-space normal into Skyrim's layout.** Values: `skyrim` (default, no change, not serialised), `swap_gb` (Blender object-space bake), `invert_b` (Marmoset right-handed, or Substance, whose DirectX and OpenGL MSN are the same), `invert_gb` (Marmoset left-handed). Preprocess applies it right after the `msn` decode to [-1, 1]: a swap exchanges G and B, an invert negates the channel (the same as 1 − v on the stored value). Only `msn` reads it; every other sub-type ignores it, and no check guesses the baker (owner: none wanted). An unset field leaves every output byte as before, so no `encode_version` arm moves. It is a per-file key (`FIELD_PATHS`), settable in `[files]`, rules, presets and `--set`.
- **IA2 The GUI row is "Baked in", in the Type group, shown only for `msn`.** Options are named by the baker with the change in brackets: "Skyrim MSN (no change)", "Blender object-space (swap G and B)", "Marmoset right-handed or Substance (invert B)", "Marmoset left-handed (invert G and B)" (owner: one row for the two invert-B bakers). It writes the key like any field row (undo, ↺ reset).
- **IA3 A set's `_msn` keeps `msn` when its diffuse goes PBR or Advanced skin (owner: the re-type was a bug).** The PBR follow-on (`model::pbr_follow::pbr_counterpart`) switches only an inferred `normal` to `pbr_normal` and `glow` to `pbr_emissive`; a model-space normal is not a tangent-space one, and `channels.msn_source` (IA1) would no longer apply. Test: `pbr_members::a_msn_member_keeps_msn_when_its_diffuse_goes_pbr`.

## Note HL — Target mod and in-development guard rails (builder, 2026-11-07)

- **HL1** The target mod is the mod folder the modder is working on. It is read only and GUI-only: `[project] target_mod` in `sdc-project.toml` holds its absolute path (`sets_doc::target_mod`, `set_target_mod`; the CLI doesn't read it, and undo doesn't change it). `AppModel::set_target_mod` refuses a folder that is, sits inside or holds the export folder (`TARGET_IS_EXPORT`, compared on canonical paths without case), and a Blocks row of the same text stops the export when an export folder picked later, or a hand-edited toml, overlaps it (`pbr_export::build_blocks`). A worker (`target_mod::read`, on a target change, `open_folder` and a finished scan) lists the root-level plugins, the slot paths of each `meshes/**/*.nif` shape's lighting texture set, and the `textures/**/*.dds` files grouped by base and suffix slot (`suffix_slot`). Loose files only: a BSA-packed target lists records only. Its records are the scan's records whose defining plugin is one of its plugins (`target_records`; all records with no target mod, none while a set target is unread); a target absent from the scan has none and no Problems row. A read target with no Record row in the dialog says why above the list: `RECORDS_NOT_IN_SCAN` when it has plugins the scan lacks, `RECORDS_NO_PLUGIN` for a folder with no plugin; the dialog's rows are rebuilt when the stored path changes, not only when the read lands. Nothing is ever written into it. Rulings 1, 3 and 7 of the target-mod design.
- **HL2** `AppModel::used_by(path)` counts the target's mesh paths (one per distinct slot-1 path, as `read` groups them, judged by the slots of the first shape that names it) whose slots name the path, plus the target records whose slots name it (ASCII case ignored). A row's own mesh path or record counts toward its own number; it is the count the dialog shows beside a texture.
- **HL3** A mesh path or a file base of the target mod is an HG mapping with a `base` (`[sets.txst] base = "textures/…"`, no plugin, FormID or EDID; `TxstMap::is_base`). The stored slot paths are the source's own; a slot the source lacks goes to the base's folder with the file's default name on the base's stem (`renamed_output`, the same rule as `output_name`). The "Record … changed" and "isn't in the latest scan" rows skip it, and one set holds one mapping: picking another source replaces it, and the same base in another case is no new mapping. A base mapping on a PGPatcher PBR set (albedo) moves its match key, not its outputs.
- **HL4** The picker lives in the mapping dialog. **Map to target…** (**Change mapping…** once mapped, with **Remove mapping**) opens one dialog: the target mod row (read only, **Choose…** / **Clear**; a refused folder shows its text in red) and one virtual list with a kind column (`Record`, `Mesh`, `File`), the EDID or path and `used by (N)` (`used_by` of slot 1, counted for the rows drawn). With no target mod the list is the scan's records as in HG. The preview keeps `record_preview` for a record and lists the slot paths for a mesh path or file base. The menu item is greyed ("Scan your mods first") with no scan and no target mod, and for an albedo PGPatcher PBR set on Community Shaders with no target mod (HG8c's reason; nothing could be mapped). With a target mod that set keeps the item, and its record rows are the disabled ones (HG8c's reason as their hover). Enter maps the chosen row while no widget has focus (a typed Enter in the search box, or Enter on a focused button, maps nothing).
- **HL5** Every PBR set gets its PGPatcher JSON entry on Community Shaders, also with no PBR albedo. `SetView::json` = `pbr`, or a set whose shader pick is `Shader::Pbr` and that is no skin or shared set and has no `pbr_albedo` diffuse; `pbr` keeps its meaning (the albedo set, the prefix owner) and the JSON readers (`json_entries`, blocks, asks, `is_json_set`, auto flags) read `json`. `derived_match`, and `match_from` (`MatchFrom::{Albedo, Mapping, BasePath}`), come from the first that exists: the mapping's base, a record's slot 1 (not for an albedo set), the albedo or diffuse, the set id's own path. A no-albedo set keeps its meshes' textures: `slot1` is the mapping's slot 1 else `textures\<derived path>.dds` (PGPatcher skips a shape whose slot 1 would point at a missing `textures\pbr` copy), every filled slot names its output path, an empty slot 2 or 6 takes the mapping's stored path else `lock_normal` / `lock_rmaos` is written; an explicit `slotN` or lock still wins. A filled slot no path names (a kept DDS with no job) is locked too (`lock_empty_slots`), so PGPatcher keeps the mesh's own. The slot paths are game paths: an output path is under the export folder, so the JSON names it as it is (HQ6), and the entry is left out, with its "mesh keeps" row, when a typed `match1` matches nothing. A set with a PBR map and no filled slot has nothing to write, so `json` also needs at least one filled slot (`finish_view` in `model/sets.rs`; an all-DDS set gets no entry). On a non-CS target such a set stays "PBR, no JSON", badge "PBR set (no JSON on this target)"; on CS a PBR pick with no filled slot has the badge `BADGE_PBR_NOTHING_TO_WRITE`, since the target is not the reason. Tests `target_mod::a_no_albedo_set_without_a_mapping_keys_on_its_base_path`, `a_mapped_no_albedo_set_keys_on_the_mapping`, `pgpsim_patches_the_written_no_albedo_entry`, `the_json_slots_name_where_the_files_were_written`.
- **HL6** A missing link is an Info row and the set still exports; a conflict stays amber. Info rows (on the set and in Problems, `bottom::NO_ALBEDO_KEEPS`, `REPLACES_TARGET`): a PBR set with no albedo ("the mesh keeps textures\<path>.dds; a vanilla diffuse isn't sRGB, so it can look washed out", owner), a diffuse no mesh of the scan uses yet (`StaticsStatus::Unused` now writes the entry: `pgp_entry_wanted` is true; LOD meshes still do not count, no scan on a non-landscape set still drops it), no texture set of the scan using the texture (`NO_TXST_USES`, weak colour, not amber), and each output the target mod already ships (`target_replaces`). Stay Warning or amber: output-path collisions and a typed match key matching nothing (the Matching header, the `pitfalls` "left out" line, no entry in the JSON). Tests `target_mod::a_diffuse_no_mesh_uses_still_writes_the_entry`, `no_texture_set_uses_this_is_info`, `a_typed_match_that_matches_nothing_stays_amber`, `a_no_albedo_set_gets_the_info_row`, `a_mapping_onto_a_shipped_file_says_it_replaces_it`, `collisions_stay_amber`.
- **HL7** The owner confirmed (2026-11-08) that a PBR shape reusing a vanilla diffuse as slot 1 works with PGPatcher 2.3.1 and CS; it often looks washed out because the diffuse isn't sRGB, which the no-albedo info row says. No in-game proof was run (the plan's Task 7 was dropped as known behaviour).

## Note HF — Set groups, partial export and textures in no set (builder, 2026-11-08)

- **HF1 A texture can be in no set; any set can be deleted (owner's words in the set-groups spec; the code calls are agent rulings).** `[project] no_set` in `sdc-project.toml` lists `[files]` keys (`SetsDoc::no_set`, `set_no_set`). Remove member puts the file there in every case: automatic, moved in (its `add` line goes too) and the only member (supersedes the 1.x DU5 refusal; a packed output still refuses). Delete set works on every set: the files that are not packed outputs go to `no_set`, the entry and its order slot go, and the toast reads `Deleted set {id} ({n} textures now in no set)` (`1 texture` for one, no count for an empty entry), one undo step. Dropping a file on a card or on the empty area (`add_member`) and **Back to its automatic set** (item menu, `back_to_automatic_set`) take it out of `no_set`. An older `remove` line keeps meaning a one-file set that exports. A no-set file deleted from disk clears its entry with an Info line (`clear_deleted_lines`, as HE2). Tests `crates/gui/tests/no_set.rs`.
- **HF2 The engine side is one edit, `SetEdit::NoSet { file }`.** `engine_inputs` appends one per `no_set` entry after the other edits. `apply_set_edits` treats it as `Remove` (a one-file set), and `prepare` collects its paths (`sets::no_set_paths`) and leaves them out of the `counts` closure with `waiting_images`, so a no-set file blocks no same-named file with `IN-DUPLICATE` (`SetEdit::Remove` still does). A `no_set` change changes `set_edits`, so it is always a full plan. A key names every file the `[files]` rule names (the path, or the path without its last extension, case-insensitively: `sets::no_set_names`), and every hit is in no set; the GUI reads the same rule (`Plan::no_set` for the plan's jobs, `no_set_names` for the document), so a hand-written extension-less key behaves the same on both sides. The engine itself never writes a no-set file: `plan_sets` records the paths in `Plan::no_set`, and `Engine::run` drops their jobs first, whatever `write_only` says (`a_no_set_file_is_never_written`, with `write_only: None`). Since it never writes, `plan_sets` leaves its job out of the BV2 writer dedupe, which stays the backstop for every file that writes, so the twin is not `IN-DUPLICATE`. Test `crates/engine/tests/no_set_twin.rs`. The no-set drop is by set since HH4.
- **HF3 What counts a no-set texture (agent rulings).** `card_hidden` hides a set whose non-packed members are all no-set (so `views`, `has_card`, `carded_sets` agree), and a packed output of it stays out of the Textures column (only of such a set, `no_set_sets`: an excluded or out-of-scope set's packed outputs stay listed, as its raw maps do). `AppModel::in_run(set_key)` is false for such a set (and for one outside the scope or excluded, HF10, HF11), `item_in_run(item)` also asks `is_no_set`, and every counter or lister of undecided, warnings or written files asks it: `undecided`, `undecided_keys`, the Problems Decide, warning and check rows, `count_export_files`, the folder size, and the Textures column's Undecided and Warnings pills and filters. The *Not in a set* pill still counts a lone texture in a one-file set and also counts a no-set one.
