The GY1 guard lives in the GUI: `PlanView::build` sets `waiting` false for a `NoSet` input (the engine keeps it waiting; exempting it there would give it the colour suggestion at High, which the GUI records into `[files]`). The item stays `NeedsDecision` and is never asked. A no-set tile's border is never amber, and its row says `No set` in place of a type or a role. Everything that opens or reveals a texture's set goes through `set_of_item` / `set_key_of_item`, which answer `None` for it. The preview's "suggested settings" notice asks `item_in_run` too, so a no-set texture shows none. Two readers are left as they are, on purpose: the Problems export-file dedupe (`bottom.rs`, the live item beside a last-export row) only decides whether a row of the last export still stands, and `problems_inner` is a cache key; neither counts or lists a question.
- **HF4 Set groups live in `[[groups]]` and the views decide what shows (owner's spec; agent rulings for the code).** `[[groups]]` holds `name`, `sets` (set ids, as `[project] order` does; a set a hand edit lists again, in its own or a later group, is read in its first group only, `SetsDoc::groups`) and `excluded` (left out when false; `SetsDoc::groups`). Membership is kept there whatever the cards do: what a group shows reads `views`, so a set with no card (Note GY5's waiting lone file, or no set) is neither shown nor counted in it, membership never keeps a card, and a group whose members all lack a card has no header (`AppModel::groups`, `group_of`) while its entry stays and the header comes back with a card. A group goes with its last set only when that member leaves the membership (Delete set, Move to group or No group, Ungroup), not when its cards hide. Names are unique ignoring case; Rename refuses a taken one with the reason, and a group's own name in another case is its own. `move_to_group`, `rename_group`, `ungroup` and `set_group_excluded` return `Result` (the read-only reason, or the refusal), are one undo step and do not re-plan. Delete set takes the set out of its group in its own undo step, so undo brings back `no_set`, the entry and the membership together. `rename_set` and `retarget_auto` carry membership, also for an automatic set that is only in a group and has no entry, and for one whose new id already has an entry (the entries stay put; membership still follows). A collapsed group is a `gui.toml` key by project and group name (`group_collapsed`, same key as `match_slots`), stored only while collapsed, with no undo step.
- **HF5 The view order pulls a group behind its first member.** `build_views` does it right after the saved order (`pull_groups`), so `index`, Move up, Move down and Move to read what is on screen; the saved `order` is not rewritten by it, and a member with no card cannot place the block. `reorder_set` on a group's first member moves the whole block; any other set moves alone, and one that would land inside another group's block jumps it: stepping up from just below a group it lands before the block, stepping down from just above it lands after the block. Reordering never changes membership.
- **HF6 The Sets column draws groups as rows (agent rulings; the look is the owner's spec).** `draw` walks `Row::Header | Row::Card` built from `visible` and the stored groups (`build_rows`): a header comes before its first member that passes the filter, so a group with no passing member, or whose only member has no card, shows no header (its `[[groups]]` entry is untouched). The header reads the views: `n` or `n of m` (`m` counts the members that have a card), the worst dot of the members shown (blocks, then warning, then ready), an `Excluded` tag; its AccessKit name is `Group Armor, 7 sets, warning, collapsed`. A collapsed group draws no member card, and a filter leaves the fold as it is, also when it scrolls to the one open set. Members sit 12 px in (`GROUP_INDENT`). A header is one row of a fixed height (the body text height, at least `interact_size.y`, plus 6) and always drawn, so it needs no remembered height: the `top` sums in `jump` and `anchored` add it for each header above the target, and an off-screen card still answers ScrollIntoView. Its widget id is the group name, so a fold above never moves an open menu.
- **HF7 A reveal opens the group of what it reveals.** `nav.reveal_set` (Move to…, the palette) and a selection that moved since last frame (a click on a texture, Go to set; `DropState::selection`, the first frame only records it) open the group of the set they lead to, when a filter doesn't hide that set. A fold made by hand while the selection stays put stays folded, and Select all sets on a header is no reveal. Cost if wrong: arrow keys through the Textures column open each group they pass.
- **HF8 Group menus and dialogs.** A header's right-click menu holds Export group (HF10), Exclude from export / Include in export, Rename…, Ungroup and Select all sets (the sets that pass the filter). The set menu and the bulk menu get **Move to group** (each group, off when every set is in it; New group…; No group; one source, `group_entries`; `MenuAction::MoveToGroup`, `ToGroup`, `ToNewGroup`, `ToNoGroup`). New group… and Rename… open one name dialog whose OK shows the model's refusal (taken name, empty) and stays open; every other refusal is the `Refused` dialog. A read-only `sdc-project.toml` disables the edits with its reason. A set dropped on a header joins the group, and only while the ≡ handle is on (no filter), because the handle is what carries the set; a texture dropped there does nothing.
- **HF9 The engine writes only the listed sets: `BatchRequest.write_only` (spec; the details are agent rulings).** `Option<BTreeSet<String>>` of set keys (`sets::set_key`, so a key holds its output root). `None` (the CLI, and a GUI whole-project export with nothing excluded and an empty `no_set`) is today's run. `Engine::run` filters first, before any event or count: `jobs`, `skipped`, `failed` and `fill_ends` keep the listed sets only, so the summary, the events and `--json` cover the run (the spec named three lists; `failed` is filtered too, else a blocked set outside the run would count as a failure of it). The files cancelled while planning are filtered in `plan`, where their sets are still known. A skipped or failed entry with no set stays. Planning ignores it: it is destructured in `sig_without_files` and left out of the hash, so a scope change re-plans nothing. The manifest is append-only with compaction, so records of sets outside the run stay as they are; there is no prune (pinned, `manifest_records_outside_the_run_stay`). The Also phases plan under other roots, so `run_also` rebases the keys onto each phase's root (`rebase_write_only`), and the Vanilla copies skip jobs outside the list. A not-run file gets no Files row: the GUI drops the plan's discovered rows of textures outside the run (`ExportModel::outside_run`), and on a clean end a row still queued whose key, or an Also phase's `<folder>/<key>`, is outside the run is dropped rather than failed "not run"; a queued row of a texture in the run still ends "not run". Tests `crates/engine/tests/write_only.rs`.
- **HF10 Export set, Export n sets and Export group set a scope (owner's spec; agent rulings for the code).** `AppModel::export_sets(keys, label)` adds every set a slot of theirs borrows from, transitively, as a whole set (`with_hosts`: the borrowed file is then fresh), then runs `export_clicked`'s path: at once when clean, else through Review. `export_group` takes the group's shown sets, excluded or not (asking by name is explicit), and leaves the flag. `in_run` answers the scope, so every reader of it (Problems rows, Review's columns, the undecided count, blocks, asks, the file count, the per-set JSON) covers the scope only; project-wide blocks (path collisions, JSON validation, the JSON name, the target mod, a missing export folder) still block. The scope is in `ViewKey` (`scope_rev`), which keys every view cache, so no cache serves a scoped answer outside it. Review's heading and button carry the label (`Export group Armor`, `Export set <id>`, `Export 3 sets`). The scope ends when Review closes, when the export ends, when a plain Export is clicked, and whenever no Review is open, no export runs, none is queued and no dialog is open (`end_idle_scope`, called after `export_sets` and every frame from `poll`; a dialog may park a scoped export, so its answer comes first), so a queued scoped export that is dropped or cancelled takes its scope with it. A queued export resumes through `export_go` at both plan-landing sites, so the scope stays. Every cache whose builder reads `item_in_run` keys on `scope_rev` and the sets-document revision: the view key, the Textures column's pill counts and lists, and the folder size. A scoped Review has no **Also export as** column. **Export set** is off with "This set has no files to export" on a set with no key (all its files gone). While an export runs, Export set, Export group and Export n sets are off with "Wait for the export to finish", and `export_sets` does nothing: a scope set then would recount the running export's lists. Also export runs only unscoped (`also_runs`; owner: "Also export can be left to a whole project export"): a scope writes the PBR folder only, and a tick neither opens Review for it nor gates it. The *Not in a set* pill keeps its meaning (HF3): a set outside a scope is not "not in a set". Export set and Export group sit outside the editing lock, as Show in Explorer does: they edit nothing. Tests `crates/gui/tests/partial_export.rs`.
- **HF11 Exclude from export leaves a group's sets out of every whole-project export.** `in_run` is false for a set of an excluded group while no scope holds it, so a whole export sends `write_only` = every shown set's key that is in the run. A host in an excluded group is not pulled into a scope unless it was chosen: its file on disk serves the borrower. The Vanilla and CM phases leave them out through the rebased `write_only` (HF9), and `also_spec` adds their ids to `[export] skip` too (`also_run_config`): no export job reads the list then, since an excluded group always sets `write_only`, but `also_grass` does (an excluded PBR grass set gives the CM folder no grass stack), and so would a later reader. Their files are left exactly as they are (bytes and mtimes, pinned).
- **HF12 Files of sets outside the run (agent rulings).** Per-set files (CS PBR, complex material, XPMF, `.csskin.json`, the Vanilla borrowed-slot copies) are prepared for sets in the run only, and Review's JSON lines list only those. The PGPatcher JSON is one file, so it keeps every entry: on a run that leaves sets out (`untouched_root`), a dep that the run did not touch passes while its file is under the plan's output root, else the entry is left out with `not exported yet` (`pbr_export::dep_ok`, used by `finish` and `copy_files`); a dep the run touched needs its row done or up to date, as before. An edited PGPatcher JSON still asks. Orphan notes ("no set binds this EDID any more" and the per-set kinds) are not made on a scoped run or while a group is excluded (`reports_orphans`), because a set left out would read as gone; a no-set texture alone does not suppress them, and the PGPatcher JSON path-move note is unchanged.

## Note HQ — the export always has the textures folder (main team, 2026-11-08)

Owner (2026-11-08): "the export should have the textures folder always." Owner ruling on set ids (2026-11-08): "Move the export root".

- **HQ1 The output root moves into the export folder.** When the input root is at or inside a `textures` folder, a request plans under `<export>\<the root's own folders from that textures folder down to the root, each named as its parent folder lists it>`: root `Mod\textures\armor` plans under `<export>\textures\armor`. `discover::root_prefix` gives the folders and `discover::effective_output_root` the root; `BatchRequest::new` and `BatchRequest::set_export` apply it from the first input folder, so the CLI, the GUI's plan and every Also phase form it in one place. A moved request keeps the export folder in `BatchRequest::export_root`: explicit `output_paths` are relative to it, not to the output root, so a PGPatcher `textures\pbr\...` prefix still lands at `<export>\textures\pbr\...` for a root inside `textures` (fix round 1). Discovery skips the whole export folder (`skip_root`) unless that folder holds the input root (HQ5). Outputs then sit at their game paths below the export folder. Things that live beside the export folder itself keep the plain folder: the PGPatcher, CS PBR, CM and XPMF JSON locations, the Also location and **Open export folder**. Tests: `crates/engine/tests/export_layout.rs`, `crates/gui/tests/export_layout.rs`.
- **HQ2 A root with no `textures` in its path plans under the export folder itself.** No folder is invented. Cost if wrong: such projects still export without `textures`; the owner is told.
- **HQ3 Writing in place keeps the input root as the output root.** With the export folder equal to the input root (the CLI allows it, Note BC; the GUI's `export_folder_problem` refuses it), `root_prefix` is empty and a DDS lands beside its source.
- **HQ4 Existing projects whose root is a `textures` folder, or inside one, write to new paths.** Their outputs and their manifest (`_sdc`, which lives in the output root) move to `<export>\textures\...`; the old outputs and records stay where they were, since nothing deletes what an earlier export wrote elsewhere without a ruling. CHANGELOG 3.0.0 says so. No `ENGINE_VERSION` or `encode_version` change: paths are not bytes or verdicts, and the goldens are unchanged.
- **HQ5 An export folder that holds the input root writes beside the source (agent ruling).** `root_prefix` starts below the export folder when that is the root or an ancestor of it (`discover::holds`, on resolved paths when both exist, so `..` or a junction cannot hide it): `sdc convert Mod\textures\armor --out Mod\textures` or `--out Mod` writes `Mod\textures\armor\x.dds`. Discovery then skips nothing, since the export folder holds every input. Only the CLI can reach it; the GUI's `export_folder_problem` refuses such a folder. Test: `export_layout::in_place_writes_beside_the_source` plans all three through `BatchRequest::new`.
- **HQ6 Consumers (agent ruling).** Job keys stay relative to the plan's output root (`sets_root`); explicit output paths are relative to the export folder (HQ1). `sets::engine_inputs` builds them that way: a PGPatcher prefix is one already, an output name's path and a record path (`record_entries`) go through `AppModel::export_rel`, and a mirrored job key is compared as `AppModel::export_key`. Read back as they are by `pbr_export::slots_of` (the HL5 `root_is_textures` workaround is gone), `member_game_path` and the PBR path-collision messages; the collisions compare job keys as `export_key` too. The Meshes "This project" paths (`output_game_paths`) use `export_key` and are rebuilt after the plan's output root lands, so they list a `textures` root's outputs. `xpmf_json::data_rel`, `cs_skin_json::output_game_path` and `cm_json` read absolute outputs against the plain export folder and need no change. The Also walk (`note_unwritten_files`) puts the prefix on the phases' job keys and skips `_sdc` at any depth (test `also_export::a_textures_root_notes_none_of_its_own_files`); the CLI's Also location is `-o` (`cli_convert::also_from_a_textures_root_writes_its_folders_in_o`). The skipped-DDS check in the settings panel joins job keys to `sets_root` (`reencode_toggle::an_export_folder_above_the_textures_root_is_still_its_source_folder`).
- **HQ7 Set ids do not change.** `set_key` and `set_id` stay relative to the request's output root, so `[sets.<id>]` in `sdc-project.toml`, `--set set:<id>`, XPMF profile names and `rel` are what they were for every project. Test: `export_layout::outputs_move_and_set_ids_stay`.
- **HQ8 One output root per request (agent ruling).** The first input folder sets the prefix; later input folders and a first input that is a file add none of their own (`shortcut:` on `set_export`). A root at or inside `textures` that holds a folder named `textures` itself or one level down gets no prefix, since its game paths already start at that nested folder (BQ8). The rule is all or nothing: files beside such a nested folder export without `textures`, and deeper nesting is not looked for (`shortcut:` on `root_prefix`). It is worked out each time a request is built, not cached: the read is the root and its sub-folders' listings and stops at the first hit, and a cache would go stale when the user adds or renames a folder between plans.

## Note IB — UV checker leaves the GUI; sdc uv and sdc dilate (NIF team for the owner, 2026-11-08)

Source: the owner's plan to move the UV-island check out of the GUI (Tessalith is for finished textures; the check belongs earlier in an artist's pipeline). Code: removed `gui::model::uv_coverage`, `gui::model::overlay`, `engine::codes::Code::ChkUvGutter`; the removed-code row in `engine::migrate::REMOVED`. Tests: `crates/engine/tests/presets.rs::chk_uv_gutter_in_allow_is_refused`, `crates/gui/tests/checks.rs::removed_check_keys_in_gui_toml_are_ignored`.

- **IB1 The GUI has no UV coverage checker; `CHK-UV-GUTTER` is removed.** Gone: the preview's "UV coverage" toggle and its overlay (so the whole preview overlay module, `ViewState::overlay` and the overlay worker message), "Check all UV coverage" and "Stop checking UV coverage", the Advanced → Checks "UV coverage" box (three boxes remain), the Problems row, `Check::Uv`, the UV worker messages and `v2_jobs_pending` (the overlay and UV reads were its only jobs), the `uv_reads_on_this_thread` test hook and the `code_text` entry. `ChecksSettings` has no `uv`; an old `[checks] uv` in `gui.toml` loads without a message and is dropped on the next save. `Code::parse` returns `None` for `CHK-UV-GUTTER`, and `migrate::REMOVED` has an `allow` row, so an `allow` list, `--allow` or `--set` that names it is refused with "removed in 3.0" and a hint to use `sdc uv`. The schemas lose the string. This supersedes the UV items of Note CL (CL28's UV use, CL30 to CL34, CL39 and CL44; the frozen 1.x file is unchanged).
- **IB2 `engine::dilate::push_pull_fill(img, valid)` fills masked-out texels with a smooth pull.** It runs a push-pull on all four channels where `valid` is false (valid texels are never written; no valid texel returns false and changes nothing). The push is the same 2x2 mean as `dilate_under_zero_alpha`; the pull takes the bilinear sample of the parent at `((x + 0.5) / 2 - 0.5, (y + 0.5) / 2 - 0.5)`, clamped to its edges. The existing nearest-parent pull copies one parent texel into a 2x2 block, so a wide fill shows blocks; `dilate_under_zero_alpha` keeps it because its bytes are pinned by the goldens. `sdc dilate` uses the new function.
- **IB3 `sdc uv <PATTERN> (--s N | --w W --l L)` writes `<stem>_uvmask.png`, an 8-bit grayscale image of a texture's UV islands.** Island texels are 255, texels within the padding of one island 127, texels within the padding of two or more different islands 191 ("too close", always on), everything else 0. The pattern is a texture path as typed (`\` or `/`, `textures\` and `.dds` optional, any case, `*` and `?` inside a segment; a folder matches the textures directly in it, `--recursive` the ones below), and a shape uses a texture in any lighting slot or as an effect source. Meshes come from the GUI's scan database (`modscan::store::default_path()` or `--scan <db>`; game-winning copies only, loose or BSA) or, with `--meshes <folder>`, every `.nif` below it.
- **IB4 The padding is `max(2, max(W, L) / 256)` texels (512 to 2, 1024 to 4, 2048 to 8, 4096 to 16, 8192 to 32), as a Chebyshev window clamped at the image edges.** The GUI's check measured 8 texels at full size and could not resolve it at 4096; the CLI scales the padding with the image, so the 191 marker means the same thing at every size. The window does not wrap, so an island across the 0/1 edge of a tiling texture is two islands and gets no padding on the far side (`// shortcut` in the code).
- **IB5 Coverage is conservative, and islands are 8-connected texel components.** A texel is an island texel when a triangle overlaps its square with nonzero area (a separating-axis test), so a sliver thinner than a texel is drawn and touching alone does not count. UVs wrap into [0, 1) with a tile cap of 16 per axis, and a zero-area, non-finite or out-of-range triangle is skipped. Meshes that touch or overlap are one island, because the image shows texels, not meshes.
- **IB6 Output mirrors the matched path below the pattern's literal folder part, and an existing file is kept.** `FS6_Arrow_IR` writes `out\fs6_arrow_ir_uvmask.png`; the folder `Harry2135` with `--recursive` writes `out\fantasyseries6\fs6_arrow_ir_uvmask.png`; `--out` defaults to the current folder. A file that exists is kept and reported unless `--overwrite` is given; writes use `write_atomic`. One stdout line per written file: path, islands, pad, too-close texels, meshes (NIF files read). Exit codes follow the CLI table: 0 all written; 1 a texture wrote nothing (no UV triangles, or the file exists without `--overwrite`); 2 a usage error, including a pattern that matches no texture and a missing scan database (the message names the GUI's Meshes scan and `--meshes`); 3 a write failed, and the other textures are still written (as in `sdc dilate`).
- **IB7 modscan gained two read-only queries and a default-path function for `sdc uv`.** `Store::used_texture_paths()` lists the texture paths for which `winning_shapes_of_texture` finds a shape (one shared query body, so the list never names a texture the lookup cannot find); `Store::winning_shapes_of_texture(norm)` returns (copy to read, NIF path, block) for the game-winning copies (a derived winner on its source copy, as `nifs_using_canonical_texture` does) whose shape uses the texture in any lighting slot or as an effect source (slot 0, not the greyscale slot). The pattern is matched against the first list in the CLI, so no new index is needed. Test: `crates/modscan/tests/uv_queries.rs`.
- **IB8 `sdc dilate <PATTERN>...` is `engine::dilate::push_pull_fill` on a texture under a mask; the engine module is now `pub`.** A PATTERN is an image file, a folder (`--recursive` for what is below) or a glob on disk, and every input, literal or glob, must be a PNG, TGA or TIFF (a pattern with no image is exit 2); `*_matid.*`, `*_uvmask.*` and anything inside a `dilated` folder below the named file, folder or glob base are never inputs (a `dilated` above it does not count). The mask is `--mask`, else beside the input `<base>_matid.*` then `<base>_uvmask.png` for the engine's set base and then the full stem; a `_uvmask` is valid at 255 only, any other mask where R, G or B is above 0. Inputs that map to one output path write the first and refuse each later one by name, `--overwrite` or not. Test: `crates/cli/tests/cli_dilate.rs`.
- **IB9 The fill kind comes from the engine's own name reading, and a name with no sub-type is data.** The stem goes through `group::split_vocab`/`split_suffix` and `suggest::suggest` (vanilla, no set) and the first sub-type decides: Normal-category except `coat_normal` and `skin_wet` is a normal map, an sRGB `sdc-base` colour content is colour, everything else is data, and a stem with no known suffix is filled as stored with "no sub-type from the name" on stdout. Colour is filled in linear light (alpha never decoded); a normal map is filled as stored and each filled texel is renormalised over all three channels (a zero vector becomes 0,0,1).
- **IB10 Masked texels are written back from the decoded sample code, never from the filled buffer.** `engine::load` returns k/255 or k/65535 as f32, so `round(v * max)` is the original code for every 8- and 16-bit sample (the test checks every channel layout at both depths); filled texels are rounded from the filled float. L and LA images fill with the L value in all three lanes and write back lane 0 (and alpha for LA). A float input (a 32-bit TIFF; EXR and HDR are not input formats) is refused for that input (`// shortcut` in the code: add them when a float push-pull is wanted).
- **IB11 The output is `<same file name>.png` in a `dilated` folder beside the input (`--out` overrides), same bit depth and channel layout.** The name stays so Tessalith still reads the suffix. An existing output is kept and reported unless `--overwrite`; an output that would replace its own input is refused. Exit codes as `sdc uv` (IB6): 0 all written; 1 an input wrote nothing (no mask, size mismatch, float, no valid texel, exists); 2 no image matches; 3 a write failed. One stdout line per file: path, kind, mask, filled texel count.

## Note HH — Sets share a helper (main team, 2026-11-09)

- **HH1 A share is not membership (owner's spec; agent rulings for the code).** `SetEdit::Share { file, to }` puts the file in `TextureSet.shares` of set `to`; it stays a member of its own set, and a share into that set, or a second one, does nothing. A shared file is read as a member everywhere a helper is read: its role joins the set's suffixes (`apply_set_edits`, `predict_suffixes`, `drop_typed_roles`, `refresh_suffixes`), it is a raw input of `plan_packing` (the first by path still takes a role, HE7) and a helper of `plan_fills` (never a target there), with its own `helper.*` keys. A helper is used when any set uses it, so it is consumed and not `PACK-UNUSED-INPUT` unless every set leaves it out. `HelperReport` stays one per file: `fills` and `options` are its own set's, `outputs` adds the sharing sets' after its own, and `shared_with` lists their keys. A sharing set and the own set of each shared file are fast re-plan partners. Test `crates/engine/tests/helper_share.rs`.
- **HH2 A shared file stands in under the sharing set's name (agent ruling).** A recipe names and places its output from its first source, so in the sharing set `shared_stand_in` gives the file the folder, stem and game folder of the set's member whose base is the key's (else its first image), as `joined_helper_names` does for a hand-set helper: a shared roughness into `textures/c/pbr/slab` writes `<export>/textures/c/pbr/slab_rmaos.dds` with its game path under `textures/c/pbr/` (HQ1's export root holds, since the output comes from a member's). `path` stays the real file's, so sizes, `consumed` and the job removal see one file. A share adds no job and writes no file of its own.
- **HH3 What a share changes (agent ruling, 2026-11-09).** The own set plans as it did. A shared roughness makes a cs set PBR, as its own would (`set_resolved.pbr`). A share into a set with no member of its own is a `SET-EDIT-STALE` ("set '<id>' has no texture of its own"), and that set is dropped as an empty one is; a share naming a missing file or set is stale as `Move` is.
- **HH4 The no-set drop is by set (agent ruling, 2026-11-09).** `plan_sets` records `Plan::no_set_sets`, the keys of the sets whose members are all no-set (the GUI's `all_no_set` rule), and the BV2 dedupe and `Engine::run` test a job's `set_key` against it, not its input: a recipe of another set whose first source is a shared no-set helper writes, and the no-set file's own one-file set (its `_rmaos` too) still writes nothing. `Plan::no_set` stays for the GUI. `write_only` and `may_wait` (GY1, members only) are unchanged.
- **HH5 Dropping a helper on a set shares it (owner's rulings; agent ruling for the code, 2026-11-09).** A shareable helper is an `ItemKind::Helper` with a `HelperRole::Raw` role. `drop_choice(Share)` on one writes `share = ["<key>"]` in the target's `sdc-project.toml` entry (`SetsDoc::add_share`, one undo step "Share <name> with <set>", no file, no `occupant` check) and refuses a target with no member with "<set> has no texture of its own; move one in first"; on its own set it does nothing, and neither does a no-set helper on the set its name puts it in (`dir/base`, where **Back to its automatic set** returns it). A no-set helper dropped on a set asks (`AskChoice { from: "no set" }`, owner), and its dialog leaves Duplicate out; every other no-set texture still moves (HF1). The dialog's Share reads "Share (use this file here too, no copy)" for a helper, and a finished texture still borrows (`borrow.slotN`). The Textures column's helper row is a drag source (`Sense::click_and_drag`, payload `DragItem`), so a no-set helper can reach a card; a click still selects. `engine_inputs` emits `SetEdit::Share` after the `NoSet` edits. Test `crates/gui/tests/helper_share.rs`.
- **HH6 The share line follows the file (agent ruling, 2026-11-09).** The HE2 clear (`clear_deleted_lines`, through `drop_entry`) covers `share` lines as it covers `add` and `remove`: a deleted shared file's line goes with one Info line in Messages. `add_member` drops the target's own `share` line for the file it moves in, since the engine ignores a share of a member.
- **HH7 What the views show (agent ruling, 2026-11-09; final review 2026-11-09).** `SetView.shared_helpers` holds the item keys of the set's `share` lines that match an item (`path_key_matches`: any case, or without the extension) and are not the set's own members, which the engine ignores (a line matching no item is a `Missing::File`, as `add` is). The set editor draws one row per shared helper under the slot rows, outlined amber, named `<file> (shared)` so the user sees why it is not a slot (controller ruling); a click anywhere on the row selects the helper, and its ✕ ("Stop sharing <file>", `AppModel::stop_sharing`) removes every line that matches the file the same way. Only the ✕ is off on a read-only document, with the reason on hover. `stop_sharing` refuses a file the set does not share ("<set> does not share <file>", no undo step) and a read-only document. The row keeps clear of the editor's floating scroll bar. The helper panel lists every set in `shared_with`, used or not, under "Shared with" as links to those sets. `channel_helpers` offers a helper whose `shared_with` holds the set, and a pick writes the file's one `[files]` row, so it applies wherever the file is used. `HelperReport.used_by` (engine) holds the sharing sets whose jobs it lands in; `items::used_by` gives their base names when its own set fills nothing, and then `target_line` and the Textures row read `used by <names>`. A helper shared but used by no set still reads "not used: <reason>".

## Note HI — Texture panel tidy (main team, 2026-11-09)

- **HI1 One label column, one control column (owner).** Every texture-panel row draws its label in a `LABEL_W` (150 px) cell, cut short with the whole text on hover, and its control in a `CONTROL_W` (200 px) cell (`settings_panel::field_label`, `control_slot`, `control_cell`). Where a grid is narrower than both columns plus ↺, a hint and a margin (the centre at 1100 px with the Advanced drawer open), `column_widths` scales both down alike, so no row runs under the drawer (rule 3.1; agent ruling, controller-approved. Cost if wrong: controls about 190 px at 1100, and narrower with the Advanced drawer open). A combo with no name of its own is named after its row (`labelled_by`), keeping its value. The Mipmaps tick sits in the control column, named "Mipmaps", with the level count or provenance in the hint column. The helper panel's rows and the cubemap rows use the same two widths; the helper's controls also give up the width of the Reset beside them where the row would not fit (`column_widths_beside`, agent ruling). Test `crates/gui/tests/texture_panel_layout.rs`.
- **HI2 Input content and DDS tag are in Compression (owner).** The Colour group is gone: the groups are Type, Alpha, Mipmaps and Compression, and Compression reads Format, DDS tag, Input content, Effort, BC7 weighting, Perceptual. `color.content`'s row is labelled "Input content"; the key is unchanged. The preview's strip still leaves both rows out.
- **HI3 The preview strip's controls are `COMPACT_CONTROL_W` (140 px) (agent ruling, owner to confirm).** Its labels take the room left (cut short, whole text on hover), so no control ends past the 280 px strip. The Advanced drawer's rows keep their content widths and their unnamed combos.
- **HI4 Helper is a tick under Sub-type (owner).** An image's Type group has a "Helper" row right under Sub-type; ticking it puts a "Helper role" combo ("Pick a role…") in Sub-type's place, and picking a role writes `helper.role`. The helper panel draws the same tick, ticked, under its Role row. A non-image has no Helper row; an image with no job (failed, skipped, packed) draws the Helper row alone, with the role combo once ticked.
- **HI5 Ticking writes nothing until a role is picked (agent ruling).** No default role is right for every file, so the ticked state is egui temp data keyed `("helper-tick", key)`, per file; any `helper.role` write from either panel clears it. Cost: one more click.
- **HI6 Unticking replaces "Not a helper" (agent ruling).** The role combo lists every role but "Not a helper"; unticking a helper writes `helper.role = "none"` (DY2), and such a file reads unticked with "set by you" and ↺ "Reset role" on the Helper row. Supersedes the 1.x DY17a (Not a helper in the combo), DY17c's "Not a helper (set by you)" wording and DY17f's "Helper role" combo under every image; Reset stays.
- **HI7 The alpha question has a Confirm button (owner).** `settings_panel::alpha_question` draws the dropdown on the proposal and **Confirm**, which answers with the shown type (the `Answer::Alpha` a pick gives); picking from the list still answers at once (FM35), and Enter on a Problems row is unchanged. Confirm's accessible name is "Confirm alpha" after the surface ("Problems confirm alpha"), so the sub-type's "Confirm" stays unique; it waits for the plan as the dropdown does.
- **HI8 Problems and Review get Confirm too (agent ruling).** They ask the same question from one shared row (`review::fix_buttons_on`); on a Problems row, laid out right to left, Confirm still reads after its dropdown.
- **HI9 A `?` and a tooltip on every settings row (owner).** Every `FIELDS` and `ADVANCED_FIELDS` row, Levels, Filter, Edge, Straight alpha, the Mipmaps tick and the Helper tick (texture and helper panels) has help: `fields::field_help` (or `MIPMAPS_HELP`, `HELPER_HELP`), shown on the label's hover, also while the row is off (`edit_off`, a lock). In the texture panel, the 3D settings window, the bulk panel and the helper panel's Helper row the label cell also ends with a weak `?` named "Help: {label}" with the same hover (not in the strip or the Advanced drawer, HI18). Aligned, the `?`s line up at the label column's right edge; in the bulk panel the `?` follows the label. The checkbox hovers (parallax, BC1 alpha, Perceptual, EXR straight) stay on the control and are those rows' help too.
- **HI10 The cube section gets no `?` (agent ruling).** It has its own wiki page (`Cubemaps.md#settings`), and the owner's screenshot doesn't show it.
- **HI11 The texture panel's Format names the format alone (owner).** `fields::format_choices` lists each format once, "BC7", with `NOT_VALID` on a kept stray pair.
- **HI12 Other format pickers keep the tag (agent ruling).** The bulk panel, the Presets window and the set editor still list "BC7 · linear", since the owner said "on this page". A Format pick on the texture panel keeps the current DDS tag when the new format allows it, else takes that format's first allowed pair.
- **HI13 The Alpha row and the Undecided box line up and agree (owner).** In the texture panel the box asks the alpha as one row, "Alpha" in the label column, the dropdown in the control column at the Alpha row's x, then Confirm; the label's hover is the SUG-LOW-CONFIDENCE reason. The sub-type question keeps its sentence, its combo and Confirm in the control column. Where the box is too narrow for a full-width dropdown and Confirm (1100 px), the dropdown gives up the difference. The strip keeps FM35's line, with Confirm added.
- **HI14 While the alpha is asked, every row reads the proposal (agent ruling).** `draw` sets the proposal on its own copy of the job, so the Alpha row, Threshold, Comparison, Coverage and the filter locks show what Confirm will give, and the Alpha row's hint is "proposed: confirm above". Nothing is written, the document stays clean and the export gate still counts the texture undecided until an answer; a pick on the Alpha row is an answer, and picking the shown value there writes nothing (Confirm accepts it). The Advanced drawer shows the unconfirmed profile.
- **HI15 A texture in no set gets the same panel (agent ruling).** That covers its Undecided box with Confirm and its Helper tick; a role picked there makes a no-set helper, which reads "not used" until it is shared (HH5) or moved into a set.
- **HI16 Unticking a shared helper writes `helper.role = "none"` on its one `[files]` row (agent ruling).** Every set that shares it stops using it, and the `share` lines stay; the tick's hover says "Also used by {n} other sets: they stop using it too." ("1 other set: it stops …" for one). Undo takes it back.
- **HI17 The 3D settings window is aligned (agent ruling).** It draws the texture panel, so its rows use the same two columns; at its default 460 px they stay inside it.
- **HI18 The preview's settings strip and the Advanced drawer show no `?` (agent ruling).** The strip's labels are about 90 px and already cut short, and in the drawer at 280 px a `?` pushed every combo about 4 px past its edge; there the label's hover carries the help (with the full label where it is cut short).

## Note HJ — Rename outputs through slots (main team, 2026-11-09)

- **HJ1 Rename outputs is for PGPatcher PBR sets only (owner).** `AppModel::renames_outputs` (`v.pbr` on the Community Shaders target, the old `record_ignored`) decides it. The Outputs entry is absent, not greyed, from the set menu, the texture menu and the palette when no selected set qualifies; a mixed selection lists the qualifying sets with their count in the label. F2 on a selection with no qualifying set opens Inputs mode, and the dialog's mode switch hides Outputs then. `set_output_name` refuses a name on any other set ("output names are for PGPatcher PBR sets"); only `None` clears.
- **HJ2 An `output_name` already saved keeps working and can be cleared (agent ruling).** Dropping it would silently move an old project's outputs. The set editor's Clear button stays. Cost if wrong: an old project keeps a name the user can no longer set.
- **HJ3 A rename that cannot be a PGPatcher `rename` writes slot commands (owner).** `prefix::rename_form` is `Key` (written as `match_diffuse` + `rename`, AN1, AA8) for a file-name `rename` on a set with at most one match row (an empty + row counts) and no `path_contains`, and `Slots` for any other rename: `json_rename` is then `None`, the entry keeps its active matches and `path_contains` and writes no `rename` or `match_diffuse`, and `slots_of` compares each filled slot against `default_prefix` (where PGPatcher looks by default) so every filled slot names the renamed file (a user's `slotN` wins, a locked slot stays unnamed). AN1 and AN2 hold for `Key` form only, so no edit is refused for a rename, the "not written" markers and pitfalls are gone, and `cs_pbr::set_key` keeps every match; the "match slots point at different textures" pitfall still fires on a renamed set (agent ruling), since a slot the set leaves empty comes from the hit's prefix.
- **HJ4 A plain rename keeps `match_diffuse` + `rename` (agent ruling).** Existing JSON doesn't change for no reason: `Key` form writes the same bytes as before (pinned by `a_plain_rename_keeps_its_json`), and AF8's pitfall still applies to it. Cost if wrong: two JSON shapes for one feature; switching every rename to slots would be a one-line change in `rename_form`.
- **HJ5 Rename outputs can change the whole path, through slot commands (owner).** A `rename` with a `\` or `/` is a whole path relative to `Data`, which is relative to the export folder: `prefix_of` returns it backslashed, with `textures\` added when missing, and `rename_form` is always `Slots`. It is checked by `check_rel_path` (no absolute path, no `..`) and `check_named` on its last component (no map suffix, no `_detail`/`_detailnorm`). The rename dialog offers *Whole path* in both modes; in Outputs mode the recipe runs over the output base path (the suffix and extension stay out of it), and unticked it runs over the last component only.
- **HJ6 The path is stored in the set's `rename` (agent ruling).** No new key, so the JSON editor's box and undo keep working; it is never written to the JSON in that form. `set_output_name` stores the last component alone when it gives the same prefix (`rename_value`), so a dialog rename that keeps the folder stays in `Key` form (HJ4), and a path otherwise. Cost if wrong: a typed `textures\pbr\<matched folder>\x` path is stored as `x`; the outputs land on the same files either way.
- **HJ7 A moved set's files stay where the user put them (agent ruling).** Not under `textures\pbr\`. Known texture paths under `textures\pbr\` are still the only ones kept out of the input-name suggestions (AO8; a `shortcut:` in `input_name_candidates`). Cost if wrong: a moved output shows up in the input-name suggestions when the export folder is scanned as a mod.
- **HJ8 A no-albedo JSON set gets no Rename outputs (agent ruling).** A set with `json && !pbr` (Note HL5) keeps its outputs at the mesh's own paths and its slot 1 at the mapping's; a vanilla set renames through Map to target (Note HG). Cost if wrong: the owner wanted those sets renamed too; `renames_outputs` would then read `json` and the slot rows would need the HL5 paths.
- **HJ9 A whole path is relative to the export folder (agent ruling).** That is the frame HQ6 gives a PGPatcher prefix, so `armor\plate` lands at `<export>\textures\armor\plate.dds` whatever the input root, and outside the plan's output root when that root is deeper. The Outputs dialog shows and compares outputs in that frame for file names too, so its rows read as game paths: the old value is the set's prefix with `/`, the outputs are `output_rel(<new prefix>, suffix)` for each output `output_candidates` gives the set, and a collision is checked, lower case, against the sources of `pbr_export::path_collisions` for every set not renamed (owned, record and mirrored `export_key` paths) and between the renamed sets. Cost if wrong: a user expecting a path relative to the input root gets a different folder; the row shows the full game path before Apply, so it is seen, not silent.
- **HJ10 A shared helper's recipe output follows the sharing set (agent ruling).** A shared helper (Note HH) writes nothing of its own; a recipe output it feeds belongs to the sharing set and moves with that set's rename only. Selecting a shared helper's tile and pressing Rename outputs renames its own set, as for any member. Cost if wrong: the user wanted the sharer renamed; they select its card instead.
- **HJ11 A rename never changes a set key (agent ruling).** A no-set file (Note HF1) has no set and offers no Rename outputs (pinned by `rename_keys_skip_textures_that_are_in_no_set_in_outputs_mode`); a rename never changes a set key (HQ7), so Export set, Export group, Exclude and `write_only` treat a moved set as before, and a scope that leaves it out keeps its PGPatcher entry while its files exist (HF12). Cost if wrong: a scoped export drops a moved set's entry as "not exported yet" until it is exported once at the new path.

## Note HR — seven OPEN items closed (main team, 2026-11-09)

- **HR1 A `remove` written by an override is opaque colour to the rules (agent ruling, owner confirmed 2026-11-09).** On a sub-type whose alpha the mesh decides (`Subtype::alpha_from_mesh`), when the source alpha varies and the winning `alpha.policy` override (`[files]` or `--set`, the last that applies) is `remove`, `layered_profile_with` matches the rules on alpha `none`, as for the mesh's own `remove` (owner O1), so cs-compact gives BC1. A `remove` from a preset or config table is unchanged: the rules still see the real alpha. Tests `mesh_alpha::an_explicit_remove_on_varying_alpha_matches_the_mesh_remove`, `a_recorded_remove_in_the_project_file_is_bc1_under_compact`, `a_later_override_over_a_remove_keeps_compact_off_bc1`.
- **HR2 The GUI records a mesh `remove` (owner).** `record_auto_decisions` writes every mesh-alpha verdict, `remove` included, as `alpha.policy` in `sdc.toml`, a mesh `remove` on a `complex_grass` file too (`tests/complex_grass.rs`) (amends FC1; closes OPEN [FC]); the threshold is still written only when the mesh set it, and an Undo stays undone. With HR1 the CLI plans the same format from the text. Test `auto_accept::a_mesh_remove_is_recorded_and_stays_undone`.
- **HR3 A file link into the output folder is skipped (agent ruling).** Discovery resolves a file link as it does a folder link (Note CV6): unless the input lies inside the output folder (an in-place run), a link whose target is under the output root is not an input and nothing is reported. A hard link is not a link and is still read; an in-place run skips nothing. Closes OPEN [CV6]. Tests `discover_group::a_file_link_into_the_output_folder_is_skipped`, `an_in_place_run_still_reads_a_file_link` (both print `skipped:` where file links cannot be made).
- **HR4 OPEN [R-fills-1] and OPEN [U] are closed by a pin test each (agent ruling).** `rfaos` is in `group::SUFFIXES`, so a pre-packed `_rfaos` joins its set without a prediction, and `Code::PackConflict` is raised for it; a mesh-evidence read error is already a Messages warning ("Scan database: ... keeping the previous mesh evidence") and the evidence is kept. Neither needed code. Tests `skin_subtypes::a_prepacked_rfaos_joins_a_forced_rfaos_set_without_fuzz`, `edges::a_mesh_evidence_read_error_warns_and_keeps_the_evidence`.
- **HR5 The late restore-Undo line names the keys (agent ruling).** After the restored edits were undone or edited over, the toast's Undo says "The restored edits were already undone or edited since: press Ctrl+Z to undo or Ctrl+Y to redo". It supersedes FB14's wording; closes OPEN [FB14]. Test `recovery::toast_undo_after_another_edit_leaves_everything`.
- **HR6 A resized cutout texture keeps its source's coverage (agent ruling, owner confirmed 2026-11-09).** When `resize::target_dims` changes the size (a `resize` mode or the power-of-two step) and coverage preservation applies, `preprocess_with` records the alpha-test coverage just before the resize (`Work::source_coverage`), and the pipeline fits every level, mip 0 included, to it (`coverage::preserve_coverage_to`); that is also the base the coverage check compares with. Under `if-changed` every output of those sub-types is written again once; only resized cutouts change. A texture that is not resized is unchanged byte for byte, and complex grass and cubemaps keep their path. Supersedes S-mips-8 (closes its OPEN line). The `encode_version` arms of `diffuse`, `pbr_albedo`, `ui_sky` and `skin_albedo` moved to the engine version: the sub-types a built-in layer can make a cutout (the mesh layer and alpha suggestion, `Subtype::alpha_from_mesh`, and the hair shader's diffuse); `complex_grass` is the other mesh sub-type and keeps its own path. A hand-written cutout on any other sub-type that is also resized keeps its old bytes under `if-changed` until that arm moves. No golden, `encode_inputs.json` or `identity.json` entry changed. Tests `coverage::preserve_coverage_to_fits_level_0`, `preserve_coverage_to_at_the_ends`, `ops::a_resize_records_the_source_coverage`, `pipeline::a_resized_cutout_keeps_its_source_coverage`, `encode::a_resized_cutout_export_keeps_the_source_coverage`.
- **HR7 A cache hit keeps its entry from the prune (agent ruling).** A `get` on an analysis-cache entry whose modification time is more than a day old (`TOUCH_AFTER`) sets it to now, so the prune, which still deletes oldest first, keeps what is used. At most one small write per entry per day; a failed touch (read-only entry) is still a hit. Amends DZ8 (closes its OPEN line). Tests `analysis_store::a_hit_on_an_old_entry_survives_the_prune`, `a_recent_hit_leaves_the_time_alone`, `a_read_only_old_entry_is_still_a_hit`.

## Note HT — audit 2026-11-10: one guarded write, per-crate test runs (main team, 2026-11-10)

- **HT1 Every JSON finisher writes through `json_finish::guarded_write` (agent ruling).** CS PBR and XPMF call it as complex material and CS skin do, so the never-overwrite order (drop the own case twin, `may_replace`, atomic write, record the hash) has one copy; notes and files are unchanged (extends FW2). Two pins came with it: `presets_creator::a_new_preset_is_not_saved_over_a_file_that_did_not_load` (AM8) and `cm_json::without_a_cm_phase_the_cm_json_follows_the_pbr_phase` (the fall-back of FW1).
- **HT2 A per-crate test run adds `-p sdc` (owner, 2026-11-10).** `cargo test -p <crate> -p sdc ...` resolves features as `--workspace` does, so the gate reuses its build. Measured with `cargo tree`: `-p engine` alone builds 15 packages under other features and `-p sdc-gui` alone 3; with `-p sdc` added, none. For this the CLI's dev-dependency on `sdc-gui` turns on `test-hooks` (no CLI test reads a GUI hook). It amends the Gates rule of 2026-10-29 in CLAUDE.md.

## Note HU — one GUI test binary (main team, 2026-11-10)

- **HU1 The GUI test files are modules of one test binary, `it` (owner, 2026-11-10).** `crates/gui/tests/it/main.rs` names each `tests/<name>.rs` with `#[path]`, so no file moved. Each keeps its own `mod support;`, and a few their own copy of another crate's fixture builder, so the binary allows `clippy::duplicate_mod`; it allows no other lint. `crates/gui/Cargo.toml` sets `autotests = false`: only the targets it lists are built. A test is named `<file>::<test>`, and one file runs with `cargo test -p sdc-gui -p sdc --test it <file>::`. Measured for the 154 merged files: 19.9 GB of exes and PDBs became 0.23 GB, and building the GUI and CLI test binaries went from 454 s to 180 s. The gate's `render_` filter still selects GPU tests only, because no test file has `render_` in its name; a file named so would move all its tests to the GPU step.
- **HU2 A test file that changes process-wide state stays its own `[[test]]` target (agent ruling).** Eight do. `app_no_local_store`, `doc_shots`, `meshes_header`, `nif_edit`, `replan_timing` and `ui_proof` point `%LOCALAPPDATA%` or `%APPDATA%` at a temp folder. `crash_recovery` installs the panic hook. `device_lost` sets the device-lost flag, which nothing clears: the next `SdcApp` frame in the process would open the restart dialog of FC4, wait for a click, then start the test exe again and exit. A trial run with `device_lost`, `nif_edit` and `meshes_header` all merged hung for hours on tests of the last two, and its log ends with the restart's `--after-device-loss`; the same run without those two passed, so which of the two causes stopped it was not separated. `ui_drive` keeps its own harness.
- **HU3 `it::every_test_file_is_built` fails when a test file is in neither place, or in both (agent ruling).** With `autotests = false` a forgotten file is never compiled, and its tests would pass by not running. A file in both places would run a second time beside tests it can disturb. It checks each `tests/<name>.rs` for its `#[path]` and `mod` lines or a `[[test]]` path, and each folder with a `main.rs` for a `[[test]]` path.

## Note HV — analysis cache: compressed thumbnails and a limit the user sets (owner, 2026-11-10)

- **HV1 A cache entry's thumbnail is stored deflated (owner: "add compressed caching"; the format is an agent ruling).** `AnalysisStore::put` writes the RGBA8 bytes through zlib at the fast level when that is smaller, else as they are. `get` tells the two apart by length alone: bytes as long as width × height × 4 are not deflated. So the header has no new field, `ANALYSIS_VERSION` does not move, and an entry written before this note still reads. A deflated thumbnail must inflate from a whole, valid stream to exactly the header's size, or the entry is a miss (DZ: every failure is a miss). An older Tessalith that opens the folder reads a deflated entry as a miss and writes it again. Measured with zlib on 600 entries of the owner's cache: an entry goes from about 67 KB to about 33 KB (the thumbnail to 47.8% of its bytes at level 1; level 6 gives 45.6%), and inflating one takes 0.19 ms against 17 ms to decode the image again. `flate2` moves from the engine's dev-dependencies to its dependencies; it was already in the lock through `esp`. Tests `analysis_store::a_thumbnail_is_stored_compressed_and_reads_back`, `an_entry_with_an_uncompressed_thumbnail_still_reads`, `a_thumbnail_that_does_not_shrink_is_stored_as_it_is`, `a_damaged_compressed_thumbnail_is_a_miss`.
- **HV2 The cache limit is a GUI setting, 500 MB unless changed (owner).** `Session::with_disk_cache(dir, max_bytes)` takes the limit, and the GUI passes `GuiSettings::analysis_cache_bytes()`: `analysis_cache_mb` in `gui.toml`, 500 when unset (an unset limit is not written), one MB being 1024 × 1024 bytes. **Advanced → Cache** has one number box, *Analysis cache limit*, from 50 to 20,000 MB; a value typed into `gui.toml` outside that range is used and shown as it is. The prune still runs once, at start (DZ8), so a new limit applies the next time the app starts and the box's hover says so (agent ruling: lowering the limit deletes nothing while the app runs). It replaces DZ8's fixed 200 MB; HR7 is unchanged. With HV1, 500 MB holds about 15,000 entries, where 200 MB held about 3,100. Tests `analysis_disk_cache::a_session_prunes_to_the_given_size_at_start`, `app_no_local_store::with_analysis_cache_stores_under_the_given_folder_only`, `settings::the_analysis_cache_limit_is_500_mb_until_set`, `advanced::the_cache_limit_box_saves_a_typed_limit`.
